## Authority Matrix (v6.8)

`signals/authority_fusion.py` declares **26 agents** in `AUTHORITY_MATRIX_NORMAL` (soldex added 2026-04-15 — SOLDEX-AUTHORITY tag at line ~194 — replaced an earlier slot; **`v5_1_strats` added 2026-06-13 by commit `795ecc4` took the count 25 → 26 and this table was not updated at the time — see P165**).

**[CORRECTED P227 — the old "20 of 26 consumed" claim was wrong on both halves.]**
`_build_fusion_signals` builds a signal for **25** of the 26 (only `vol_alpha` has no branch), but `fuse()` actually **acts on 11 in NORMAL mode**: 2 DECIDE (quant, kraken_quant) + 3 CONFIRM (regime, two_stage, structure) + the **6 named in `ADVISE_WEIGHTS_BY_REGIME`** (short_bias, funding_rate, onchain, llm_sentiment, sentiment, flow) — plus `risk` (veto) and `macro` (cap) affecting exposure without direction, and `drl` as a 12th only when its authority is ACTIVE (it is SHADOW). **The remaining 12 ADVISE agents are dropped at `authority_fusion.py:~906`** (`advise_weights.get(agent, 0.0)` → `continue`): their signals are computed and attributed every tick but never consumed. Whether that is intended has never been decided — a one-shot `[P227-ADVISE-WEIGHTS]` log now names the roster per process. Also note (P227 audit): `regime_direction` has **no live producer** (only the synthetic verification path writes it), so the regime CONFIRM falls back to quant's own direction and can never disagree; and fusion's Layer-7 sentiment veto is dead code (no ADVISE agent's `veto_active` is ever read).

## Non-Negotiable Rules

1. **Constitution is supreme** — no trade without alpha gate pass
2. **P0 Safety cannot be bypassed** — kill switch, stale data guard, rate limiter
3. **Existence Fuse** — 28d window, -5% PnL → system halt, manual recovery only
4. **DRL Authority** — SHADOW since 2026-08-07 (P198; was ACTIVE 2026-04-22 → 2026-08-07). Re-promotion requires a clean retrain (P164/P179–P184-fixed pipeline) that passes the P182 baselines and the P166 cost-aware gate on FORWARD data — never on backtest alone.
5. **Single exchange** — Kraken only (Binance/Deribit in `legacy/` for historical refs)
6. **Three trade_gate call sites** — main veto_chain, authority_chain, AND p0_safety_integrator ALL call `trade_gate.check()`. Fix ALL three when changing the gate API.
7. **CLAUDE.md discipline** — runtime-state changes, new pitfalls, and authority-matrix edits MUST update this file in the same commit.

---

## CRITICAL: Trade Frequency Reality Check

Before suggesting any new patch, audit, or "optimization", Claude MUST first ask:

1. Has the system executed any trade in the last 24h? Last 7d?
2. If 0 trades, what is the documented bottleneck (with log evidence)?
3. Is the proposed patch directly addressing that bottleneck?

If the system has had 0 trades for >7 days while "running normally":
- Stop suggesting new patches
- Stop suggesting new audits
- The bottleneck is NOT in the patches — it's in the system's
  fundamental ability to convert signals to trades
- Recommend ONE thing: revert to a known-trading version,
  or relax thresholds aggressively until trades happen,
  THEN add safety back

Anti-pattern Claude must avoid:
- "Let me suggest 8 more audit dimensions" (over-engineering on
  a system that doesn't trade)
- "Let me write a 1126-line patch prompt" (compounding complexity
  on a non-trading system)
- "Let me validate one more thing" (analysis paralysis)

The user's time is finite. A non-trading system is ZERO ROI
regardless of how clean the architecture is.

## Lessons (Process Discipline)

### Lesson: Two Kinds of Stale State

Stale state has two forms in trading systems:

1. **Code stale**: Conversation memory ≠ codebase state.
   Example: `[SOTA-G2]` already wired but I assumed not.
   Mitigation: VERIFY codebase before propose.

2. **Data stale**: Earlier diagnostic snapshot ≠ today's state.
   Example: Tier 4 said STRUCTURE 48% killer; today's analyzer
   shows STRUCTURE 0%, WEEKEND 83%.
   Mitigation: Re-run diagnostic before applying patches based
   on prior data.

Both are equally dangerous. Today's session ran into both at
least 4 times. Future Claude sessions must check both before
proposing any change.

### Lesson: Defensive Mitigation Is Not a Fix

P52/P56 made the fallback non-catastrophic. This is good
operational hygiene but does not address root cause. Future
discipline: when applying defensive mitigation, log the gap
explicitly so it doesn't appear "fixed" in dashboards while
the root cause continues to fire.

### Lesson: Heisenbug Investigation Discipline

When state is correct at observation point A but wrong at
observation point B, do not patch the gate. Trace the state
mutation path between A and B. Three standard candidates:
  - Mutation paths post-init
  - Observation gaps (rate-limited logs hiding frequency)
  - Object identity (shallow vs deep copy, instance vs class)

Today's weekend_config bug is a textbook example. CLAUDE.md
P45 flagged it; follow-up trace was not done.

### Lesson: Parallel-Edit Discipline (operator + Claude)

The operator and Claude often edit the codebase IN PARALLEL.
Operator commits land between Claude's `git status` checks. Claude's
mental model of "what's in main" lags reality by minutes-to-hours.

This session's evidence: P82/P83/P84 ALL had operator-titled sibling
commits (24ffcd6, 2d0a93c, 72ea60f) between Claude's pushes. One of
those introduced a P15-shape bug (`ShadowLedgerWriter.frozen_allocations`
read with no writer) that Claude's code didn't touch but cascaded to
**10 container restarts in 6 minutes** before P85 emergency-fixed it.

**Three rules to enforce parallel-edit safety:**

1. **Re-pull before EVERY non-trivial commit.** Do `git fetch origin
   && git log HEAD..origin/main` before staging. If origin has new
   commits, REVIEW them before committing — operator may have already
   fixed/changed/refactored what you're about to touch.

2. **Every new attribute READ must defend with `getattr(...)` or
   verify the writer exists in the SAME commit.** P15-shape bugs
   (reader exists, writer missing) recur because the reader-author
   trusts a contract the writer-author didn't honor. With parallel
   edits, that contract drift is invisible at write time. Pattern:

       # WRONG — assumes writer exists somewhere
       value = self.shadow_ledger.frozen_allocations

       # RIGHT — defaults safely + logs gap
       value = getattr(self.shadow_ledger, 'frozen_allocations', None)
       if value is None:
           logger.warning(f"[X] frozen_allocations attr missing; "
                          f"degraded behavior: <what we do instead>")

3. **NEVER add code that exits the process on a missing internal
   attribute.** The reconciler's strict-contract refusal to start LIVE
   when ORDER_CHECK FAILED was a correct safety pattern, but cascading
   to "exit and let docker restart" amplified ONE missing attribute
   into a 10-restart loop. Either:
   (a) the safety check tolerates the failure mode (defensive guard
   degrades gracefully, logs WARNING), OR
   (b) the safety check has a circuit breaker (after N consecutive
   failures, stop trying to restart and require manual intervention —
   prevents docker-compose's `restart: always` from making it worse).

**Forcing function:** the P70 CI gate (codebase-invariants workflow)
runs the silent_failure_audit + lint_silent_swallow on every push.
A new reader added without a defensive `getattr` will trip the lint
if it falls inside a try/except: pass shape. But a reader OUTSIDE a
try/except (like `frozen_allocations`) won't — the strict-contract
caller (reconciler) wrapped it in try/except internally and surfaced
the AttributeError as a top-level FAILED. **The CI gate cannot catch
attribute-access-outside-try/except**; this requires either operator
discipline or a separate scanner pass (filed for future P-entry).

**Rule 4: When adding a new helper method, grep `def <name>` FIRST.**
P87 (commit 088b865) hit a method-collision-shadows bug — added a new
`_clamp_size_to_balance` method to `execution_manager.py:601`, but a
PRE-EXISTING method with the same name lived at `:1545` with a
different signature (4 args returning float vs 5 args returning tuple).
Python silently picks the LAST definition, so callers expecting the
new signature got the old one → `TypeError: takes from 4 to 5 positional
arguments but 6 were given` on every entry attempt → 4-minute
production outage. The hotfix renamed to `_v2`. Lesson: long files
(execution_manager.py is 2400+ lines) make manual scanning unreliable;
EXPLICIT `grep "def <name>"` before adding any non-trivial helper
catches collisions at write time. Add to the standard pre-commit
mental checklist alongside the parallel-edit rules above.

**The CI gate cannot catch this either** — both methods are valid
Python; collision only fails at call time when callers expect
different signatures. Filed under "static analysis can't see
method-resolution-order bugs"; remediation is purely operator
discipline + the grep habit.

## Known Pitfalls (source of repeat bugs — read this before deploying)

> **History:** Detailed P-entries older than the last ~30 days have been moved to [archive/CLAUDE_history.md](archive/CLAUDE_history.md) to keep this file scannable. The summaries below + the foundational invariants (P1-P8) are what every session should load.

### OPERATOR DECISION 2026-08-08: keep the Coinbase sleeve TRADING while forward evidence accumulates

Asked to choose between continuing to trade a signal with no demonstrated edge
and cutting to SOL-only / `""` and waiting, the operator chose **continue**.
Recorded here because it is a live-money decision, not a code change, and the
next session needs to know it was made deliberately rather than by inertia.

**What is being accepted.** The sleeve is at **−5.47%** (−$218.67) on a trend
signal whose `base_edge_bps = 40.0` is a constant chosen to clear the alpha
gate, live daily Sharpe ≈ **−4.5**, and the P200 retrain returned **no edge**.
Continuing means accepting further drawdown while the evidence matures.

**Why it is defensible now and was not two days ago.** Every control that would
bound that drawdown is, for the first time, actually connected to the venue that
holds the risk:

| control | state at the decision |
|---|---|
| Alpha gate (Non-Negotiable #1) | binds via gated intent (P206) — currently flattens ETH/SOL |
| Net exposure cap (P144) | armed on the sleeve book, 0.50 (P208) |
| Per-asset cap | `post_leverage_caps` in sleeve equity (P210) |
| Protective stops | resting at venue, all routed assets, 10% (P197/P205/P207) |
| Sleeve drawdown halt | 5.5% of 15%, sticky across restarts (P150/P195) |
| LIVE drawdown halt + kill switch | wired and able to fire (P201) |
| Existence fuse | fed and persisted, ACTIVE (P209/P211) |

**The three evidence streams this is waiting on** (none promotable yet — do NOT
act on partial data, that is the P147/P198 mistake):
  * **Existence fuse** — 28d window meaningful ~**2026-09-04**.
  * **`trend_regime_gate`** — shadow only; promote to `enforce` only on ≥3–4
    weeks FORWARD data via `scripts/trend_regime_review.py`. Early forward
    numbers point the OPPOSITE way to the in-sample hypothesis.
  * **`derivflow`** (P219/P223) — squeeze vs exhaustion, judged by the P166
    cost-aware gate. At most one can be right; both being noise is expected.

**Operational consequence: STOP DEPLOYING unless something is broken.** The
sleeve was deployed ~15 times on 2026-08-07, and several warmups are in-memory —
`micro` needs `min_samples=5` at one sample per 4H tick, i.e. **~20 h of
uninterrupted uptime**, which it has never had. Frequent deploys are themselves
why parts of the agent layer look dead. A quiet run IS the work now.

**Revert path, unchanged and one step:** `scripts/coinbase_set_assets.sh ""`
makes the sleeve inert; `coinbase_use_gated_intent: false` restores the pre-gate
driver. Neither needs a code change.

### Recent pitfalls (last ~30 days)

### P312. [BUILT 2026-08-19] The SECOND producer/consumer boundary closed — P310 covered the shadow ledgers and explicitly left this one pinned by a hand-copied literal, which is the pattern that let P295d through
P310 built the contract for the shadow-ledger boundary and recorded, honestly, that it did **not** cover `agent_ic_review` → `seat_check` — "pinned by that entry's own literal-report fixture". That is exactly the arrangement P310 exists to condemn: a fixture and a reader written by the same hand, agreeing with each other, incapable of catching a shape mismatch. So the exemption is closed rather than left as a documented gap. Tests appended to `tests/test_p310_producer_consumer_contract.py` (8 → 12); three falsification probes red-then-restored.
- **The producer now OWNS its report shape.** The nesting was built inline in `main()` — nothing a consumer could import, so `seat_check` had to guess it, and guessed `agents.<name>.<h>` against the emitted `agents.<name>.horizons.<h>`. Every agent parsed to n=0 and the seat controller recommended vacating every seat on a live book from nothing (P295d). Extracted `build_report()` / `build_agent_entry()` / `HORIZON_CONTAINER_KEY` / `AGENTS_CONTAINER_KEY`, and `main()` USES them — a seam nothing calls is decorative (P170), and a test pins that the literal `{"horizons": rows` exists in exactly one place.
- **The contract test builds its report THROUGH the producer's functions.** If the shape ever moves, the test's report moves with it and the reader fails — instead of the reader silently parsing to n=0. Falsification confirms the direction that matters: moving `HORIZON_CONTAINER_KEY` in the PRODUCER turns the consumer's tests red (2), reintroducing the P295d flat-shape read turns them red (2), and making `main()` stop using the seam turns the decorative-seam pin red (1).
- **One assertion is about the failure MODE, not just the value:** a shape mismatch surfaces as `n=0`, which `decide_seat` treats as "below the evidence floor" — indistinguishable from a genuinely thin window. That collapse is what made the bug invisible for a week, so the test asserts no agent parses to n=0 from a real report, and an end-to-end case runs the actual CLI and requires exit **3** (a measured verdict) rather than exit 2 (refusal on unreadable input).
- **Refactor safety verified against production, not against a fixture:** the rebuilt skeleton was compared field-for-field with the real `agent_ic_20260817_061002.json` pulled from the server — top-level keys and a full agent block both identical. A pure refactor of a cron-run tool has to be proven output-identical against its own output, not asserted.
- **Numbering:** shipped as P313 and renumbered to P312 — a parallel session claimed 311 for cascade-threshold work in the same tree. Sixth collision of the campaign.
- **Mitigation pattern:** when a fix records "I did not cover X", that sentence is a scheduled defect, not documentation — the next incident at X will cite it. Close the exemption or accept it explicitly with a reason that is not "the other one was harder".

### P317. [FIXED 2026-08-19, operator: "any remaining tasks"] The strategy-aging tracker did not know the name of the strategy driving the book — found by a routine live-log sweep
`analytics.strategy_aging | WARNING | Unknown strategy: regimebook`, three times per tick. `main.py:10294` writes `primary_strategy = "regimebook"` whenever the regimebook seat is enforced (P298 armed it), and the tracker's vocabulary is still the **v3.2 twelve**. So `record_signal` took its unknown-name branch and returned `""` — **the one strategy actually deciding was the one strategy this tracker recorded nothing about**, and it said so ~18 times a day. Tests `tests/test_p315_aging_tracks_the_live_decider.py` (10); three falsification probes red-then-restored.
- **Impact stated rather than dramatised, because I traced it before fixing it.** The only external caller of `get_weight_modifiers` is a WEEKLY (168h) block in `core/execution_service.py:~3292` that LOGS a CRITICAL below 0.7 and an INFO above 1.1 — **nothing multiplies a weight by the result**, so no order changed and no risk control was blind. What was actually lost is the weekly degraded-strategy report's ability to say anything at all about the live decider; what was gained was recurring noise.
- **Three separate defects, three fixes.** (1) The vocabulary now contains `regimebook`, classified **universal** — a regime book picks its leg FROM the regime, so bucketing it bull/bear/volatile would assert something false about it. (2) The unknown-name warning is latched **per name** (not globally — that is the P193 latch bug, where the first unknown silences every later one) and now states the CONSEQUENCE and the fix rather than just the fact (P240). (3) The `get_weight_modifiers` docstring claimed *"WHERE CONSUMED: Quant Agent: Applies to strategy weights / effective_weight = base_regime_weight * aging_modifier"* — **a wiring that does not exist anywhere in the tree.** That is P177 in a docstring: a reader reasons from it and concludes a control exists. Replaced with what the grep actually shows, plus the grep itself so the next reader can re-verify rather than trust.
- **Nothing was added speculatively, and that is pinned.** Only `regimebook` — `trend` already arrives as `trend_following`, and the whale seat does not overwrite `primary_strategy` at all. A vocabulary entry no producer emits is the **inverse** defect (P310), so the test asserts `whale` and `trend` are absent, and a companion test pins the PRODUCER still emitting the name the vocabulary now accepts — the P310 rule applied in the direction that is easy to forget.
- **A false positive I fixed on my side rather than by relaxing someone else's guard.** The parallel session's P307 condition-pin scanner flagged my `assert "NOTHING is being recorded" in msg` because its regex looks for `==|!=|and|or|not|is` inside an asserted string, and my log fragment contains the bare word *"is"*. It is not a condition pin — but that guard deliberately errs toward catching too much, which is the right direction for a guard against `if False and <cond>` pins, so the assertion was reworded. **Weakening a guard to admit your own change is never the fix (P248), and it is no more the fix when the guard is technically over-broad.**
- **Also corrected, mine:** I first attributed a `+1 no-log-early-exit` scanner finding to my own latch (the log became conditional, so an unlogged early exit is real). Re-running after the parallel session's tree settled showed the gate CLEAN — the delta was their in-flight `main.py`/`health_validator.py`, not mine. No baseline was bumped. **Attribute a baseline delta against a settled tree; in a shared checkout a scanner count is a measurement of two sessions at once.**
- **Mitigation pattern:** a per-tick WARNING naming a component is worth one trace even when it looks cosmetic — this one was the live decider being invisible to a tracker, and the trace also turned up a docstring asserting a control that was never wired. And when a tracker's vocabulary is a hardcoded list from an old version, the thing most likely to be missing from it is whatever changed most recently, which is exactly what you most want tracked.

### P329. [FIXED 2026-08-20, operator: "end to end audit"] ONE transient venue 502 disarmed the inter-tick emergency watchdog for 23 minutes — P110's backoff could not tell "the venue rejected the exit" from "we could not read the venue"
Operator pasted three live alerts and asked for an end-to-end audit. Tests `tests/test_p329_transient_venue_read_does_not_disarm_watchdog.py` (18); seven falsification probes red-then-restored.
- **THE INCIDENT, reconstructed from the live log.** At 23:39:53 a single `[COINBASE_SLEEVE] reconcile failed: HTTPError: 502` — **one occurrence in the entire log** — made `_reconcile_ok` False for one 30s cycle. `sleeve_fast_risk_action` correctly returned `SKIPPED_STALE` (P141: never act on last-known state). The caller then mapped that to `on_exit_failed()`, which engaged **P110's 30-minute backoff and logged CRITICAL**. ETH's price-move EXIT_ONLY trigger stayed suppressed until the 00:03 4H anchor refresh cleared it: **23 minutes 09 seconds of a disarmed emergency watchdog**, while ETH sat 7.0-7.3% from its anchor.
- **The move was REAL, which is what makes it a risk finding rather than a false alarm.** Measured over the same log's 1,660 samples: ETH ranged **1916 → 2321 (21.1%)**, SOL **77.24 → 87.08 (12.7%)**. So this was not the P156 stale-anchor artifact — the anchor was fresh and the market genuinely moved. **Nothing was lost only because the book was FLAT** (`positions=FLAT`, all three NOOP on every tick), which is luck, not design.
- **THE CONFLATION.** P110's backoff exists for a **STRUCTURAL** rejection — spot locked by a stop-loss that cannot be cancelled — which persists, so retrying every 30s is pointless spam. `SKIPPED_STALE` is the **opposite**: a momentary failure to READ the venue, in which **no exit was ever attempted**, and which self-corrects on the next cycle. Suppressing on it is exactly backwards: an unreadable venue during a fast move is when the watchdog matters most.
- **The decisive detail that makes the fix free of execution risk:** on `SKIPPED_STALE` the staleness guard in `sleeve_fast_risk_action` returns **before** `execute_target`, so a retry **cannot place an order** — it costs exactly one reconcile call, which the loop would make anyway. That ordering is now pinned by a test (with docstrings stripped, because the helper's own docstring names `execute_target` several lines above the code and an unstripped scan fails on a correct implementation).
- **Fixed by splitting the two conditions rather than weakening either.** `on_exit_failed(..., transient=False)`: structural is byte-identical to P110; transient writes **no** `_exit_failed_at` (so no suppression can be computed from it), counts a per-asset streak, WARNs on an isolated blip and escalates to CRITICAL only when SUSTAINED (6 cycles ≈ 3 min) — because a venue unreadable for minutes while we may hold risk IS actionable even though one blip is not (P303's shape). **The default stays `False`** so the legacy Kraken caller — which reports a genuine order `REJECTED` — is untouched, and the sleeve caller passes the flag explicitly. `ERROR` is deliberately left structural: an exception inside the helper is not evidence the next call will succeed.
- **The streak resets when the venue answers**, driven from the caller on any status that proves the reconcile succeeded. Without it a long-lived process accumulates isolated blips into a permanent CRITICAL that no longer describes the present (P303/P265f), and the reset is guarded so a permanently-unreadable venue cannot clear its own streak.
- **A severity inconsistency inside one tick, now visible in the log.** At 23:39:53 the trigger alert had `has_position=False` and correctly logged INFO ("asset is FLAT — no position to act on", the P240 downgrade); the very next line on the same asset in the same second went CRITICAL, because `on_exit_failed` never received the P240 treatment. Same tick, same asset, two alerts, opposite severity policies.
- **[P329b — the same shape one layer out, from the FRED alert in the same paste.]** **9 of the 10 FRED log lines in a 16h live window were ERROR-level timeouts forwarded to Discord**, while the GCI's own log said the affected indicator *"serves a NEUTRAL mock ... contributes nothing to Macro"* — i.e. an alert the operator can neither act on nor be harmed by, every 4H (P202/P240). Worse, `Releases fetch timed out` fired on **every** refresh, so the one genuinely diagnostic signal (an endpoint that never answers) was buried in its own noise. Now WARNs on isolated failures and ERRORs **exactly once** per sustained streak, with the CONSEQUENCE in the message rather than just the fact. **Verified before changing anything: `event_window` — the only product of that fetch — has no consumer outside the module, so nothing downstream is affected**; that is stated in the message so the next reader does not re-derive it.
  - The four handlers tripped `no-log-early-exit` (+4) because they now log through a helper and **the linter cannot see method-call logging** (P252). `# noqa: silent-swallow` with that rationale at each site rather than a baseline bump — zero baseline movement.
- **Also checked and deliberately NOT changed:** the sleeve's 502 warning is already HTML-stripped (145 chars — P304's filter working); `positions=FLAT` and 0 errors since boot; the P240 FLAT downgrade is behaving exactly as designed on SOL.
- **[P329c — a THIRD instance of the same class, found in the same WARNING sweep.]** `[LLM_SENTIMENT] Haiku API error for SOL: 400 — "You have reached your specified API usage limits. You will regain access on 2026-09-01 at 00:00 UTC."` The Anthropic account is **capped until 2026-09-01** — an operator-side billing state, exactly analogous to CryptoPanic (P322). **The engine handles it correctly**: sentiment falls back and still reads `src=headline_heuristic status=live_heuristic tradeable=True`. The defect is the retry policy: `_classify_haiku_error` knows 401/403/404/422 and 429, and **400 is in neither list**, so a hard-dated account cap fell to the bare-warning `else` with **no backoff at all** — retried once per asset per tick, ~18 calls/day, for the twelve remaining days. That is P293b/P319 verbatim in a third feed. Now classified non-retryable, with the cooldown sized from **the reset instant the API itself states** — unlike P319, where the month boundary was a guess about the vendor's billing cycle. Fail directions pinned: an ordinary malformed-request 400 is deliberately NOT silenced (that is a bug worth seeing, and hiding it behind a long cooldown is the opposite of the fix), and unparseable wording falls back to the standard hard-disable cooldown rather than to "retry immediately". The message states it is a BILLING state and that sentiment stays tradeable, because an alert an operator cannot debug must say what still works (P202).
- **[P329d — the operator re-pasted FRED timeouts after the deploy, and checking them found a gap in MY OWN tests.]** The pasted lines were in the pre-P329b format and the persistent log dates them **00:01:55**, before the 03:45 deploy — so they predate the fix, and the first post-deploy macro refresh (03:46) produced zero FRED timeouts. But "it has not fired yet" is not evidence a fix works, and re-reading the tests showed they exercised `_report_endpoint_failure` **directly** while nothing pinned that the except handlers actually REACH it — the P234 shape (a test proves the code was written, not that it runs), in the entry that quotes P234. Closed with four tests that drive a REAL `asyncio.TimeoutError` through `_fetch_series`/`_fetch_releases` and assert the emitted line carries the new format, the consequence, and exactly one ERROR per sustained streak; reverting one handler to its bare `logger.error` turns three of them red. The watchdog half was verified the same way — the real `sleeve_fast_risk_action` + `on_exit_failed` chain, end to end: `SKIPPED_STALE` without reaching `execute_target`, and `evaluate()` still returning **EXIT_ONLY** afterwards with no suppression state written. **Mitigation pattern: when a fix routes existing logging through a new helper, the helper's tests are not the fix's tests — induce the real failure at the real call site, or the wiring is unpinned exactly where it matters.**
- **Numbering:** shipped as P327/P327b and renumbered to P329/P329b — the parallel session claimed 327 and 328 in the same tree. Seventh collision of the campaign.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) a backoff is a claim that *retrying will fail the same way* — before adding one, ask whether the failure is structural or transient, because for a safety control the two demand opposite responses; (b) when a control refuses to act on missing data, check what the REFUSAL is then used for — here a correct P141 refusal was fed into a mechanism that converted it into 30 minutes of blindness; (c) an alert's severity must track the action available to the operator, and when two alerts about the same event disagree by three levels in the same second, one of them has not been looked at since it was written.

### P338. [AUDITED + FIXED 2026-08-20, operator: "i want a line by line audit" -> "go ahead with all fixes"] Entry-quality filters were LIQUIDATING held sleeve positions, and the whole execution-layer rejection surface — 23 reasons — defaulted to the same thing
A read-through of the live money path (`run_live` end to end, the sleeve driver, the veto→sleeve translation chain, `execute_intent_v2`'s rejection surface, the entry gates) plus mechanical sweeps. Tests `tests/test_p338_entry_vetoes_and_dead_switches.py` (51) + the P276 guard corpus widened (`tests/test_sleeve_gated_intent_p206.py` 37); eight falsification probes red-then-restored through the P328 harness; full suite green; gate clean, **zero baseline movement**.
- **THE ROOT CAUSE OF THE TOP TWO FINDINGS IS ONE FACT, and P275 recorded half of it.** In LIVE, `_get_effective_position_state` (main.py:~24083) reads `market_data["current_exposure"]`, which `data_mgmt/market_data_pipeline.py:2073` fills from `_paper_positions` — the KRAKEN book, `{}` since the 2026-06-13 flatten. So **for a routed asset it is structurally 0.0 no matter what the Coinbase sleeve holds.** P275 traced that to the EXIT stack and correctly called the result dormant (`soft_stop`/`gambler_exit`/`exit_alpha`/`adaptive_stop` cannot fire on the sleeve). **Nobody traced it forward to the ENTRY gates, where it is not dormant but wrong — and wrong in both directions.**
- **[LIVE] An SNR/volume/structure ENTRY FILTER was market-flattening a HELD perp.** BitBeast (`[WIRE-VETO]`, ACTIVE on all three assets) decides "is this a NEW entry?" from that always-zero exposure, so every actionable un-vetoed tick reads as a new entry **even while the sleeve is long**; its rejection writes `[VETO] …`, which `_SLEEVE_FLATTEN_INTENDED_VETOES` classified as flatten. Verified through the real translator, not inferred: `[VETO] SNR too low: 0.81 < 1.20` → `FLATTEN(target=0.0)`. **That is the P287 category error — "an ENTRY-quality veto; flattening a HELD position on it is a category error" — in three more guards**: `[OP_BUDGET]` (concurrent-OPPORTUNITY budget) and `[GAMBLER_GATE]` (`tranche_target == 1`) have the same shape, narrower. All three now HOLD. **HOLD is not a loosening and the test says so explicitly:** from flat, HOLD leaves the book flat, so the rejected entry is still rejected; from a position, the position survives with the P197 venue stop still guarding it and every real exit path (signal flatten, stop, FastRisk, FORCE_FLAT, fuse) untouched.
- **THE SAME FACT, POINTING THE OTHER WAY, and deliberately NOT fixed.** The STRUCTURE fractal-break veto (main.py:~14138) fires only when `_existing_exp >= 0.001`, so for a routed asset it **can never fire** — every tick takes the T1-bypass and applies a permanent **×0.75** size haircut instead. Its two roster entries are classifications with no reachable write site. Making structure sleeve-aware means feeding sleeve state into `position_state`, which would arm the entire dormant exit stack on a live account — a P141 activation, not a bugfix. Recorded at the roster, pinned by test.
- **[LATENT, and the guard that should have caught it had a blind spot] ALL 23 of `execute_intent_v2`'s rejection reasons defaulted to LIQUIDATE.** main.py:~17053 stamps any non-benign execution result onto `intent.veto_reason` (prefixing `[EXECUTION] ` when not already bracketed) — the SAME object `_live_intents` hands the translator later in the same loop iteration. Measured by running every one through the real translator: **23/23 FLATTEN, 0 in any roster.** Several are unambiguously *wait / smaller / not THIS order*: `min hold:` (blocking an EXIT), `per-asset`/`global rate limit:`, `[REBUILD_COOLDOWN]`, `ADDON_BLOCKED` (a refused pyramid add liquidating the base), `[FLIP_GATE] alpha=` (a refused FLIP means keep the position), `[AUDIT M3] Below Kraken min:`, `EXPOSURE_BELOW_MINIMUM_VIABLE` (Kraken fee economics). Now classified: 14 HOLD, 9 flatten-intended, two of the nine (`ExecutionGuard:`, `[AUTHORITY_CHAIN]`) kept AS-CURRENT with the reason, because each is a MIXED bag and splitting it needs its own analysis (the P265 `[PATCH-1] Schema fail:` precedent).
- **One of the 23 is REACHABLE TODAY, and it is a data fault.** `Invalid price` (`core/execution_service.py:608`) sits **BEFORE** the P152 routed-asset skip at :648 — every other reason is behind it and therefore unreachable while the Kraken book is empty. So an unreadable `current_price` on an actionable tick stamped `[EXECUTION] Invalid price` and **liquidated the perp book**: the P265b data-integrity class, through a door P265b's enumeration did not cover. Pinned by a test that asserts the ordering, so if it ever moves the reachability is re-derived rather than assumed.
- **THE DURABLE FIX IS THE GUARD, not the strings.** P276 built the roster-completeness test precisely so "a NEW veto writer goes RED until its author classifies it", and P287 widened its corpus once for exactly this reason. **Its corpus still omitted `core/execution_service.py`** — the one file that mutates `intent.veto_reason` in-tick (:1080) and supplies 23 further reasons indirectly. It was invisible for a second reason too: the extractor only understood the `veto_reason = "..."` assignment form, while these arrive as `return {"reason": "..."}`. The guard now models **the actual path by which a reason becomes a veto**, applying main.py's own stamping rule and importing the benign roster from the producer (P172) rather than restating it. Anti-vacuity: it must extract ≥15, and it currently extracts exactly the 23.
- **[LIVE, bounded] A check that could never take its other branch, converting an ABSENCE into an active penalty.** `signals/profit_max_adapter.py:~729`: `is_aligned = (htf > 0 and dir > 0) or (htf < 0 and dir < 0)`. **`htf_trend_direction` has NO producer anywhere** — P174 recorded it as COPY_ONLY (main.py:12497 relays a market_data key nothing writes) and the repo's own orphan scanner still reports it that way; its only fallback, `agent_signals["lead_lag_edge"]`, is a cross-exchange dislocation in bps rather than a daily trend. So in EXHAUSTION `is_aligned` was **always False** → a permanent ×0.7 conviction and ×0.7 urgency penalty stamped `"htf_aligned": False`, asserting a counter-trend position on a feature that does not exist (P2/P223). Now three branches: unknown is RECORDED as unknown and takes the phase-generic ×0.9; the heavy counter-HTF penalty is **preserved intact** and becomes reachable the moment a real producer exists. **Fail direction stated because this is a LOOSENING on the no-reading path (×0.7 → ×0.9), and measured inert on the venue that trades:** `conviction_multiplier` reaches only `intent.quant_confidence` and `execution_urgency` only `core/execution_service.py`, both past the P152 return — so it changes no live order today and would if the exposure channel is reconnected.
- **A guard of mine that was too broad, caught by its own first run.** The premise pin ("`htf_trend_direction` still has no producer") first matched ANY subscript write and fired on the known COPY_ONLY relay — a detector that cannot tell a copy from a computation would have to be silenced, and a silenced guard is worse than none (P330's lesson, one day later). Rewritten with `ast` to flag only a write whose VALUE does not itself read the same key, plus an anti-vacuity test that a genuine computation is still detected (P174).
- **[LATENT] A 13th profit_max switch that controlled nothing — and it is the only one that could turn off the branch above.** `profit_max_exhaustion_requires_htf_alignment` (main.py:2048) is declared, has an exact counterpart consumed at `profit_max_adapter.py:~735`, and was **neither parsed (`from_file` / `_LATE_CONFIG_KEYS`) nor passed into `ProfitMaxConfig(...)`** at :4498. P313's sweep took that roster from 3/12 to 12/12 and missed this one, on both halves. Behaviour-neutral (both defaults True) and verified by round-trip: the live profile still reads True, and a profile setting it false now reads False where it previously returned True. The ctor pin is **structural (`ast`), not a substring** — a renamed kwarg or a deleted line goes red, which `"x" in src` cannot do (P234/P307b).
- **[MISLEADING] The package that OWNS the Coinbase code printed "Execution Venue: KRAKEN ONLY" on every import.** `exchange/__init__.py` auto-runs `print_exchange_banner()` at import of the package containing `coinbase_adapter.py`, `coinbase_sleeve.py`, `routing.py`, `cutover.py`, and its docstring added *"DO NOT: Add exchange routing/switching"* — both falsified 2026-06-13. Its three constants (`SINGLE_EXCHANGE_MODE`, `ACTIVE_EXCHANGE`, `FROZEN_EXCHANGES`) have **zero readers tree-wide**; the real DEX prohibition is enforced by `core/exchange_guard.py`, which never consults them. Banner and header corrected to name the actual venues; the constants kept (removing a module export is a contract change) and annotated unread. Same class as P239's main.py header fix — **a mitigation applied to one instance of a class is not applied to the class** (P171/P226), and this instance sat in the package the fix was about.
- **Three smaller knobs, dispositioned rather than left "known":** `signals/authority_fusion.py:719` reads `getattr(self, 'confidence_to_exposure_factor', 1.0)` — a "configurable scale factor" with **no assignment anywhere**, so base exposure is exactly `decider_signal.confidence`; annotated at source (the P307 no-effect-switch class, in the reader direction). `defense/governor_integration.py:121` defaulted `ENABLE_REGIME_TRANSITION_BUFFER` to **True** while `configs/sota_flags.py:86` declares it **False** — a getattr default is a policy statement about the ABSENT case and must agree with the declaration or it silently overrides it; corrected and pinned by reading the declared value rather than restating it. `execution.default_order_type` deleted from the live profile with a provenance note: zero consumers, sitting among keys that ARE read, so it read as configuring order type while controlling nothing (the P311 dead-switch treatment).
- **Verified clean, so nobody re-derives it:** orphan-read scanner `ORPHAN=0 HOT=0`; a config-vs-code sweep over the whole live profile found only three unread leaf keys (one deleted here, one already recorded as documentation by P230's own note in the file, one a false positive from a dynamically-composed lookup key); calibration registry 3/3 resolving within horizon; the P321b price interlock behaves correctly on px=0/-1/NaN. **One residual recorded, not fixed:** `resolve_seat_edge(..., price=None)` — `None` BYPASSES that interlock entirely, so the safety currently rests on the call site supplying a price rather than on the signature. P334 pins the call site; a required parameter would be stronger.
- **Numbering:** shipped as P337 and renumbered to P338 — the parallel session claimed 337 mid-work (and used it twice). **Tenth collision of the campaign.** The pre-flight conflict check the operator asked for is what made it cheap: it established zero production-file overlap, that CLAUDE.md was the single shared file, and that a bare `git commit` would sweep their in-flight `sota_integration.py` — so this landed with a **pathspec** (P314) after their entries were committed.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) when a venue changes underneath a system, the state that describes "do we have a position?" changes meaning for every gate that reads it — and the gates split into two failure modes, not one: those that ask "is this a new entry?" start saying YES forever, and those that ask "do we hold something?" start saying NO forever. P275 found the second class and called it dormant; the first class was liquidating positions; (b) a veto string is a CONTRACT the moment any consumer classifies on it, and the classification guard must model the PATH by which a reason reaches the consumer — here 23 reasons were invisible because they travel as a returned dict and are stamped somewhere else; (c) a two-branch check on an input that can be ABSENT is a three-branch check with one case missing, and the missing case is the one that actually occurs.

### P337. [MEASURED 2026-08-20, operator: "do the ma filter and halt vs drawdown"] Three of P325's four exposure gaps DISSOLVE under measurement — including one where acting would have halved live sizing on a units error — and the fourth is unsettleable on 8 observations
Asked to resolve two live-money decisions, both turned out not to need one. New instrument `training/scripts/sleeve_drawdown_probe.py`; report `training/reports/sleeve_drawdown_p337.json`; tests `tests/test_p337_sleeve_drawdown_units.py` (10); two falsification probes red-then-restored. **No config was changed, and that is the finding.**
- **THE HALT GAP IS A UNITS ERROR, and I nearly acted on it.** P325 compared SOL's certified peak-to-trough drawdown (−199 pts at 1.0 exposure) against the 15% sleeve halt. Those are different quantities: the certified figures are peak-to-trough drawdowns of an ADDITIVE per-bar sum (P301's convention), while `CoinbaseSleeve` computes `dd = (basis − equity)/basis` with `basis = sleeve_start_equity + external_flow_usd` — an **INCEPTION anchor with no ratchet** (it reproduces the live `dd=2.3%` exactly). Peak-anchored asks "how much of the gains were given back"; inception-anchored asks "are you below the money you put in". For a profitable book they diverge without limit.
- **Measured over 2020-2026 at the live 0.15 fractions, compounded, with honest per-contract fees (P315) and carry (P245):** the combined book's peak-to-trough drawdown is **−28.1%** while its worst inception-anchored drawdown is **2.5%**, and the 15% halt would have fired **ZERO times in 5.8 years**. **My own intermediate numbers were wrong in the same way** — I first computed a `cummax` drawdown, got "27 firings, halted 33% of bars", and was one step from recommending that live fractions be halved. The correction came from reading the sleeve's actual formula rather than trusting either P325's figure or my own.
- **The residual risk is real, small, and COLD-START shaped.** The halt is forgiving only once a profit buffer exists, and the live sleeve has none (it sits 2.34% BELOW basis). Sampling every possible start date over the history, worst inception-anchored drawdown in the first year: **0.15 → median 6.3%, p90 15.9%, max 22.9%, and 12% of cold starts trip the halt**; 0.10 → median 4.1%, p90 10.7%, 0.3% trip it. **Disposition: leave 0.15.** A 12%-per-cold-start chance of one manual-review event is what a halt is FOR; the alternative (0.075) fits the halt by halving the certified return (+331.5% → +115.0% over 5.8y). Recorded as a decision with its number rather than left as an unexamined default.
- **THE "40% IDLE RISK BUDGET" IS NOT A SIZING PROBLEM EITHER.** The `ma_filter` ledger is **374 of 432 rows `no_target`** — 87% of ticks the book is FLAT, which is the design (P325's own arithmetic: ETH ~6 round trips/year, SOL ~13). The budget is idle because the certified books are flat by construction, not because of fractions or filters. Raising fractions would change nothing about the idleness; it would only scale the rare active periods.
- **THE MA FILTER CANNOT BE SETTLED, and now with a number instead of a shrug.** Its purpose-built ledger (P236) holds **8** `ma_disagrees_entry` rows in 432; whale's holds **2** in 166. That is why `compute_shadow_ic` returns IC **exactly 0.0000** — the scored series is dominated by constant zeros, the P236/P294 shape. Eight observations cannot judge a veto.
  - **A window-shopped near-miss, recorded so nobody repeats it.** `agent_disagreement_review` returns NOT EARNED at the standing 90d window, **EARNED at h4 on 30d** (disagree mean −47.0bps, t=−2.26, n_eff=32.8), and NOT EARNED at 10d. I ran three windows and one passed; **acting on it would have been selection, not evidence** — and the tool's own PER-SEAT-ERA block says the pooled number is "mostly the trend seat's behaviour, not the current book's" (`book_seat` h4: agree n=1, disagree n=2). The 90d window is the pre-committed one and it says NOT EARNED.
  - **A correction to my own framing from earlier today.** I reported the vetoes as "the binding constraint, 7 of 7 ETH entries blocked". Over the ledger's whole history the MA filter blocked **8 of 58 ticks that had a target = 14%**; the 7-of-7 was a current-window artifact of model_alpha sitting in its 3%-of-the-time short reading on ETH. The realized cost remains ≈ **−$0.53**.
  - **Disposition: both LEFT ARMED**, unchanged from P324 — but for a sharper reason. Evidence accrues to the ledgers whether or not enforcement is on (the harness records the counterfactual either way), so there is no evidentiary cost to waiting, and the measured cost of waiting is a fraction of a dollar. What would change the answer is the ledger reaching ~30 `disagrees_entry` rows, which at 14% of actionable ticks and 87% flat is many months away — that is the honest clock, not a date.
- **What the data DID settle, in the other direction:** `model_alpha` is not a saturated output (45d on ETH: +0.00 57% / +1.00 40% / −1.00 3%), so the veto signal is sound and the question is genuinely about policy, not about a bug. That check is what separated P334 (a real code defect, fixed) from this (a preference that measurement cannot decide).
- **Numbering:** shipped as P335 and renumbered to P337 — the parallel session claimed 335 and 336. Tenth collision of the campaign.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) before acting on any "X is tighter than Y", read BOTH definitions at source — here two quantities shared the word "drawdown" and the comparison was invalid, which no amount of re-deriving either number would have revealed; (b) when a gap is stated as a ratio of two recorded figures, the failure mode is usually the units, not the arithmetic; (c) running an instrument at three windows and reporting the one that passes is selection — if a window was not pre-committed, the result is a hypothesis, and saying so is cheaper than the entry that would later have to withdraw it.

### P337. [MEASURED 2026-08-20, operator: "can you tackle these"] The filters CAN be judged now — "structurally forward-only" was half wrong, and against the decider they actually filter their premise INVERTS
I had recorded the two entry filters as unmeasurable until the ~09-07 read, because they "judge AGENT OUTPUTS, which were never stored" (P296's triage, repeated in P324/P332/P335). **That is half right, and the wrong half cost three weeks of assumed waiting.** The ADVISOR is an agent output and IS stored — 130 days of it, in the attribution logs I had already been reading — and the DECIDER is the regimebook, whose direction is DETERMINISTIC from price and funding. So the pair reconstructs backwards. `--decider book` added to `analytics/ic/agent_disagreement_review.py`; tests (34); the P316 rule applied to a wait of my own for the second time in two days.
- **THE RESULT, over 130 days against the RECOMPUTED book rather than the retired trend seat** (signed forward return of the decider's own direction, bps):
  | advisor | agree h1 / h4 | disagree h1 / h4 | contrast (agree−disagree) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | model_alpha | −2.1 / −14.3 | **+7.9 / −7.9** | **−10.0** (t −0.72) / **−6.4** (t −0.14) |
  | whale | +6.1 / −6.0 | +1.8 / −4.2 | +4.3 (t 0.37) / −1.7 (t −0.05) |
  **The filters' premise is that disagreement marks BAD entries. Measured against the book, model_alpha's disagreement marks entries that did BETTER on both horizons**, and whale's marks no difference. The sign is inverted for one and absent for the other.
- **This is thin and must not be over-read.** 61 and 102 disagreements, n_eff 15–28 at h4, every |t| ≤ 0.72 — nothing here is significant, and the earlier P324 numbers (which pointed the filters' way) were not either. What changed is not significance, it is WHICH DECIDER was measured: P324 read the trend seat because that is what held the seat during the window, and the filters do not filter the trend seat any more (P320c: a claim whose premise moved is not evidence for the configuration now running).
- **It is also mostly a BTC measurement** — disagreements split BTC 52 / SOL 8 / ETH 1 for model_alpha, because the book is FLAT most of the time (452 joined records from 2,857 attribution rows). That is the honest reason the sample is small, and it will not improve quickly: the book's flatness is the strategy, not a data gap.
- **What it changes and what it does not.** It does NOT earn a flip — a live control should not move on |t| < 1 in either direction. It DOES remove the expectation that the 09-07 read will vindicate the filters: the best-powered look available, against the right decider, points away from them. Combined with P335's single live instance pointing the OTHER way (+62bps saved), the honest summary is that **nothing measured so far distinguishes these filters from noise, and the two readings disagree with each other**.
- **A misleading caption of my own, fixed in the same pass.** The per-era table prints "the decider's identity changed mid-window (trend -> whale -> regimebook)". Under `--decider book` the decider is CONSTANT, so that caption would claim a change that did not happen and describe a different measurement than the one that ran. It now prints a DATE BAND caption in book mode, pinned by test.
- **Fail directions pinned:** a record predating the book's recomputed history is SKIPPED, never counted as a flat book (P2 — a flat book would land in the agree/disagree buckets and dilute both); and the book series is IMPORTED from `training.funding_legs_lab` rather than restated, so this measures the DEPLOYED book (P172).
- **The falsification harness caught TWO vacuous guards of mine in this entry, both the same cause:** `book is not None` and `continue` each occur TWICE in the file, so both pins matched the innocent site and stayed green with the defect present. Fixed by giving `assert_guard_live` a `near=` anchor that restricts the search to one site AND refuses an anchor that is itself ambiguous. **Anchor uniqueness is never something to assume (P238), and it is no safer inside a shared helper than in a one-off test** — the helper built to close the substring-pin trap had the trap one level up.
- **Mitigation pattern:** "we cannot measure this yet" is a claim with a structure — it names an input that does not exist. Check each input separately: here the advisor was stored all along and the decider was computable, and only their COMBINATION had never been assembled. Three weeks of waiting rested on not decomposing one sentence.

### P336. [INSPECTED + FIXED 2026-08-20, operator: "inspect those unavailable ones and the eth one"] Two boot warnings were about modules retired on purpose, one "unavailable" module is real and stdlib-only — and the ETH veto turns out to have SAVED money on its only instance
Tests `tests/test_p336_archived_imports.py` (5); three falsification probes red-then-restored.
- **THE TWO WARNINGS WERE NOISE, and now say so.** `orchestration.sota_integration` safe_imports **21** modules; exactly **two** are absent — `infra.event_replay` and `core.plugin_registry` — and both exist under `archive/`. So every boot logged two WARNINGs about modules nobody intends to have, which is the P202/P303 shape: an alert whose only resolutions are theatre or ignoring it. `safe_import` now takes `archived=`, reporting an expected absence ONCE at INFO **naming the archive path**; anything else still WARNs. A probe that makes the mute blanket turns the guard red — a guard weakened to admit a known case stops catching the unknown ones (P248) — and a second guard asserts every OTHER safe_import target still resolves, so a third disappearance surfaces rather than being pre-absolved.
- **`enable_event_replay: bool = True` is INERT and annotated.** Its consumer is guarded by `and EventReplayManager`, which is None, so the flag reads as enabled and can never take effect — the P307 no-effect-switch class. Kept (removing a config field is a contract change) with the reason at source.
- **THE THIRD "unavailable" IS REAL, and the P214 reading holds up.** `training/regime/regime_classifier.py` **exists** — 240 lines, **stdlib-only** (logging/dataclasses/datetime/enum/typing) — and is absent only from the IMAGE, because `training/` is dockerignored bar an allowlist. Verified at the call site rather than from the record: its consumer returns immediately unless `intended_direction < 0`, and its only effects are `max_short_exposure *= 0.7` and, below a bearish score, `allow_new_short = False` — **tightening-only, shorts-only**. It is genuinely live-relevant now (BTC's book is currently −1.00), and the veto it would feed, `[V6 SHORT FILTER]`, is deliberately classified **flatten-intended** in the P276 roster. Shipping it is one Dockerfile line and arms a never-executed decision path on a live account — still an operator call (P141), now with the facts checked rather than cited.
- **THE ETH VETO: correct, live, and its one measured instance went the OTHER way to my framing.** `model_alpha` emitted `-1.00 / conf 0.51 / dq 1.0` — a fresh, full-quality reading, so the veto is acting on a live signal, NOT a stale stash (the P265 failure mode it could have been). Measured over the retained log: **8 blocks, all ETH, all `ma=-1.00 dir=+1.00`, 8 of 144 ETH ticks (5.6%)**, BTC and SOL zero — one contiguous window spanning the book's entire first directional stretch (01:46 → 05:00).
- **And it saved money on that window.** ETH moved **−0.62%** from 01:00 to 05:00 while the book wanted LONG, so the block was worth roughly +62bps. **That is n=1 and is not evidence** — a coin flip provides a sign too (P320c) — but it is the opposite of the implication in my previous report, where I described the filter as costing the campaign its first tradeable signal. **The cost framing was a hypothesis; the only observation available contradicts it.**
- **What does NOT change:** the filter still fails its pre-committed bar (P324: model_alpha disagreement −7.8/−31.2bps at t=−1.41/−1.38; contrast t=1.00/1.24), and one favourable instance neither earns it nor should move a live flag. The ~2026-09-07 pooled read (P332) remains the thing that settles it, and it is now measurably reachable rather than theoretical.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) a startup warning about a DELIBERATELY retired module is indistinguishable from one about a broken dependency — mark the expected absence and name where it went, but never by muting the class; (b) before repeating a cost argument, price the actual window: I had a concrete, cheap measurement available (the asset's move during the block) and had asserted the direction without taking it.

### P335. [AUDITED 2026-08-20, operator: "see if any agent, strategy, module that suppose to be on, but off"] Nothing is off by accident — and the audit caught the FIRST tradeable signal of the campaign being vetoed by a filter measured NOT EARNED
A three-part sweep: recorded armings vs the live config, modules reporting themselves unavailable, and per-agent emissions. The config half is clean; the interesting finding was live.
- **CONFIG: zero drift.** All **23** armings this file records were verified present and correct in `configs/live_high_risk.json` — `coinbase_use_gated_intent`, `regimebook_mode=enforce`, `trend_following_mode=enforce`, `coinbase_maker_first`, `venue_aware_spreads`, `venue_true_hold`, `venue_aware_funding`, `per_contract_fees`, `seat_alpha_calibrated`, both filter enforces, the five P298 flips, `sentiment_zscore_mode=historical`, `real_1h_price_change`, `fast_risk_sleeve_enabled`, `alpha_gate_hold_ratio=0.65`, `reentry_cooldown_ticks=2`, `flip_persist_ticks=2`, and the two deliberate falses (`cryptopanic_enabled`, `v5_1_strategies_live`). **Nothing recorded as ON is off.** (A first, regex-based pass reported two "mismatches" — both false positives, a stale historical sentence and a nested dict key. A checker that reads prose cannot distinguish a superseded decision from the current one; the reliable version enumerates the flags explicitly.)
- **MODULES: one genuinely off, and it is billing, not code.** `[LLM_SENTIMENT] Haiku hard-disabled (reason=usage_limit_400_until=2026-09-01T00:00Z)` — the **Anthropic account hit its usage cap**. The agent is NOT dark: it degrades to the headline heuristic and still emits (`src=headline_h…`), and the parallel session's P329c correctly gave the hard-dated 400 a dated backoff instead of retrying it as transient. **Operator action if Haiku is wanted back before 2026-09-01: raise the account cap.** Everything else reporting "unavailable" is recorded-deliberate: the Ensemble Regime Classifier (P214 non-ship), `infra.event_replay` and `core.plugin_registry` (optional by construction), DRL `mode=DISABLED` (SHADOW since P198).
- **AGENTS: the silent ones are starved or by-decision, not broken.** Live trace: `quant` (the decider) directional on BTC −1.00 and ETH +1.00, `sentiment`, `model_alpha` and `drl` (shadow) all emitting; `micro` at dq0.8 with dir 0.00 is the P316 warmup that needs ~20h of uninterrupted uptime and has not had it (this session deployed ~10 times); `kraken_quant`, `vol_alpha`, `v5_1_strats`, `two_stage`, `short_bias` are the P215/P216/P228 recorded set.
- **THE LIVE FINDING, and it is the one that matters for making money.** ETH's book turned directional for the first time this campaign, the alpha gate **PASSED** it — `AlphaGate={pass=True,est=66,thresh=60}` — and the sleeve then reported:
  ```
  COINBASE-MANAGE] tick summary: BTC=NOOP(dir=+0.00), ETH=MA_VETO(entry,ma=-1.00,dir=+1.00), SOL=NOOP
  ```
  **The first tradeable signal of the campaign was blocked by `coinbase_ma_filter_enforce`** — the filter P324 measured as NOT EARNED (model_alpha disagreement: −7.8/−31.2bps at t=−1.41/−1.38; contrast t=1.00/1.24; both fail the pre-committed bar). It reproduced across the P334 deploy, so it is a standing block, not a one-tick artifact.
- **This converts P332's arithmetic into an observation.** The book takes ~6 round trips a YEAR on ETH; a veto that blocks entries on model_alpha disagreement — which fires on roughly half of ticks — removes a large fraction of a very small number of trades, on a mechanism certified over six years, on the authority of a signal whose own instrument cannot distinguish it from noise. **P324 left both filters armed because the measurement could not discriminate; what has changed is that the cost is now being paid and can be seen.**
- **NOT disarmed here.** Removing a veto is a LOOSENING on a live account, which is the operator's call (P141) — the same rule that kept it from being armed silently. The revert is one line: `"coinbase_ma_filter_enforce": false`. The alternative is to accept the block until the ~2026-09-07 pooled read (P332) settles the filter on its own ledger.
- **Mitigation pattern:** an audit of "what is off that should be on" should also ask what is ON that is blocking something — the config half of this sweep was clean, and the finding came from watching one tick of the live decision chain end to end.

### P334. [CORRECTED 2026-08-20, operator: "resolve sol's fee issue"] The fee is a PERCENTAGE of notional, not a flat charge per contract — P315's structural claim rested on 5 fills spanning no price range
Resolving SOL's assumed fee produced a venue quote for all three assets, and the quotes refute the model. Tests `tests/test_p315_per_contract_fees.py` rewritten where they asserted the refuted property (23); lab re-priced.
- **HOW SOL RESOLVED, and it needed no fill.** The CDE SDK's **preview** endpoint returns the fee for a payload without creating an order — the P195 probe already used it and nobody had read it for FEES. Run in-container read-only: **BTC $0.67, ETH $0.30, SOL $0.46**.
- **THE REFUTATION, from the only within-asset price move available.** P315 concluded a flat ~$0.60/contract from 5 BTC fills at **64,105–64,330** — a price range of 0.35%, which cannot distinguish a flat dollar fee from a percentage. Today BTC is 69,280:
  | asset | source | px | notional | fee$ | **fee_bps** |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | BTC | fill (maker) | 64,105 | 641.05 | 0.601 | **9.38** |
  | BTC | fill (taker) | 64,330 | 643.30 | 0.635 | **9.87** |
  | BTC | preview | 69,280 | 692.80 | 0.670 | **9.67** |
  | ETH | fill (maker) | 1,916 | 191.65 | 0.264 | **13.78** |
  | ETH | preview | 2,252 | 225.23 | 0.300 | **13.32** |
  | SOL | preview | 84.44 | 422.20 | 0.460 | **10.90** |
  **Price +7.7%, fee +5.5%.** A flat fee predicts **+0.0%**; a percentage predicts **+7.7%**. bps are stable per asset across the move; dollars per contract are not. `CDE_FEE_PER_CONTRACT_USD` is now `CDE_FEE_BPS`, price-invariant.
- **WHAT THIS VOIDS, stated plainly because P315 is cited a lot.** Its six-year re-pricing charged a flat $0.635/contract from BTC $10k to $69k — **~63bps/leg in the early years against a true ~9.9**. Re-run at the corrected rate: book **144.50 → 187.98** (Sharpe 0.558 → **0.727**), trend_only 120.53 → **132.56**, buy_and_hold 186.26 → **193.99** (Sharpe 0.575), cost **131.96 → 88.48**, funding-leg increment 23.97 → **55.42**. P315's DIRECTION survives — the book still loses to holding on return — but by **6 points, not 42**, and the book now beats holding on Sharpe by a clear margin. **P315's contract-size sweep is void outright**: with a percentage fee, contract denomination does not change bps at all, so "the strategy needs 2x the contract size" was an artifact of the refuted model.
- **SOL's value did NOT move, and that is the point.** A quote is not a fill, and P315's revision rule lowers a fee only on ≥20 filled legs. SOL stays at the worst measured rate (**14.51bps**, ETH's taker) with provenance still `assumed_no_measured_fill` — but the venue's own 10.90 is recorded beside it as `CDE_FEE_BPS_VENUE_QUOTE`, so the assumption is now **auditably conservative rather than arbitrary**. That is what "resolved" means here: the number stopped being unverified.
- **Live effect, all small and mostly the safe direction:** at today's prices BTC taker 9.17 → **9.87**, ETH 12.34 → **14.51** (both RAISES), SOL 15.04 → **14.51** (−0.5bps). The gate is priced on current prices, so it was never exposed to the low-price error; only the BACKTEST was.
- **Two guards that had to change with the model, and one dead branch I created and caught.** The tests asserting "bps double when price halves" and "the smaller-notional contract is dearer" encoded the refuted property — rewritten to assert price-INVARIANCE and the per-asset RATE difference (ETH is dearer than BTC for a real reason, just not the one P315 gave). And the lab's refusal became unreachable, because the unknown-asset fallback always returns a number: a None-only check reads exactly like a guard that never fires (P174). It now refuses on the fallback too — pricing six years of one asset on another asset's fee is the same fabrication as the 3bps constant.
- **The price is still REQUIRED even though it no longer enters the arithmetic**, deliberately: `resolve_seat_edge` uses "can this asset be priced" as its effect-interlock (P321b), and dropping the dependency would silently make that interlock inert — a check that cannot tell "armed" from "took effect", which is exactly what P321b exists to prevent.
- **Mitigation pattern:** a model fitted over a range that does not vary is not a model, it is a coincidence — before asserting a functional FORM (flat vs proportional), check the spread of the independent variable in the sample. P315 had 0.35% of price range and drew a structural conclusion from it; 8% was enough to refute it.

### P334. [FIXED 2026-08-20, operator: "can we leverage the existing data to check if there is code revision needed"] The T1 volume-collapse guard was INVERTED at tick time — normal volume refused, severe collapse admitted — because one call site of eleven discarded the correction its own producer computed
The operator declined a risk-preference decision and asked whether the DATA could settle a code question instead. It could, and this is what it found. Fix in `defense/constitution.py`; tests `tests/test_p334_volume_collapse_guard_inversion.py` (12); two falsification probes red-then-restored.
- **THE OBSERVATION, from one live line.** 2026-08-20 04:11:44, ETH's certified book long and through the gate — `[REGIMEBOOK-SEAT] ETH: dir=+1.0 edge=88bps`, `ALPHA_GATE: PASS (66bps > 55bps)` — and then `[TRANCHE] ETH: refusing T1 entry in volume collapse (effective=0.021, raw=0.021, bar_progress=0.04)`. **`effective == raw` is the tell:** the producer computes `volume_ratio_effective = max(raw, raw / max(bar_progress, 0.20))` = **0.105** for that tick, comfortably above the 0.05 threshold, and the guard used 0.021.
- **THE MECHANISM, provable from code alone.** `effective_volume_ratio(md, is_4h_bar_close=True)` returns the RAW ratio by its first branch, and **`is_4h_bar_close` has no producer** (P173: nothing writes the key, so main.py's `.get("is_4h_bar_close", True)` is always True). Of **eleven** call sites in the tree, ten pass nothing (default False) and get the correction; only the live T1 entry guard passed the flag — so the one consumer gating real entries was the one throwing the correction away.
- **AND IT DOES NOT MERELY ADD NOISE — IT INVERTS THE GUARD.** `volume_ratio` is current-bar-volume / SMA(**full**-bar volumes), and the 4H tick fires ~10 min into a new bar, so `bar_progress ≈ 0.04` on EVERY tick. With true volume pace k the raw ratio is ≈ k×0.04, against a refuse-band of `[0.01, 0.05)`:
  | condition | raw | verdict |
  |---|---|---|
  | normal (k=1.0) | 0.040 | **REFUSED** — false positive |
  | half volume (k=0.5) | 0.020 | REFUSED — *this is what blocked ETH* |
  | severe collapse (k=0.2) | 0.008 | **ALLOWED** — falls below the `>= 0.01` artifact clause |
  **The worse the collapse, the more likely it passed the guard meant to catch it.** With the producer's correction the direction is restored: normal 0.040 → 0.200 (allow), severe 0.008 → 0.040 (refuse).
- **The fix is to pass nothing**, matching the other ten sites. The correction is **self-disabling on a complete bar** (`bar_progress >= 1.0` → `effective == raw`), so the flag was redundant with information `market_data` already carries — which is why nobody else passes it. This is not a loosening chosen on a hunch: it makes one consumer consistent with the helper's documented purpose (*"Use pace-adjusted volume on partial 4H bars to avoid false collapse reads"*) and tightens the severe-collapse case that was previously admitted.
- **The premise is pinned, not assumed.** A test scans the tree for any write of `is_4h_bar_close` and fails if one appears — the whole analysis rests on the key being absent, and P173 already recorded that a future producer would change the meaning of the default (there it was load-bearing for the T1→T2 escalation gate, where True is correct). **One flag, two consumers, opposite correct values.**
- **What the data ALSO settled, in the other direction — no code change.** The same investigation tested whether the two P324 vetoes were a saturated-output defect (the P224 shape). They are not: over 45 days of attribution `model_alpha` on ETH reads **+0.00 57% / +1.00 40% / −1.00 3%**, and `whale` **+0.00 63% / −1.00 19% / +1.00 18%** — real, varying, mostly-silent signals. Tonight's persistent `ma=-1.00` on ETH is a genuine 3%-of-the-time reading, not a stuck value. **P324's conclusion stands: that one is a risk preference, not a bug**, and asking the data whether it was a bug is what established the difference.
- **Scope correction the data forced on my own framing.** I had presented P325's "~40% of the sleeve risk budget idle" as a sizing decision. It is not: ETH's entries were blocked **7 of 7** by the vetoes and, on the one tick that got past them, by this guard. Raising fractions would have changed nothing. **Gap 3 was downstream of gap 4 and of this defect** — and the realized cost so far is ≈ **−$0.53** (ETH is −0.03% since its book turned long 4h ago), so this is a structural finding, not an observed loss.
- **Numbering:** shipped as P331 and renumbered to P334 — the parallel session had claimed 331 through 333. Ninth collision of the campaign.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) when a guard reads a derived quantity, check whether the DERIVATION reached it — `effective == raw` in a log line is a one-glance proof that a correction was computed and discarded; (b) a boolean whose two consumers need opposite values is not a flag, it is two flags sharing a name, and the one with no producer will silently pick the wrong side for somebody; (c) before deciding whether to loosen a control on preference, ask whether it is doing what it says at all — this one was refusing normal markets and admitting the collapses it existed to stop.

### P333. [BUILT 2026-08-20, operator: "finish all remaining tasks"] The September read dates had no alarm — five of six lived only in a doc and a script nobody runs unless they remember
The last structural gap in the September machinery. The P237 trend tripwire is croned and shouts on its own date; the other five read dates (09-07 through 09-16) did not exist anywhere that runs. **"A decision procedure that depends on someone re-running a tool by hand quietly becomes never" is this repo's own finding (P230)** — it is why the P199 shadow-IC gate sat unrun for months. Tests appended to `tests/test_p271_breadth_september.py` (26); four falsification probes red-then-restored.
- **The split that makes it schedulable:** the countdown is pure date arithmetic over the `CANDIDATES` table — no ledgers, no parquets, no network — while the EXAM needs the operator's OHLCV (P213). So `--countdown-only` is the ALARM half and runs in-container on a cron (Mondays 06:50), exiting **3** when a read window has closed and **0** when nothing is due. A test asserts the alarm block touches neither the pull nor the scorer, or it could not be croned at all.
- **The alarm states what it is NOT.** When it fires it prints the exact command, points at the decision tree, and says the promotion is a config change a human makes (P141) — because an alarm that reads like "the read happened" is worse than none. Pinned by test.
- **Also fixed in the same pass: `september_check` ran the scorer only per-asset** — see P332; both reads now run and the pooled one governs for a poolable family.
- **A trap I walked into writing the guard, for the fourth time this session:** the test forbade `refresh` inside the alarm block, and the block's own comment says *"no pull, no refresh, no scoring"* — so the scanner matched its own explanation (P177). Fixed by routing through `tests/_source_scan.code_only`, which strips comments. **Any test that asserts a string is ABSENT from source must scan comment-stripped code**, or it will eventually fail on the sentence explaining why the string is absent.
- **Measured while sweeping, and NOT a defect — recorded so it is not re-investigated.** `[REGIME] Low confidence (nav=0.17, gmm=0.54) — reducing exposure to 50%` looked like a threshold calibrated against the pre-P307 saturated GMM (~0.99997) now tripping on honest posteriors. Counted since the refit: **19 cold-start skips vs 5 penalties** (79% escape rate), and the 5 fired at gmm 0.53–0.57, genuinely below the 0.6 hatch. The control is working as designed on honest input; the suspicion was reasonable and the measurement refuted it (the P300 pattern).
- **Mitigation pattern:** when a decision has a DATE, something that runs must know the date. Splitting a tool into an alarm half (no data dependencies, croned) and an exam half (operator-local) costs one flag and converts a calendar entry into a mechanism.

### P332. [FIXED 2026-08-20, operator listed the four remaining items] The September reads were wired to a clock that CANNOT FIRE — and auditing the other three "waits" confirmed them real, with numbers
Applied P316's own rule to my own remaining items ("we have to wait" deserves the same audit as any other claim). One of the four was a defect; three are genuine, and now say so with measurements instead of estimates. Tests appended to `tests/test_p271_breadth_september.py` (22); pre-commitment recorded in `docs/SEPTEMBER_DECISION_TREE.md` BEFORE any read.
- **THE DEFECT: `september_check.py` ran the per-asset read only.** P293g measured that a 30-day window can certify only **IC ≥ 0.302 at the 16h horizon** against an economic bar of **~0.13** — so every 16h September verdict would have been computed on a clock that cannot fire. That is the P174 shape (a check that cannot pass) sitting on the reads this entire roster is waiting for, and P299 had already built `--pool-assets` for exactly it. **Nothing called it** (P170: a mechanism nothing calls is decoration).
- **Measured on the pulled ledgers the day this landed, pooling roughly TRIPLES n:** `ma_filtered` 116 → **348**, `regimebook` 101 (BTC) → **573**, `regimebook_adj` → **300**, `whale_filtered` → **90**. The scorer now runs BOTH ways and a refusal in either is a refusal — a missing pooled read must not hide behind a healthy per-asset one (P199, one level up).
- **WHICH READ GOVERNS is now pre-committed, 18–27 days before the earliest read**, because deciding it after seeing a verdict is a goalpost move: for a `POOLABLE_FAMILIES` member the **POOLED** read governs promote/kill; for anything else the per-asset read governs, since merging genuinely per-asset claims is the P294 defect. The per-asset table is printed either way as **diagnosis** — it shows WHERE a family works, which is what P307c needed to reconcile two labs that appeared to disagree — and a pooled PASS whose breakdown is positive on one asset and negative on the others is an era/asset-fragility finding to record, not a reason to promote (P243/P244).
- **The other three waits, audited rather than restated:**
  * **The two entry vetoes: genuinely forward-only, verified at the record.** `ma_filter`/`whale_filter` judge AGENT OUTPUTS, which were never stored historically (P296's triage), so no offline shortcut exists — unlike `sentvariant`, which P296 settled on 3,116 days of history. The wait is a data fact, not a clock choice.
  * **SOL's assumed fee: still 0 measured legs.** `fill_quality.jsonl` holds **BTC 7, ETH 2** records and **no SOL fill at all**, so the P315 `assumed_no_measured_fill` provenance stands. Worth stating precisely: P315's revision rule needs **≥20 filled legs** per asset to LOWER a fee, so even BTC (7) cannot yet be re-derived downward — the constants may only be RAISED until then.
  * **The banded promotion: a real calendar wait (2026-09-09, 20 days)** — and the defect above is exactly what would have made that read produce nothing.
- **STANDING OPERATOR DECISION, reaffirmed 2026-08-20: LEAVE THE XRP.** 12,300 XRP (~$12,385 at $1.007, 53% of the $23,205 portfolio) stays unmanaged on Kraken by explicit instruction, first recorded in P272 and repeated here. It is not a gap, it is a decision — the system manages the sleeve and does not trade, value or risk-account for the bag. Recorded so the next audit reports it as a choice rather than re-deriving it as a finding.
- **Mitigation pattern:** an instrument that ASSEMBLES a decision procedure has to be checked against the arithmetic of the gate it feeds — this one pulled, refreshed, scored, counted down and printed a verdict table, and the one thing nobody had checked was whether the exam it ran could pass at the horizon that matters.

### P331. [FIXED 2026-08-20, operator: "resolve all remaining work"] The venue's HTML error page was still reaching the log — P304 closed two of three doors, and the third fired on a live 502
Found by a routine post-deploy sweep, not by a report: `::CLOUDFLARE_ERROR_1000S_BOX::` sitting in the engine log with ~30 lines of markup around it. Tests appended to `tests/test_p304_vendor_log_hygiene.py` (34); two falsification probes red-then-restored through the P328 harness.
- **The source, traced rather than guessed:** the last timestamped line before the markup was `exchange.coinbase_sleeve | WARNING | [COINBASE_SLEEVE] reconcile failed: HTTPError: 502 Server Error: Bad Gateway <html>`. P304 stripped the vendor page from the SDK logger AND from this file's EQUITY warning — and left the RECONCILE warning interpolating the raw exception. **A mitigation applied to one instance of a class is not applied to the class**: P171 for the BOM, P226 for the scanners, P323 for the timestamps, here for the HTML, in the same file that already imported the stripper eighty lines below.
- **The guard is written for the CLASS, not the instance.** Rather than pin the one line that was found, a test asserts that NO `logger.*` call in the sleeve interpolates a bare `{e}` — the whole file, forever. A probe that adds a brand-new raw-exception log call elsewhere in the file turns it red, which is the direction that matters: the next 5xx will arrive through whichever door nobody has looked at.
- **Fixed a mypy finding I was about to introduce, and one that was already there.** The optional-import fallback `def _shb(x): return x` does not match `strip_html_body(msg: str) -> str`, so mypy flags the conditional variants `[misc]` — `exchange/` is in CRITICAL_DIRS, so that is a CI failure. Both fallbacks (mine and P304's) now carry the real signature: **sleeve findings 2 → 0**, fixed at source and never baselined (P284b/P287g). A fallback that does not type like the thing it replaces is a latent defect as well as a gate failure.
- **Severity, stated honestly:** cosmetic. The 502 itself was handled correctly — the sleeve refused to act on a stale snapshot, and the parallel session's P329 fixed the watchdog that the same 502 disarmed. What was wrong was that an operator reading the log got a Cloudflare page instead of a status code.
- **Mitigation pattern:** after fixing a vendor-noise class, grep the SAME FILE for every other site that formats an exception — P304 fixed the two it was looking at and the third sat eighty lines away, in a handler that fires on exactly the same failure.

### P330. [FIXED 2026-08-19, operator quoted the two residual items] The condition-pin scanner was flagging PROSE, and the commented-out-line defeat got a shared helper — both found by the P328 harness, twice inside the helper written to close them
Two items were left as "reworded my message" and "fixed with a trap". Neither was resolved at its cause. Tests: `tests/test_p307_gmm_and_defaults.py` (36) + `tests/test_p328_falsify_harness.py` (23); eight falsification probes red-then-restored, all run through the P328 harness.
- **THE FALSE POSITIVE WAS THE GUARD'S, NOT THE AUTHOR'S — and its own roster proves it.** The condition-pin detector matched `==|!=|>=|<=|not|and|or|is` inside any asserted string, so English prose containing one of those words read as a source pin of a condition. Of the **9** entries it had accumulated, **2 were prose**: `reconnect loop is armed` and the bare word `not`. It had also sent three authors to reword a log message (P317 once, P328 twice). **Three reworded messages is how a guard gets weakened by the fourth author**, so the fix is precision, never leniency.
- **`_is_condition()` requires the captured text to PARSE as a Python condition** — an expression containing a `Compare`, a `BoolOp`, or a `not`. Prose does not parse at all.
