Measured against every known case: all **7** genuine pins survive (`resp.status == 304`, `_ws_press is None`, `ri.published_at < cutoff`, `_enf_usd if _enf_usd is not None else 0.0`, `-1.0 if _exf > 1e6 else (...)`, `(_live_intents or {}).get(_m_a)`, `i.source == name`), both prose entries drop, and all three historical false positives drop. Precision **7/9 → 7/7**, recall unchanged, roster lowered **9 → 7** as the parallel session's own staleness test demands.
- **RECALL IS PINNED EXPLICITLY, because that is the direction that goes quiet.** A detector that stops seeing real pins reports clean, and clean is what a healthy repo also reports. `TestTheConditionPinDetectorIsPrecise` parametrises the ten real conditions that MUST be detected and the seven prose strings that must not, plus an anti-vacuity check that every surviving roster entry is itself parseable. A probe that drops `ast.Compare` from the detector turns **8** tests red.
- **THE SECOND ITEM: `assert_live_line` in `tests/_guard_pins.py`.** P311 built `assert_guard_live` for Python conditions; there was no analogue for a line in a shell script, YAML or Dockerfile, where the cheapest defeat is simply a `#`. P328b hit exactly that — a pin on the deploy script's cleanup trap passed with the trap commented out. The hand-rolled check is now shared, and it distinguishes ABSENT from COMMENTED, because collapsing them sends the author looking for the wrong thing (P2 applied to an error message).
- **The helper needed two rounds, both caught by the harness rather than by me.** (a) The first version only rejected a line STARTING with a marker, so `true # trap ...` still read as live — the probe came back VACUOUS. Fixed by stripping inline comments. (b) The fix then listed `--` among the markers and **cut the very line it existed to protect**: shell long flags (`--force`, `--detach`) are preceded by whitespace and look exactly like a SQL comment, so the pinned text vanished and every guard read as dead — the probes came back `ALREADY RED`. **That is the same over-broad-detector mistake this entry had just fixed in the condition-pin scanner, committed minutes later by the author fixing it.** Markers narrowed to `#` and `//`, overridable per caller, with the reason at source and a test that a long flag is never treated as a comment.
- **A deliberate asymmetry, stated because it is a judgement call:** a comment marker inside a quoted string is cut too. For a PIN the safe error is "this looks commented" — it fails loudly — rather than "this looks live", which passes over dead code.
- **The harness's scoreboard for one session: four weak guards caught, three of them mine, two inside the helper written to close the class.** Every one printed as a passing line under the old ad-hoc style.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) when a guard produces its third false positive, the guard is the defect — fix its PRECISION and pin its RECALL in the same change, because the tempting alternative (a blanket exemption, or rewording the fourth message) is how a real guard dies; (b) a detector for "X is present in source" must model how X is DEFEATED, and the two cheapest defeats are a comment and a prefix, neither of which a substring can see; (c) when narrowing a detector, check its markers against the file type it will actually read — `--` is a comment in SQL and a flag in every shell script this repo owns.

### P328. [BUILT 2026-08-19, operator: "resolve these"] Two verifications that measured the WRONG SUBJECT — a probe that checked a file change instead of a behaviour change, and a deploy gate that checked the checkout instead of the commit
Both were recorded as lessons in P327 and neither had a mechanism. They are the same shape one level up from the defects they guard: **the check ran, reported, and was about something other than what it claimed.** Harness `tools/falsify.py`, deploy fix in `scripts/hetzner_deploy.sh`, tests `tests/test_p328_falsify_harness.py` (16); six falsification probes red-then-restored — run through the new harness itself.
- **HALF ONE: a probe that breaks nothing is now an ERROR, not a line of output.** This repo falsifies its guards by hand, and the discipline has caught real vacuous guards (P174, P238, P293d, P319). But the harness was an ad-hoc script per entry whose verdict was a line a human had to NOTICE:
  ```
  PROBE a stamped module is unregistered  -> 37 passed
  ```
  That line means **the probe failed to probe**, and it is visually identical to a line meaning the guard is fine. In P327 it was mine: the probe added a COMMENT, matched its anchor exactly once, changed the file, exercised nothing.
- **`Probe` declares what it expects to break, and five clauses are enforced — each one a way a probe lies:** the anchor matches EXACTLY once (zero = never applied, an "ANCHOR MISS" that is easy to skim; more than one = it edited somewhere you did not read, P238); the replacement actually CHANGES the file; the targets were GREEN BEFORE (probing an already-red suite proves nothing, and the red is usually someone else's); the targets go RED after — **the clause that catches the P327 case**; and the file restores BYTE-IDENTICALLY, verified by comparison, in a `finally`, with no `git checkout` anywhere (P265: reversal by checkout reverts to HEAD rather than to the pre-probe state, which silently discards uncommitted work — pinned by a test that the harness never shells out to git).
- **It paid for itself on its FIRST real use, against a guard I had just written.** Probing "a failed scan no longer refuses the deploy" came back **VACUOUS**: my test asserted `"SCAN_RC" in src`, and replacing the guard with `if [ 0 -ne 0 ]` left that symbol present elsewhere. The guard was strengthened to pin the exact conditional plus the refusal that must follow it, then re-probed red. Under the old style that probe would have printed `-> 5 passed` and I would have moved on.
- **HALF TWO: the deploy pre-flight scanned the working tree, so in a shared checkout it adjudicated another session's uncommitted edits.** It happened: `9dff12e` was clean — CI green, and a clean clone's gate green — and the deploy refused on **+4 swallow findings in `fred_feed.py` / `coinbase_sleeve.py` / `fast_risk_tick.py` / `main.py`**, all files a parallel session was still editing. Step 1 pulls `origin/main`, so scanning the checkout answers a different question. **The working tree cannot tell you what you committed (P311b).** Step 0b now builds a worktree at `DEPLOY_SHA` and scans THAT; measured live, the same moment: working tree **exit 1**, worktree at the deployed sha **exit 0**.
- **A worktree rather than a file copy, for a specific reason.** `git archive | tar -x` produces a tree with no `.git`, and the authority audit REFUSES to run without a git-grep engine rather than emit false findings (P158) — so a bare copy cannot be scanned at all, which I confirmed by trying it (exit 2). A test asserts `git grep -P` works inside a worktree, so the claim behind the design choice is pinned rather than asserted.
- **The fallback ANNOUNCES that it changed subject.** If the worktree cannot be built the scan degrades to the checkout and says so, naming what that measures. A silently different subject is the entire defect; a degraded path that hides the degradation would reproduce it.
- **Two self-inflicted problems fixed in the same pass, both familiar:** a `subprocess.run(..., text=True)` without `encoding=` in my own test (the P294 guard caught it — on Windows that decodes with the ambient codepage), and the P307 condition-pin scanner flagging `assert "WARNING: could not build a worktree" in src` because of the bare word **"not"**. That is the P317 false positive for the **third** time; as there, the assertion was reworded rather than the guard weakened (P248).
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) when a verification's verdict is a line of output rather than an exit code, it is not a verification — the reader has to notice, and a tired reader will not; (b) a check must state WHICH SUBJECT it measured, and any degraded path must announce when the subject changes; (c) the two halves of this entry were both written as "lessons" the day before and neither would have survived contact with the next occurrence — a lesson recorded in prose is the thing this file exists to convert into a mechanism (P280).

### P327. [BUILT 2026-08-19, operator: "do the root cause fix"] Measured constants get ONE enforced contract — the mechanism for the class that produced P315, P316 and P326
P326 fixed an instance: it built a producer for the seat-alpha table. The root cause is that **a constant derived from a measurement had no enforced way to say who produced it, from what data, when, and how to re-derive it** — so each author invented a convention, and three separate incidents followed. Module `core/calibration_registry.py`, instrument `scripts/calibration_check.py`, tests `tests/test_p327_calibration_registry.py` (37); seven falsification probes red-then-restored.
- **THE CLASS, stated as the three incidents rather than as a principle.** **P326** — the seat-alpha table could not be re-derived: its whole derivation was one docstring sentence, implementing exactly that sentence gave BTC 240.3/58.4/44.0 against the shipped 2.3/68.5/24.1, and the convention turned out to have three clauses. **P315** — CDE fees were modelled as a percentage for the life of the system while the venue charges per contract, and nothing recorded when the model had last been checked against a fill. **P316** — the cascade threshold was calibrated from an 11-day noisy proxy while six months of the direct series sat on disk, because nothing recorded what data it came from.
- **The tree had three conventions for the same job, and the worst-covered constant was the one that prices every trade:**
  | constant | provenance before | drift guard | staleness |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `seat_alpha.REGIMEBOOK_ALPHA_BY_ERA` | `_MEASURED_ON` + `_MEASURED_BY` | P326 `--verify` | none |
  | `cde_fees.CDE_FEE_PER_CONTRACT_USD` | a per-call `provenance` string + a prose rule | none | none |
  | `FrictionComponents.CDE_SPREAD_BPS` | **none** — an inline literal inside `__post_init__` | P290 test | none |
- **The contract has five fields and each one exists because a specific incident happened without it:** `producer` is the COMMAND that re-derives it (P326's gap was precisely that a module NAME is not a derivation, so a test asserts it starts with `python` and names a file that exists); `source` is the data it came from, so "is there a better source?" is answerable without archaeology (P316); `measured_on` makes age visible (P315); `staleness_days` is a DECISION with `staleness_reason` recorded beside it, so the horizon cannot be quietly raised the first time the check goes red; `revision_rule` states which direction may move on what evidence, so a refresh cannot smuggle in a loosening (P167).
- **BOTH DIRECTIONS, which is what makes it a contract rather than a list (P310).** Every registry entry must `resolve()` to a live symbol — an entry describing a deleted constant is worse than no entry, because it reads as coverage (P174). **And every module carrying the stamps must be registered**, detected by parsing module-level assignments (not a substring, so a mention in a comment cannot satisfy it). Without that half the next author stamps a module, believes they have declared provenance, and the check never looks at it. The registry NEVER restates a value — `resolve()` imports the live one, pinned by a test that no measured literal appears in its code.
- **The probe that stayed GREEN is the one worth recording.** "A stamped module is unregistered" passed — because my probe added a *comment* to `cde_fees.py`, which unregisters nothing. The real condition (remove its registry entry while the stamp remains) fires with the right message. **A falsification probe that stays green indicts the PROBE first (P238), and here it did.**
- **Coverage was extended, not just the mechanism built** — a registry over one entry would be the P170 shape. `cde_fees` gained the stamps; `CDE_SPREAD_BPS` was hoisted out of `__post_init__` into a stamped module-level constant, which also gives P290's drift guard a real single source instead of a restated table. **The hoist COPIES rather than aliases**, because `set_spread_venue` mutates the instance per venue at runtime and an alias would let a live tick write back into the measurement itself — pinned by a test, and probed.
- **The instrument runs, rather than being remembered (P230).** `scripts/calibration_check.py` prints age/horizon/producer/source per entry: **0** fresh, **3** stale (with the producer command and the revision rule printed, since several may only move one way), **2** REFUSED when an entry does not resolve or the registry is empty — because "I could not read it" must never read as "it is fine" (P159/P199/P213). It never edits a constant, pinned. Added to the Monday evidence cron (06:45) and to the image via BOTH halves of the allowlist — the Dockerfile COPY and the `.dockerignore` negation — since naming a file in the build recipe is not putting it in the build context (P192); dropping either half fails the existing gate, verified.
- **Current state: 3 registered, all resolve, all within horizon** (seat alpha 1d/180d, fees 1d/90d, spread 4d/90d). The horizons are decisions with reasons: 180d for the seat table because it rests on six years but its VALIDATION era is open-ended, so new data lands entirely in the era the gate leans on; 90d for fees and spreads because a venue can change a schedule quietly and both are measured from instruments that only accumulate when we trade.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) when the same defect appears three times in three different constants, the fix is not a third producer — it is a contract that makes the fourth constant declare itself; (b) a provenance convention that can be used WITHOUT registering is not a contract, so detect the convention's own marker and require registration, or it decays into decoration; (c) staleness must be reported by something that RUNS — a date in a docstring is not a horizon, it is a fact nobody re-reads.

### P326. [RESOLVED 2026-08-19, operator: "can you resolve this"] The failed control reconciled by enumerating conventions — and the answer it unlocked REFUTES the change I was about to recommend
P325 left one item open: my per-round-trip measurement of BTC's book did not reproduce the shipped calibration, so the trend-only comparison beside it was untrustworthy. Resolved by finding the convention rather than re-running the comparison. New producer `training/seat_alpha_calibration.py`; tests `tests/test_p326_seat_alpha_producer.py` (15); six falsification probes red-then-restored, one per clause.
- **THE METHOD, because guessing would have taken longer than enumerating.** The shipped table's derivation was one sentence — *"funding_legs_lab, 6y, honest per-contract fees, gross bps per round trip = 2 x gross per unit turnover"* — and my implementation of exactly that sentence gave BTC **240.3 / 58.4 / 44.0** against the shipped **2.3 / 68.5 / 24.1**. So I enumerated candidate conventions (gross / net / gross+carry / gross−cost) x two era slicings and printed all of them beside the shipped values, using **ETH and SOL as a clean probe**: the P315 reports record `active_bars: 0` for both, i.e. their funding legs never fire, so book ≡ trend-only there and any correct convention must reproduce their numbers exactly.
- **THE CONVENTION HAS THREE CLAUSES AND THE SENTENCE STATED ONE.**
  1. **Gross only** — as documented, and the only clause that was right.
  2. **Eras index the POSITIONS frame directly** (`ERAS` applied with `.iloc`, no adjustment for the `MIN_BARS` warmup `build_positions` drops). Correcting for that offset — the obvious "fix", and my bug — moves BTC pre_design **2.3 → 243.2**. A hundredfold, on the number that decides whether BTC trades.
  3. **A position already standing when a window OPENS is not turnover.** This only bites where a window opens mid-position, which is why it looked like noise: ETH's book is **+1 at the pre_design open and flat at the other two**, and pre_design was the only ETH cell that disagreed (245.9 vs 251.7). The boundary diagnostic confirmed it before the fix, rather than after.
- **Verified end to end: every cell, both assets, exactly** — ETH 251.7 / 88.1 / 52.1 and BTC 2.3 / 68.5 / 24.1, medians 88.1 and 24.1 as shipped.
- **THE ANSWER, and it inverts the recommendation I was drafting.** Under the reconciled convention, BTC:
  | series | pre_design | design | validation | **era-median** |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | full book (live) | +2.3 | +68.5 | +24.1 | **+24.1** |
  | trend-only | **−3.6** | +137.3 | **−10.4** | **−3.6** |
  My broken numbers said trend-only was "decisive" at +291.2 / +102.7 / +70.8. **It is negative in two of three eras and its median is below zero — worse than the book it would replace.** Dropping BTC's funding legs would not let BTC trade; it would make its per-trade edge negative. **P325's gap 7 is CLOSED as not-a-lever**, and had I trusted the uncontrolled numbers I would have recommended a change that makes BTC worse.
- **And it corrects the framing, not just the number.** The legs remain expensive and uncertified (gross 113.5%, cost 89.7%, Sharpe 0.153, CI spanning zero) — **and they are also what makes BTC's edge era-STABLE**: the book is positive in all three eras while trend-only earns everything in one and loses in the other two, which is the era-fragility P243/P244 treat as disqualifying. Both things are true, and only the first was visible before.
- **THE DURABLE FIX IS THE PRODUCER, and this is a P310 instance nobody had named.** The constants that stop BTC trading were **hand-copied literals with no runnable producer** — exactly the class P310/P312 closed for record shapes, here for a NUMBER. `training/seat_alpha_calibration.py` recomputes the table, `--verify` exits **3 on drift** and **2 on missing data** (operator-local, P213: "no data" must never read as "the calibration changed"), and `--series trend` prices an alternative position series through the identical convention, so the question "would dropping the legs help?" is answerable rather than arguable. It IMPORTS the shipped table and the lab's machinery rather than restating either (P172), pinned both ways.
- **Fail directions pinned:** no turnover returns **None**, never 0.0 — "the position never moved" is not an edge of zero, and a fabricated zero would drag any median toward not-trading for a reason nobody measured (P2); a negative edge passes through **unclamped**, because "this series loses money per round trip" is the finding; `--verify` REFUSES to compare a non-book series against the book's table, since that would report drift that is really a different question.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) when a control fails, enumerate conventions against a cell whose answer you already know — ETH's zero-active-bars book was a free oracle, and it identified all three clauses in one run where guessing had already cost two; (b) a measurement convention with N clauses documented as one sentence is not reproducible, and the gap only shows up when someone re-derives it — ship the producer with the constants, or the next session re-derives them wrongly and acts on it; (c) the value of the control is not that it catches arithmetic slips, it is that it catches slips whose output looks DECISIVE — the wrong numbers here were larger, cleaner and pointed at an action.

### P325. [AUDITED + FIXED 2026-08-19, operator: "end to end audit to find the gap between our current system and the system can actually trade and make money"] Rule #3 has been BLIND since the deposit — and the gap is not signal, it is exposure, drawdown tolerance and one accounting artifact
Audited from the money outwards rather than from the pitfall log. Fix + tests `tests/test_p325_fuse_excludes_flows.py` (17); six falsification probes red-then-restored; full suite green; gate clean, zero baseline movement.
- **THE LIVE DEFECT, found in one log line.** Two accumulators over the same account disagreed by 189 percentage points on the same tick:
  ```
  [P209][FUSE-FEED] sleeve equity=$10,819.46 delta=+0.00 window=+186.88%
  [COINBASE-PNL]    equity=$10,819.46 pnl=$-252.56 (-2.28%)
  ```
  The sleeve ledger is right: **P293h subtracts detected transfers there, and the fuse feed — written the same day, four thousand lines away — computed a bare `equity - anchor`.** So the 2026-08-16 deposit of ~$7,074 entered Rule #3's 28-day window as PROFIT (7074 / 3785 pre-deposit equity = +186.9%).
- **IT DID NOT MIS-STATE THE FUSE, IT DISABLED IT.** The fuse suspends on a NEGATIVE window (−15%). A window at +186.88% cannot go negative on any loss this sleeve can produce, so **Non-Negotiable Rule #3 has been inert since 2026-08-16 and stays inert until that point rolls out of the window (~2026-09-13)** — and any further deposit re-arms the blindness. The P293h/P294 note said the ledger "keeps reporting the inflated figure until an operator sets `external_flow_usd`"; what it missed is that a second consumer of the same quantity was a SAFETY CONTROL. Fixed at the arithmetic (a pure `fuse_flow_adjusted_delta`), reusing the sleeve's own cumulative detector (P172) rather than a second implementation. **Severity bounded honestly and pinned by test: the sleeve's 15% and the LIVE 25% drawdown halts are unaffected** — both read equity against a peak, and a deposit raises both sides, leaving the ratio unchanged. One of three overlapping loss controls was blind, not all three.
- **The load-bearing test is the one that keeps a loss visible:** a real −$60 tick landing on the deposit tick must still record −$60. An adjustment that swallowed the whole delta would forgive losses, which is the direction P287 spent an entry closing. And the reference PAIR moves together — advancing or restoring the equity anchor without the flow reference would re-subtract the same transfer on every later tick (P287's half-a-reference lesson, on the flow half); an absent persisted value restores as **None**, not 0.0, because a numeric fallback on a state file predating the field would subtract the ENTIRE cumulative flow as one tick's transfer (P261b: a migration case is a first-boot case with pre-existing state).
- **A NameError I caught before deploying, not after:** the first draft read `_cb_sleeve`, which appears elsewhere in main.py; the name in scope at the feed is `_fz_sleeve`. `except (TypeError, ValueError)` does **not** catch NameError, so it would have escaped into the block's outer handler and could have skipped the fuse record entirely — the P193/P234 class, in a fix for a safety control. Pinned by a test that fails if the wrong name returns.

- **[P325b — the poisoned window CORRECTED on the live volume, 2026-08-20.]** P325 fixed the arithmetic and left the historical point in place, self-healing ~2026-09-13. With Rule #3 inert for 25 more days that was worth closing rather than waiting out, and the correction is precedented: P261 reset a kill switch whose activation was arithmetic fiction, and this is the same fiction with the opposite sign.
  - **The surgery was decided by reading the consumer, not by guessing.** `_calculate_window_metrics` computes `window_pnl = sum(r.realized_pnl ...)` — `cumulative_pnl` feeds NO decision inside the fuse (carried forward and serialised only; the `cumulative_pnl` in api/server and the dashboard is a different, top-level field). So zeroing one record was sufficient, and exactly ONE qualified: idx 89, `realized_pnl +7074.28`, stamped 2026-08-16T03:55:28.
  - Engine stopped, file backed up (`paper_positions.json.bak-p325-*`), the record zeroed with the removed amount kept beside it as `external_flow_excluded_usd` (an audit trail the loader ignores), and `cumulative_pnl` rebased from that index so the stored series stays self-consistent. **Measured before and after: window sum(realized_pnl) +7041.19 → −33.09.**
  - **A step that would have broken it silently:** the edit ran as root on the host volume, leaving the file `root:root` while the container runs as `hmats` — the next `_save_paper_positions` would have failed, with the P287 ERROR as the only sign. Ownership restored before the restart.
  - **Verified on the first post-restart tick, the only evidence that counts (P295b):** `[P209][FUSE-FEED] ... window=-0.88% state=ACTIVE`, matching the predicted −33.09/3771.41 to the decimal. Rule #3 can now fire on a genuine −15% window instead of sitting 200 points above its own trigger.

**THE AUDIT — where the gap actually is.** Measured, in order of how much money each one moves:

| # | gap | measurement | who decides |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **~86% of wealth is not at risk** | sleeve $10,819 of $23,205 total (12,300 XRP = $12,385, held by explicit instruction). At-risk today ≈ **$3,246 = 14% of wealth** | operator |
| 2 | **Risk halts are tighter than the strategy's own certified drawdown** | SOL trend-only historical maxDD −199 pts at 1.0 exposure → **≈ −26% realized at the live 0.15 fraction**, against a **15%** sleeve halt (ETH ≈ −12%) | operator |
| 3 | **~40% of the sleeve's risk budget is idle** | fractions 0.15/0.15/0.15; BTC cannot clear its own gate, so its 0.15 is unusable. Caps allow ETH 0.25 / SOL 0.20 (net 0.45 < the 0.50 cap) | operator |
| 4 | **Two entry vetoes armed without evidence, on a book with 6–13 entries/year** | P324: neither clears its bar. A veto that blocks a third of entries removes a third of the return | operator |
| 5 | **Rule #3 blind** | +186.88% window vs −2.28% actual | **FIXED here** |
| 6 | **A dead feedback loop haircuts asserted alpha 25%** | live: `est 24.1->18.1bps (perf_factor=0.75, hit_rate=0.50)`; all three `update_hit_rate` writers sit past the P152 early return on the dead Kraken path, so `_rolling_hit_rate` is frozen at its `0.5` initialiser forever | recorded |
| 7 | **BTC's funding legs are the most expensive and least certified component** | gross 113.5%, cost **89.7%** (79% of gross), net 24.0% over 5.79y, Sharpe **0.153**, CI [−0.57,+0.84]; removing them RAISES the book's Sharpe (0.586 vs 0.558) | needs a measurement |

- **The system trades nothing right now and only ONE asset is structurally blocked.** BTC is refused correctly on its own calibration (24.1bps/RT asserted vs a ~42bps threshold). ETH and SOL are flat because their books say flat (`WEAK_CONSOLIDATION`, not bull) — when directional they clear comfortably (88.1 and 221.7 against ~47 and ~52). **Long flat stretches are the design, not a fault:** derived from cost ÷ per-round-trip friction on the committed reports, ETH runs **~6 round trips/year** and SOL **~13** — 30–70× less turnover than the trend seat's ~442/year, which is why fees stopped being the binding constraint.
- **The honest expected value, stated so it cannot be oversold.** At honest fees over ~5.8y the books net BTC 144.5 / ETH 191.2 / SOL 502.9 against buy-and-hold 186.3 / 253.8 / 703.3 — **every book loses to holding on RETURN and wins on Sharpe and drawdown.** Scaling the two assets that can trade to their live 0.15 fraction gives ≈ **5%/yr (ETH) + 13%/yr (SOL) ≈ 18%/yr on the sleeve ≈ $1,950**, or ~8%/yr of total wealth. Every figure is an additive per-bar sum on a window this repo has read many times (P260), and SOL's share is dominated by SOL's own appreciation.
- **A measurement of mine that FAILED ITS CONTROL, reported rather than used.** To price gap 7 I measured per-era gross bps per round trip for the full book vs trend-only, reusing the lab's own builders. The trend-only column looks decisive (BTC +291.2 / +102.7 / +70.8 against the book's +240.3 / +58.4 / +44.0) — **but the book column does not reproduce the shipped calibration (2.3 / 68.5 / 24.1)**, so by the rule written into that script's own docstring the trend-only column is not trustworthy and no recommendation rests on it. What DOES stand is artifact-backed and independent of it: the legs add ~4%/yr of return for 79% of their gross in fees, at a Sharpe of 0.153 whose CI spans zero, and they LOWER the book's Sharpe. **[RESOLVED by P326: the convention had three clauses and the note stated one; BTC trend-only measures -3.6 / +137.3 / -10.4, an era-median BELOW zero, so gap 7 is not a lever.]**
- **A refinement of my own P324 disposition, in the direction that matters.** I left both entry vetoes armed partly because "the measured entries lost money in every bucket" — but that window is the TREND seat's ~442 trades/year. The book takes **6–13 entries/year**, so a false veto is far more expensive per entry than that reasoning implied, and the case for disarming is stronger than I concluded. It is still an operator decision; what changed is the price of being wrong.
- **Also verified and NOT a gap, so nobody re-derives it:** the fixed `coinbase_max_contracts_by_asset {1,3,1}` in the live profile looked like it would cap ETH at 3 contracts against an equity-scaled target of 8 — P274 anticipated exactly that and makes `_sized_contracts` the cap as well as the target, so the fixed values bind only when the price is unreadable (ETH sizes to **8**, SOL **4**, BTC 2). Checking the code rather than the config is what separated a false alarm from gap 3.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) audit from the money outward — the top three gaps are exposure, drawdown tolerance and idle capital, none of which is a signal problem, and none of which any amount of model work would have found; (b) when two accumulators measure the same quantity, print them side by side once — 189 percentage points of disagreement was visible in a single tick's log and had been sitting there for three days; (c) a fix applied to one consumer of a quantity must be checked against every other consumer, and the one to check FIRST is whichever one is a safety control.

### P324. [MEASURED + BUILT 2026-08-19, operator: "any remaining task"] Two live entry vetoes are armed and NEITHER has a measurement that clears its bar — including the one piece of evidence that armed the other
Audited the "remaining" items instead of restating them (the P316 rule: "we have to wait" deserves the same audit as any other claim). A fresh WARNING+ sweep on the new build was clean — every line matches the P303/P308 triage — so the audit moved to the item P315 recorded and did not fix. New instrument `analytics/ic/agent_disagreement_review.py`; tests `tests/test_p324_disagreement_instrument.py` (17); three falsification probes red-then-restored.
- **THE INCONSISTENCY, and it is mine.** P298 **subordinated** whale to the regimebook as a DIRECTION source — explicitly because the book is certified over six years and whale's own IC is 16h **t=0.26** — and in the same entry left `coinbase_whale_filter_enforce` armed so whale can **VETO that same book's entries**. One signal, two contradictory precedence rules, decided in one sitting. The filter's own call site says *"this ships enforcement-OFF with a ledger … the ledger is what earns the flip"*, and it was armed **one day after that ledger began** (measured: the ledger opens 2026-08-18 06:55, 120 rows ≈ 1.03 days, 2 BTC actions).
- **So the veto was measured rather than argued about — and the measurement is possible NOW, not in 123 days.** P236 answered exactly this question for `model_alpha` by counterfactual on the attribution logs; that analysis was hand-rolled and nobody could re-run it (the P230 shape). Generalised into a standing instrument over 130 days of attribution logs, with **the verdict rule pre-committed in the docstring before the first run**: a veto is EARNED iff, pooled, the DISAGREE bucket's mean signed forward return is negative, |t| ≥ 2 **overlap-corrected** (n_eff = n/h, P231), and AGREE > DISAGREE.
- **RESULT — both armed filters: NOT EARNED** (90d, decider = `quant`, i.e. whatever holds the seat; signed forward return of the DECIDER's own direction, bps):
  | advisor | agree h1 / h4 | disagree h1 / h4 | disagree t h1 / h4 | contrast Δ h1 / h4 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | whale | −0.9 / +1.8 | **−9.1 / −10.1** | −1.79 / −0.49 | +8.2 (t 1.02) / +11.9 (t 0.42) |
  | model_alpha | −0.5 / +3.6 | **−7.8 / −31.2** | −1.41 / −1.38 | +7.3 (t 1.00) / +34.8 (t 1.24) |
- **THE CONTROL IS THE FINDING: `model_alpha` fails too, and it is the ONLY evidence any sleeve filter was ever armed on.** P236 reported **−78.9bps, t=−3.42**; on 90 days the same measurement gives −31.2bps at **t=−1.38**. P236's t was **not overlap-corrected** — the identical artifact P231 caught in `agent_ic_review` and P230 caught in model_alpha's own IC (4.4 → ~2.2). Running the instrument against a known-earned case is what a new instrument owes (P174), and here the known-earned case turned out not to be earned.
- **P297's rule applied before acting on the FAIL: check what else the criterion rejects.** My rule tests the disagree bucket's LEVEL against zero, which is not the contrast a filter exploits — so a two-sample Welch t on (agree − disagree), on overlap-corrected sizes, was added and **reported, never swapped into the verdict** (moving the test after seeing the number is the selection sin; a test pins that the verdict block cannot read it). It **agrees**: t = 1.02/0.42 and 1.00/1.24. The criterion was not the wrong lens.
- **THE DECISIVE NUMBER, and it is the reason no flag was flipped: in this window EVERY bucket loses money.** At h4 the AGREE buckets return **+1.8** and **+3.6** bps gross against **~28bps** of honest round-trip friction (P315). The window measures the **trend seat**, whose economics P315/P318 established as the problem — 257–261 of the ~264 disagreements fall in the trend-seat era, against **1–5** in the whale-seat era and **0–6** in the book-seat era. **A filter's value can only be measured against a decider that makes money; here the base case loses, so "the filter helps" and "the filter is irrelevant" are indistinguishable.**
- **DISPOSITION: both flags LEFT AS THEY ARE, and that is a decision with a reason, not inertia.** Disarming is a LOOSENING — it adds entries to a book whose measured entries lost money after friction in every bucket — and flipping a live flag on non-discriminating evidence is what P141/P270 exist to prevent. What is now on record is the uncomfortable true state: **armed without evidence, and the available evidence cannot settle it.** The one thing that changes is that the question is no longer someone's memory.
- **P315's reading is refined rather than overturned:** "two days of evidence cannot have earned it" stands and is now quantified; what is new is that 90 days cannot earn it either, and cannot condemn it. The per-asset signs are the only thing pointing anywhere — whale disagree negative on 3/3 assets at h1, model_alpha on 3/3 at h4 — which is a property a coin flip fakes one time in eight per advisor, i.e. a hypothesis, not a result (P320c).
- **Wired as a MECHANISM, not a note (P235/P280):** both advisors run in the Monday 06:15/06:16 evidence cron, so the book-seat window is read as it accumulates rather than when someone remembers. Re-arming or disarming needs this instrument to return EARNED (exit 3) or the pooled `whale_filtered` ledger to pass P166 — `whale_filtered` IS in `POOLABLE_FAMILIES`, so its clock is ~123 days, not the ~330 an unpooled 16h exam would need (P293g).
- **Fail directions pinned:** a silent advisor buckets as `advisor_silent`, never as disagreement (P2 — otherwise every dark agent would read as a veto with perfect coverage, contradicting the filter's own fail-OPEN semantics); a flat decider is excluded entirely (there is no entry for a veto to act on); fewer than 30 disagreements REFUSES rather than reporting "not earned"; an advisor absent from the attribution stream exits 2 naming it a wiring gap, because "not recorded" must never read as "no effect" (P199).
- **Also confirmed while there, so nobody re-derives it:** nothing is training and **nothing is on a refit cadence** — 12 DRL folds and 27 supervised folds carry a promotion verdict and **0 passed** (supervised blockers: CI includes zero 27/27, lost to buy-and-hold 17, to SMA-200 16); `configs/regimebook/` does not exist (P250 deleted the leak artifact) and the only exported-weights artifact belongs to the withdrawn `mlpshadow` (P285c); last training compute 2026-08-16. The live decider is a RULE BOOK, and the `thresh=inf` lines two assets print every tick are the documented NO_TRADE/quiet-regime sentinel, not a defect.
- **The verdict rule and the bucketing are PURE FUNCTIONS, because the first draft pinned them as source text.** `decide_verdict` and `classify_bucket` are tested by being CALLED — the P234/P307b rule, and the parallel session's frozen-roster guard caught my draft doing exactly what it forbids. Five falsification probes red-then-restored, including one that makes `main()` stop calling `decide_verdict`: a seam nothing calls is decoration (P170/P312), so the behavioural tests would otherwise be testing code the tool does not run.
- **A false positive I fixed on my side rather than by relaxing their guard (P248, and P317 hit the identical one):** the condition-pin scanner flagged `assert "wiring gap, not a verdict" in blk` because its regex looks for `not` inside an asserted string. It is prose, not a condition — but the guard errs toward catching too much, which is the right direction for a guard against `if False and <cond>`, so the message was reworded. Note the scanner names the 10th pin in sort order as "the new one", which pointed at an innocent file; find yours by running its own regex over your file.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) when a control is armed on one measurement, re-run that measurement with the corrections learned since — P236's t predates the overlap correction this repo now applies everywhere else, and the control it armed is still live; (b) a filter can only be judged against a decider with positive post-friction expectancy, so check the AGREE bucket against friction before reading anything into the DISAGREE bucket; (c) two contradictory precedence rules for one signal (subordinate as a driver, authoritative as a veto) is a decision nobody made — it is what happens when direction and veto are decided in separate paragraphs.

### P323. [FIXED 2026-08-19, operator: "inspect why the coinbase sleeve pending"] `positions_fresh` was PERMANENTLY false — a double timezone marker, and the identical bug had already been fixed once for the sibling file
The sleeve itself is healthy; what reads as "pending" is `/health` reporting `"positions_fresh": false` on every call. Tests `tests/test_p323_positions_freshness.py` (8); two falsification probes red-then-restored; full suite 5,410 passing; gate clean.
- **THE MEASUREMENT that separates the symptom from the cause.** The live sleeve is fine — `[COINBASE-SLEEVE] buying_power=$10,803 positions=FLAT`, `[COINBASE-MANAGE] tick summary: BTC=NOOP, ETH=NOOP, SOL=NOOP`, and **`manage pending` appears ZERO times** in the log (that P263 heartbeat text is a first-tick-after-restart artifact and was not what fired). `/health` reports `"positions_fresh": false` while `paper_positions.json` was written **seconds earlier** — mtime 06:36 and its own `saved_at` 06:36:58.
- **CAUSE: `"saved_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat() + "Z"`.** `isoformat()` on an AWARE datetime already emits `+00:00`, so the stored value is `...+00:00Z` — a double timezone marker. `api/server._is_fresh` then does `ts.replace("Z", "+00:00")`, producing `...+00:00+00:00`, which `datetime.fromisoformat` rejects; the exception is swallowed and the function returns False. **So the flag could never be True at any age** — it was reporting the parser, not the file (the P159/P174 shape: a check that cannot pass is indistinguishable from one that failed).
- **IT IS MORE THAN A COSMETIC FLAG.** `/health` is `healthy if (engine_state_fresh or positions_fresh)`, and that OR exists so a dashboard-export failure cannot by itself mark the container unhealthy — **precisely the P160 scenario, where the dashboard writer can fail silently**. With `positions_fresh` stuck False the redundancy was gone and `/health` depended on `dashboard_state.json` alone. The container has been one silent dashboard failure away from a 503 that positions-freshness was designed to prevent.
- **AND IT WAS ALREADY FIXED ONCE, FOR THE SIBLING.** `main.py:~20068` carries a **2026-04-22** comment describing this identical failure for `dashboard_state.json` — *"was `.isoformat() + \"Z\"` which produced malformed \"...+00:00Z\" (double tz marker). API /health datetime.fromisoformat then failed silently -> fresh=False -> container marked unhealthy"* — and fixes it with `.replace("+00:00", "Z")`. The `paper_positions.json` writer four thousand lines away was left alone. **A mitigation applied to one instance of a class is not applied to the class** (P171 for the BOM, P226 for the scanners, here for a timestamp) — and the fix's own comment even named the pattern to match.
- **Fixed at BOTH ends, deliberately.** The writer now emits `.replace("+00:00", "Z")` like its sibling; and `_is_fresh` tolerates the malformed form, because **every file already on the volume carries the old shape until it is next rewritten** — fixing only the writer would leave the flag false until each file happened to be re-saved. The reader change strips a trailing `Z` only when an explicit offset is already present, so it cannot turn a genuinely stale or garbage stamp into "fresh" — pinned by tests that a 30-day-old stamp and a non-date both still read False (the fix must not trade a false "stale" for a false "fresh", which is the worse direction).
- **A cross-file contract test, since the two halves live apart and had drifted:** whatever the writer produces must satisfy the reader, asserted by round-tripping the writer's exact expression through the real `_is_fresh`. Plus a pin that the 2026-04-22 sibling fix stays fixed, so the pair cannot diverge again.
- **`_is_fresh` is imported without importing `api.server`** (fastapi is absent in CI) by compiling the single function out of the AST — a guard that skips on the machine where it matters is not a guard (P194).
- **Recorded, not fixed here:** `isoformat() + "Z"` appears at **7 further sites** (`agents/microstructure_agent.py` x2, `model_alpha_agent`, `onchain_graph_alpha`, `onchain_sentiment_fusion`, `risk_agent`, `scripts/reconcile_flatten_2026_06_12.py`). Those write `asof_ts` into signal envelopes rather than a freshness file, so none is known to reach a `fromisoformat` consumer — but the class is live in the tree and the next consumer that parses one will hit exactly this.
- **[P323b — the CLASS closed, and one of the seven was NOT latent.]** P323 fixed one writer and recorded seven more as "no known `fromisoformat` consumer". Checking that claim rather than trusting it: `asof_ts` genuinely has no production parser (only tests asserting `endswith("Z")`) — **but `scripts/reconcile_flatten_2026_06_12.py:126` writes `state["saved_at"]` into `paper_positions.json`, the exact field and file `_is_fresh` parses.** So running that operator script would have re-broken `positions_fresh` on the spot. **My "none is known to reach a consumer" was wrong for one of the seven**, and grepping for the FIELD rather than for the pattern would have shown it in one command.
  - **One of the seven was worse than malformed:** `agents/onchain_sentiment_fusion.py` used `datetime.now()` — **NAIVE** — plus `"Z"`, which LABELS local time as UTC (P40/P97). That is a wrong VALUE, not just an unparseable one, and it only looks right because the server runs UTC.
  - All seven now route through a small `_iso_utc()` that NORMALISES tzinfo before formatting, so a naive input can never be relabelled, and emits exactly one `Z` — preserving the `endswith("Z")` envelope contract every existing agent test asserts (131 of them still pass).
  - **A mechanism rather than a fourth instance (P280):** a guard scans every production directory for `isoformat() + "Z"` and fails. It reads comment- AND docstring-stripped source, because every remaining mention in this repo is an explanation OF the fix, and a scanner that matches its own explanation is worthless (P177, fourth sighting). An anti-vacuity test builds a synthetic offender and asserts the scanner still sees it (P174) — without it, stripping too much would make the guard pass forever.
  - **Process note, my own:** the CLAUDE.md splice for this entry was run as `python -c "..."` with backticks in the string, so **bash command-substituted the entry text before python ever saw it** — the commit landed with code and tests and NO CLAUDE.md entry (a Rule 7 miss caught by re-reading the file, not by any check). Quoted heredocs are safe; double-quoted `-c` strings are not.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) when a fix's own comment says "match the pattern used elsewhere", grep for every OTHER writer of the same field shape in the same commit — that comment was written in April and the sibling stayed broken for four months; (b) a boolean that is always False is reporting its parser, not its subject, so any freshness/health flag deserves the round-trip test (write it, read it back) rather than a unit test of either half; (c) fix a malformed-data bug at BOTH ends when persisted files carry the old shape, or the correction does not take effect until every file happens to be rewritten.

### P322. [PLANNED + BUILT 2026-08-19, operator: "make a plan and act upon it"] Making the CryptoPanic exit safe — and the audit found a FABRICATION HAZARD sitting on the exact path the exit would take
The plan was four steps: stop mock headlines reaching the model; build a clean disable; pin the independence; record the arithmetic and name the one step only the operator can take. Step 1 turned out not to be hygiene. Tests `tests/test_p322_cryptopanic_exit_is_safe.py` (11); four falsification probes red-then-restored.
- **THE HAZARD, and it is on the obvious exit path.** `mock_mode` is derived as `not bool(CRYPTOPANIC_API_KEY)`, and `_fetch_mock` invents **3-10 items per currency titled `"Mock BTC News 0"`, stamped within the last 240 minutes** — fresh enough to clear the 4h window and satisfy the `_c3_live` starvation gate. `is_mock` was recorded in the meta dict and consulted **only** by the metrics path (`if _cp_metrics and not _cp_is_mock`); the headline loop never looked at it. **So the intuitive way to cancel the plan — delete the API key — would have sent fabricated titles to Haiku and returned the reading stamped `status=live`.** P2/P223: a fabricated value is indistinguishable from a measured one, and this one arrives pre-stamped fresh. Latent today only because a key is configured; the next operator action under consideration flips it on. Fixed at the boundary: **mock means NO news, never FAKE news** — `is_mock` is still reported (the metrics gate reads it), but the headline list is empty with a named reason.
- **THE CLEAN DISABLE.** `cryptopanic_enabled` (P201 trio: declared, parsed, consumed; **default TRUE, absent from the live profile — so this commit changes nothing by itself**). When false the feed is left **UNBUILT**: no requests, no mock, no warnings. That is the point — the env-var route does not disable the feed, it converts it into a fabricator, and a test asserts the disable branch sets the feed to `None` rather than constructing a mock one. The reason deleting the key is the wrong lever is recorded AT the construction site, because that is where the next reader will reach for it.
- **THE COST PICTURE, from the operator's own plan table plus measured demand.** Growth monthly **$199/mo for 3,000 requests**; Growth weekly **$50/wk (~$217/mo) for 600/week**. Measured demand is **3 requests x 6 four-hour ticks = ~18/day = ~540/month = ~18% utilisation**, i.e. **~$0.37 per request actually issued**. Weekly is worse per request, and $50 x 8 weeks is close to the ~$400 bill P154 recorded, so which tier is active is itself worth checking. **Unresolved and genuinely operator-side: 540/month cannot exhaust 3,000, yet the API answers 429 — so either the plan is smaller than Growth-monthly or something outside this engine spends the key. Only the dashboard usage counter distinguishes those, and no amount of probing will.**
- **WHY LEAVING IS CHEAP — measured, not argued.** With the quota exhausted and CryptoPanic contributing **zero** fetches, `llm_sentiment` reads `src=haiku status=live tradeable=True` on all three assets from **20/8/6 RSS headlines** (P314). The experiment is already running. Its unique fields feed `_score_cryptopanic_metrics`, which sits **below** the Haiku return — reachable only when Haiku produces nothing — and its `panic_score` never reaches `market_data` at all (that comes from `macro_crowd_context`, P287). All three facts are now pinned, so a regression cannot quietly make leaving expensive later; the `panic_score` pin scans every CryptoPanic line in main.py and fails if one ever writes that key.
- **THE HONEST CAVEAT, unchanged from the recommendation:** leaving is cheap **because this agent is worth little**, not because RSS is proven better. `llm_sentiment` 90d IC is +0.040 (4h) / +0.048 (16h), both insignificant, and it reaches an order only through P298 de-risking-only conviction channel. What is being given up is a fallback and a 99.95% SLA on a signal that has not earned its keep either way.
- **THE OPERATOR STEP, deliberately not automated (P141).** Cancel or downgrade in the CryptoPanic dashboard, then set `"cryptopanic_enabled": false` in `configs/live_high_risk.json` and redeploy. **Do NOT delete `CRYPTOPANIC_API_KEY`** — that is the mock path, and while P322 now makes it harmless it is still the wrong lever. Revert is one config line. And per P293 own recorded position, the freed ~$199/mo should **not** be immediately spent on more data: buying a feed for a signal that has not cleared P166 is the P269 mistake.
- **Mitigation pattern:** before recommending an operator action, walk the path that action creates. The recommendation here was "cancel", the obvious implementation was "delete the key", and that implementation silently enabled a fabricator — a defect that existed for the life of the feed and would only ever have surfaced by someone acting on the advice.
- **[P322b — ARMED 2026-08-19 by explicit operator instruction: "Set cryptopanic_enabled false and redeploy"]** Surgical +2-line insert into the live profile (flag + provenance note); the P322 test pin flipped from must-be-ABSENT to the DECIDED value (P237), so a silent revert to `true` AND a silent deletion of the key both fail — either is a live behaviour change that should be argued for rather than drifted into.
- **[P322c — the disable did not disable, caught by reading the live log after deploy (P295b)]** The flag cleared main.py's handle, and the feed came up anyway: `agents/sentiment_llm_agent.py` calls `get_cryptopanic_feed()` **directly at two sites**, bypassing the runner entirely. A handle is not a switch when the singleton has other callers. Fixed with a module-level `set_feed_enabled`/`is_feed_enabled` in the feed itself, checked inside `fetch()` **before** the mock branch, so every caller present and future is covered by construction. An unreadable switch defaults to **ON** (P2: a broken import must not silently disable a feed the operator is paying for).
- **[P322d — the fix I shipped took a live signal DARK, and the same live-log read caught it]** With CryptoPanic disabled, SOL went `src=f&g status=fallback_global headlines=0 **tradeable=False**` — it had been `src=haiku tradeable=True`. **`fetch_headlines_with_meta` blends THREE sources (CryptoPanic + CC News + RSS), and every CryptoPanic bail-out was a `return meta`** — my two new guards at offsets 1244/1353 and, more importantly, the **PRE-EXISTING `if data is None: return meta`** — while the RSS blend sits at offset 7818 of the same function. So whenever the FIRST source had nothing, the other two never ran: a curated-feed outage silently took the whole agent dark instead of degrading to the free sources that exist precisely for that case. **Latent for the life of the function** (CryptoPanic always answered *something*), made permanent by the disable. Restructured so CryptoPanic is ONE OPTIONAL SOURCE OF THREE: shared locals (`now`/`win`/`cutoff`/`seen_titles`/`headlines`) hoisted above all vendor work so they cannot stop being reachable, all three bail-outs set `_cp_usable = False` and fall through, `needs_refresh` gated on `_cp_usable` so a declined source never issues a request, and `window_seconds` published unconditionally. `meta["error"] = "no_data"` keeps its historical value so no consumer changes.
  - **My own P322 tests had pinned the BUG.** They asserted `headlines == []` on the mock path — a claim that is only true while the CryptoPanic bail-out *also* kills RSS, so the correct implementation fails them. Rewritten to isolate the contribution under test (RSS stubbed empty) and split into two claims that an early return cannot satisfy together: no *fabricated* title reaches the model, AND a genuine RSS headline still does. Three falsification probes red-then-restored (restore either early return; drop the `_cp_usable` guard on `needs_refresh` → the "a DISABLED feed must never issue a request" assertion fires).
  - **[P322e] The commit went CI-RED on ONE mypy finding, and my attribution of it was wrong twice.** `union-attr: Item "None" of "CryptoPanicData | None" has no attribute "recent_news"` — `_cp_usable` is a plain bool, so it cannot narrow `data` for the type checker even though it is exactly equivalent to `data is not None` here. Fixed at source (`if _cp_usable and data is not None:`), never baselined (P284b/P287g). **Two process errors on the way there, both worth more than the fix:** (a) the gate's mypy scans `CRITICAL_DIRS` = risk/core/defense/analytics/signals/execution, so I reasoned "my file is in `agents/`, therefore it is not type-checked" — **false: mypy FOLLOWS IMPORTS**, and the scan reaches 162 findings inside `agents/`. A directory list is not a file list. (b) My tree-vs-tree attribution reported "IDENTICAL" because the two scans ran in one `&&` chain after a `cd` — **both halves executed in the HEAD~1 tree, so I compared it against itself**. A comparison that cannot show a difference is the P174 shape applied to a diagnostic; re-run in separate subshells it showed the finding immediately. Also: the abbreviated-sha CI query returned zero runs silently (the API needs the full sha), so I read "no runs yet" as "still pending" for ten minutes while the answer was sitting there — and the repo slug in my query was wrong besides.
  - **[P322f] "The feed is left UNBUILT" was still false, and the gap was an OBSERVABILITY one.** The agent read the switch AFTER calling `get_cryptopanic_feed()`, so a disabled feed was muted but still CONSTRUCTED — four `[CRYPTOPANIC] Initialized / restored backoff / cached news dropped` INFO lines at every boot. It issues no requests, so this costs nothing; what it costs is the ability to VERIFY the config. A disabled feed whose boot log is identical to an enabled one is indistinguishable from a flag that did not take effect — which is precisely the reading that sent this session chasing a working disable for a second round. The switch is now read before construction at BOTH call sites, and the invariant is pinned by counting constructions rather than by grepping source. Also fixed here: `test_an_unreadable_switch_defaults_to_ON` was a source-text pin (`"_cp_on = True" in src[i:i+400]`) that broke the moment a SECOND site gained the same guard, because `index()` found the other function — rewritten to make the switch itself raise and assert both callers still use the feed. **Fail direction unchanged and re-pinned: an unreadable switch leaves the feed ON** (P2 — a broken import must not silently stop a paid feed).
    - Three more `union-attr` findings appeared as I went, all the same shape as P322e (`feed`/`data` are `X | None`; a bool cannot carry the narrowing) — caught BEFORE pushing this time by running mypy over the dirs CI actually scans, and each fixed at source by restating the `is not None` the flag already implies.
  - **Mitigation pattern:** an early return in a function that blends N sources is a claim that source 1 is a PRECONDITION for sources 2..N — check whether that is true before writing one, because it is usually false and the failure is silent until source 1 goes away. And when a test asserts an *absence*, make sure the absence is attributable: `headlines == []` could not tell "CryptoPanic declined" from "the whole blend died", which is exactly the distinction that mattered.

### P321. [ARMED 2026-08-19, operator: "our goal is to let the system up and running, and can make profit" -> "sure"] Honest economics ARMED — and restating the goal exposed that I had been scoring the system against a bar the operator never asked for
The two halves of the P315/P320 correction are now live in `configs/live_high_risk.json`. The operator restating the objective changed two of my own recommendations, which is the substance of this entry.
- **THE REFRAME, and it is the important part.** I had been reporting "the book loses to buy-and-hold" as though it settled profitability. **At honest per-contract fees, over six years, all three books are PROFITABLE: BTC +144.5%, ETH +184.1%, SOL +336.2%.** Underperforming buy-and-hold is a BENCHMARK result, not a profitability result, and the goal is profit. P296 recorded this exact trap in the opposite direction ("beats flat is free for a long-biased rule") and P301 gave the rule — *choose the baseline from the mechanism's claim* — and I still applied a benchmark the operator had not set.
- **CORRECTION 1: "drop SOL" was WRONG.** I had tested SOL unbanded and with my own fitted 4/4/24 bands, never with **P256's own selected `min_hold=6`**. Re-priced at honest fees: **336.2% → 545.4%, Sharpe 0.81 → 1.13, maxDD −199.1% → −125.5%, cost 100.4% → 46.2%.** On its own selected parameter SOL beats buy-and-hold on **Sharpe AND drawdown**. Testing a config other than the one an asset was actually selected with is not a test of that asset.
- **With each asset on its own best config at honest fees, the book beats buy-and-hold on Sharpe and drawdown on ALL THREE** — BTC banded 0.59 vs 0.55, ETH unbanded 0.72 vs 0.58, SOL banded 1.13 vs 1.08, every drawdown shallower. It loses on raw return, which matters less than it sounds: the sleeve runs at ~0.45 max net exposure against holding's 1.0, so a higher-Sharpe strategy at lower exposure is the one that can be scaled toward the cap.
- **CORRECTION 2: era-MINIMUM was the wrong statistic for this goal.** It is right for a pure safety control (assume the worst era repeats) and it asserts BTC 2.3 / SOL −20.8, which stops every asset. Switched to the era-**MEDIAN** — BTC **24.1**, ETH **88.1**, SOL **221.7** — because the gate's job is to price EXPECTED edge against expected cost, and the median cannot be carried by one dominant era the way the mean can (BTC 80.3, SOL 354.7 — the P243/P244 era-fragility). **The STATISTIC is pinned by test**, so a silent switch to either extreme fails, and it is recorded as a risk-preference choice rather than a measurement upgrade.
- **ARMED, both together:** `coinbase_per_contract_fees: true` + `seat_alpha_calibrated: true`, interlocked in `core/seat_alpha.resolve_seat_edge` so neither can ever ship alone (P318: the fee alone rejects trades earning 2.5x-27x cost; the alpha alone is a pure loosening). The `_p321_honest_economics_note` in the profile carries the decision, the expected effect and the one-line revert — a bare `true` loses why it was armed.
- **EXPECTED LIVE EFFECT, stated before the deploy:** **BTC STOPS entering** (24.1 against a ~35bps threshold) and any open BTC position flattens on the next managed tick; **ETH (88.1) and SOL (221.7) continue**, now on honest economics with wider pass margins than the fiction they had before. Watch for `[P315-FEE]` and `[P319-ALPHA]`/`[P320-ALPHA]` lines on the first tick — their absence means the interlock did not fire and the flags are not doing what this entry claims (the P295b lesson: verify a deploy by reading the log for the thing you changed).
- **What this does NOT establish, and it is the honest limit.** Every number above is a BACKTEST on a window this repo has read many times (P260's ~7 reads), and P320c already recorded that the regime-conditional recent reads are not statistically distinguishable from zero (ETH t=−0.72, BTC t=−0.03). **The live forward exam for the banded configuration already exists and is 102 of ~180 rows** (`regimebook_adj_*`, P256), reading out early September through the P166 gate. **The band promotion is deliberately NOT done here** — that is the one remaining piece where waiting buys real information rather than delay.
- **Also deliberately not done:** SOL routing is unchanged (all three assets stay routed; `scripts/coinbase_set_assets.sh` is operator-run by design, P141), and there is no config-only way to zero an asset in any case — both sizing paths floor at one contract (`_sized_contracts` returns `max(1, ...)`, `target_for`'s fixed fallback does `int(... or 1)` then `max(1, fixed)`).
- Tests: `tests/test_p320_seat_alpha_calibration.py` re-pinned to the median + the DECIDED flag values (P237/P270, so a silent revert of either half fails), `tests/test_p315_per_contract_fees.py`'s not-armed guard converted the same way. Full suite **5,388 passing**; gate clean, zero baseline movement.
- **[P321b — HOTFIX, caught on the FIRST live tick by reading the log, not by the suite.]** The first tick after arming printed `[P320-ALPHA] BTC: seat alpha +24.1bps/round-trip (era_median) replaces the asserted 30.0` **and**, on all three assets, `[P315-FEE] BTC: per-contract fee not priceable (px=0.0) — keeping the modeled figure`. **The alpha half applied and the fee half did not** — precisely the combination P318 identified as a PURE LOOSENING (calibrated alpha against cheap modelled fees), live for one tick.
  - **Cause: the gate block read `market_data.get("price")` — a key NO producer writes.** The price lives under `current_price` (main.py reads it that way at :7427, :8457, :9220 and elsewhere). That is the P2 reader/writer mismatch, committed inside the fix for a mispricing, by the author who had spent the day cataloguing exactly that class. Fixed to read `current_price` with `price` as a fallback, and pinned by a test that fails if the block stops reading a key a producer actually writes.
  - **The DURABLE fix is the second one: the interlock now gates on EFFECT, not on the FLAG.** Both flags were true, so a flag-based interlock was perfectly satisfied while the correction it existed to protect had silently not happened. `resolve_seat_edge` now takes the price and REFUSES the calibrated alpha whenever `cde_fee_bps` cannot price that asset — so a fee failure can never again leave the loosening half applied alone. **A flag being on is not evidence that the thing it enables took effect**, and an interlock built on flags cannot tell those two states apart.
  - Verified: `px = 0.0 / -1.0 / NaN` all fall back to the asserted 30.0, while a good price gives 88.1. An anti-vacuity test pins that the call site actually supplies the price, or the whole check is inert (P174).
  - **Mitigation pattern:** verify an arming by reading the live log for BOTH halves of what you armed — the suite was green, the flags were true, the config note was correct, and the thing still did not happen. Only the log showed it (P295b, again).
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) when the objective is restated, re-derive the VERDICTS, not just the plan — two of my recommendations reversed on the same evidence purely because I had been scoring against buy-and-hold instead of against zero; (b) never judge an asset on a configuration it was not selected with — SOL looked like the obvious drop under two configs and beats holding under its own; (c) the choice of statistic (min / median / mean) is a risk-preference decision that changes which assets trade, so it belongs in a pinned constant with its reasoning, never inside an ad-hoc calculation.

### P320. [MEASURED + BUILT 2026-08-19, operator: "resolve this"] The asserted alpha calibrated to the SEAT'S OWN holding horizon — and the calibration is a VERDICT: SOL asserts a negative edge, BTC clears in one era of three, ETH clears in all three
P318 left exactly one blocking item: the gate compares a per-TICK alpha constant against a per-ROUND-TRIP friction, so the fee correction could not be armed alone. This resolves it by replacing the constant with a measurement. Module `core/seat_alpha.py`; tests `tests/test_p320_seat_alpha_calibration.py` (17); five falsification probes red-then-restored; full suite 5,381 passing; gate clean, zero baseline movement.
- **THE MEASUREMENT** (`training/funding_legs_lab`, 6y, honest per-contract fees; gross bps per ROUND TRIP = 2x gross per unit turnover, which is the quantity `check_alpha_gate` actually compares against):
  | asset | pre_design | design | validation | **MIN (asserted)** |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | BTC | 2.3 | 68.5 | 24.1 | **2.3** |
  | ETH | 251.7 | 88.1 | 52.1 | **52.1** |
  | SOL | 427.6 | 221.7 | **−20.8** | **−20.8** |
- **The MINIMUM is used because a safety control must assume the worst era repeats** (P167). The mean would be a deliberate loosening (BTC 2.3 → 31.6, SOL −20.8 → **+209.5**) and needs its own entry. That choice of statistic IS the decision, and it is pinned by test.
- **Read off the table this is a verdict, not a knob.** **SOL asserts a NEGATIVE edge** — it loses money per round trip in the most recent era, so it can never clear friction and stops trading on its own arithmetic, independently confirming the risk-column reading (hold beats the SOL book on return, Sharpe AND drawdown). **BTC's worst era is 2.3bps against ~28bps of honest round-trip friction, and even its validation era (24.1) sits below cost** — profitable in one era of three, the era-fragility P243/P244 treat as disqualifying. **ETH clears in every era (min 52.1)** and is also the certified, un-fitted P247 config that beats hold on Sharpe (0.72 vs 0.58) with 41% less drawdown.
- **THE INTERLOCK IS THE LOAD-BEARING PART.** `seat_alpha_calibrated` requires `coinbase_per_contract_fees`, and vice versa, because each half alone moves the gate the WRONG way: calibrated alpha alone raises ETH's assertion 22.5 → 52.1 while the fee stays ~3x understated (a pure loosening); honest fees alone reject trades whose realized edge is 2.5x-27x their cost (P318). They are halves of one correction. Enforced inside `resolve_seat_edge`, so it is pinned by CALLING the function rather than by reading source — a source pin proves the code was written, not that it runs (P234/P307b).
- **With BOTH armed, the gate's answer is ~flat:** BTC **−32.9**, ETH **−3.9**, SOL **−80.3** against their thresholds. ETH is the only near miss and it fails only through the regime/smart-beta multiplier (~1.156): at a neutral multiplier it clears by +3.7. Both flags stay OFF and absent from the live profile — arming them stops trading, which is an operator decision (P141), and it is now a decision between two honest numbers rather than a bet on two wrong ones.
- **Fail directions, all toward NOT trading:** an unknown asset asserts 0.0 (a seat with no measurement must not trade on one); a negative calibration passes through NEGATIVE rather than being clamped, because "this seat loses money per round trip" is information the gate should act on and clamping would silently upgrade it to merely-unprofitable; a FLAT book asserts nothing (a round-trip value must never be asserted for a position not being taken); an uncalibrated SEAT keeps its own constant, so calibrating one seat cannot silently re-price another whose horizon was never measured.
- **Two guards from other entries fired during this work, both correctly, and both were re-expressed rather than weakened.** (a) P256's `test_alpha_constant_is_not_looser_than_the_trend_seat` pinned the literal `30.0 * abs(` inside the seat block; the constant moved into the resolver, so the guard now asserts the PROPERTY behaviourally (default path returns exactly `30.0 x |dir|`), which is strictly stronger — a substring cannot tell 30.0 being asserted from 30.0 merely appearing. (b) P318's own anti-rot guard said "if the seat ever becomes horizon-calibrated, RE-OPEN the arming decision rather than leave it blocked by a stale comment" — it fired on exactly that change and was updated to its resolved state. Neither was relaxed to admit this work (P248).
- **A structural hazard found while shrinking the seat block, worth its own note.** P256 and P265 both guard the seat with a fixed 5,000-character window from the `REGIMEBOOK SEAT` marker, and this change initially pushed the P149 sleeve-bridge write past it — so an unrelated test failed with a message about the P170 two-dict trap. The fix was to move the reasoning into `core/seat_alpha.py` and leave ~5 lines at the call site (now 4,899 chars to the bridge). **A character-count window is a guard whose failure mode is a confusing message about the wrong defect**, and it silently penalises documenting a decision at its call site. Recorded rather than changed, because widening it would weaken two other workstreams' guards.
- **[P320b — the follow-up that checked the one number the whole thing now rests on, and it VINDICATED the control I suspected.]** With both corrections armed, ETH fails by only **−3.9**, and the difference between clearing and failing is entirely the regime/smart-beta gate multiplier. Live composition: `[Aggression] gate_mult=0.99` then `[SMART_BETA_APPLY] gate 0.99→1.14` — two hardcoded constants, `NEUTRAL_DRIFT` ×1.10 and `VOL_COMPRESSED` ×1.05 = **×1.155**. Neither has ever been validated (P225 audited smart beta and left them as asserted), and **36 of 36** applies in the retained log carry both, so today it is a permanent tightening.
  - **First correction to my own read:** it is NOT a constant wearing a conditional's name. The `NEUTRAL_DRIFT` tag fires on `regime in {QUIET_ACCUMULATION, WEAK_CONSOLIDATION, NEUTRAL_DRIFT}`, which is **54.4%** of history — it is only 100% *right now* because the market is in QUIET_ACCUMULATION.
  - **Second, and it settles the question: the tightening is pointed the RIGHT way.** Book gross per round trip, split by whether the gate tightens: **ETH 204.1 (neutral) vs 252.0 (other); BTC 28.1 vs 112.6.** The book earns materially less exactly where smart beta demands more alpha, on both assets. So ETH's −3.9 rejection is a legitimate verdict, not an artifact of an unvalidated control — the suspicion was reasonable and the measurement refuted it.
  - **THE SHARPEST NUMBER, and it explains the live loss.** The P320 calibration is the era-minimum of the UNCONDITIONAL edge; the apples-to-apples figure is the era-minimum WITHIN the regime the multiplier actually applies to. Measured: **ETH validation-era neutral-regime = −103.0 bps/RT (31 legs, gross −16.0%), BTC = −1.5.** Conditioned on the regime that has actually prevailed, the book has been LOSING in the most recent era on both assets — which is consistent with the live sleeve's −$251 over 66 days, and means the shipped 52.1 assertion is if anything OPTIMISTIC for current conditions.
  - **[P320c — CORRECTION, same session, operator asked me to investigate my own verdict.] Both halves of "the book should not be trading in the current regime, and the live P&L agrees" were OVERSTATED, and one was a self-contradiction.**
    - **The regime-conditional loss is NOT statistically established.** Block-bootstrapped on the per-bar gross: **ETH −16.0% gross, t = −0.72, CI90 [−54.9, +21.8]; BTC −0.6%, t = −0.03, CI90 [−20.6, +20.3].** Both CIs comfortably span zero. The point estimates are negative and that is all — I built a verdict on **31 legs** (ETH) and then leaned on it as though it were a measurement.
    - **The live P&L does NOT agree, and I had already said so myself.** The clean window (where the 2026-08-16 deposit is correctly booked, so PnL is real) is **2026-08-18 07:03 → 08-19 04:39 — 21.6h, ≈5 ticks, −$27.79**. The −$251 accumulated over 66 days almost entirely under the TREND seat; the book has been decider for about a day. Earlier in this same session I wrote "the live sleeve has never yet been a test of the book" and then cited the −$251 as confirmation. **That is the P228 failure applied to my own reasoning: a claim reused without re-checking whether its premise still held.**
    - **What the corrected evidence supports, stated precisely:** arming the two flags stops trading because the calibration is deliberately CONSERVATIVE (era-minimum, so a safety control assumes its worst era repeats) — **not because the book has been shown to be unprofitable now.** Those are different arguments and collapsing them made the case sound stronger than it is. The unchanged, large-sample findings stand: per-contract fees ~3x the model, the per-tick-vs-round-trip units mismatch, book 144.5% vs hold 186.3% over 6y on return, ETH ahead of hold on Sharpe and drawdown, and the SmartBeta tightening pointed the right way.
    - **Verified while checking, and it held:** `regime_mapping` confirms 2/3/4 are the neutral set, and the parquet, training GMM and deployed GMM share one mtime — so the conditional analysis used a coherent artifact set (P215) rather than labels from a superseded fit. That was a genuine candidate flaw and it checked out.
    - **Mitigation pattern:** a point estimate on a thin conditional slice is a hypothesis, and the moment it is used to justify a live decision it needs an interval — the sign alone is what a coin flip also provides. And when an earlier message in the SAME session already recorded the limitation ("never yet a test of the book"), citing the same number as evidence later is not a new inference, it is forgetting one.
  - **Why the calibration is nevertheless left UNCONDITIONAL:** the regime-conditional cells are badly under-powered (ETH pre_design neutral is **14 legs**, giving a meaningless +503 bps/RT). Shipping a table built from 14-leg cells would be fitting noise, which is the failure this whole campaign exists to avoid. The system's design already handles regime the right way — an unconditional alpha assertion plus a regime multiplier ON THE THRESHOLD — and both halves are now measured to point in the correct direction.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) when a gate's two sides are measured in different units, calibrate BOTH or neither — an interlock in code is the only thing that stops a future session shipping the half that happens to be ready; (b) prefer the worst-era statistic for anything a safety control asserts, and pin the CHOICE of statistic, because the same data yields +209.5 or −20.8 for SOL depending on it; (c) when a calibration replaces a constant, the result is often a verdict about what should trade at all — here it independently reproduced the drop-SOL conclusion from a completely different direction.

### P319. [PROBED + FIXED 2026-08-19, operator: "check cryptopanic plan, if we arrange this api properly"] The answer is no — and one of the two reasons was a fix of MINE that was inert on the live system
Probed from the trading server rather than reasoned from the code (the P293b lesson: the vendor's own error body outranks my arithmetic about its quota). Tests `tests/test_p319_cryptopanic_backoff_and_probe.py` (12); four falsification probes red-then-restored, one of which exposed a vacuous pin of my own.
- **WHAT THE PROBE ESTABLISHED.** We are on `growth/v2`; **`developer/v2` 404s and `v1` 403s**, so the plan tier is baked into `BASE_URL` and cannot be changed by editing a URL (P154's warning, now verified rather than quoted). The key answers `429 {"info":"API monthly quota exceeded"}` on **every** path including the combined form, so the exhaustion is vendor-confirmed. **What could NOT be established, and is therefore recorded as operator-side rather than guessed: the plan's true monthly limit.** Both the plans page and the API docs are JS-rendered — 12,710 bytes of HTML that reduce to "You are using an outdated browser" — so scraping settles nothing. **P154 measured ~540 requests/month against a Growth plan's nominal 3,000 and concluded the bill was a billing question; the quota being exhausted at that rate contradicts one of those two numbers, and only the dashboard can say which.**
- **DEFECT 1, mine: the persisted backoff made P299 INERT.** P299 replaced the month-long monthly-quota lockout with a daily re-probe — but only for a backoff computed **from then on**. The value already on disk was `2026-09-01T00:00:00Z`, restored verbatim on every boot (`[CRYPTOPANIC] restored state: news=0 age=75510s backoff=2026-09-01T00:00:00+00:00`), so the live feed stayed dark until September exactly as before and my fix did nothing where it mattered. **That is P261b verbatim — a migration case is a first-boot case with pre-existing state, and "the new code computes it correctly" says nothing about the value already persisted.** A restored backoff is now capped at the re-probe interval. Capping only ever SHORTENS, so a legitimate `Retry-After` backoff is untouched and the fail direction stays "retry sooner than necessary"; an ABSENT backoff stays absent, because missing is not a backoff (P2).
- **DEFECT 2, measured rather than guessed: we issue THREE requests where one might do.** One per currency, per fetch. If `currencies=BTC,ETH,SOL` is an **OR**, a single request returns the same corpus for a third of the quota — and on a plan whose real limit we cannot read, a 3× reduction may be the difference between fitting and not. If it is an **AND**, combining returns only posts tagged with all three: a coverage collapse that reads exactly like a quiet news day (P2). **Neither the API (429) nor the docs (JS-rendered) can settle it, so the change was NOT made.** Instead a one-shot probe measures it the first time the quota is healthy, LOGS what each outcome would mean, and acts on nothing — converting "someone must remember to test this" into a mechanism (P230/P280). It is gated on a fully healthy cycle (spending a request while the quota is blown is the behaviour P293b and P299 spent two entries removing), persisted so it runs once ever, and wrapped so a measurement can never break the feed it measures.
- **A VACUOUS PIN OF MY OWN, caught by its own probe.** `test_the_probe_is_one_shot_and_persisted` asserted the string `"combined_probe_done"` appears in the source — and stayed **green** when the PERSIST key was renamed, because the string still appeared in the RESTORE. The marker would then not survive a restart and the probe would re-run every process, which is the exact cost it exists to avoid. Rewritten as a behavioural round-trip through disk, then re-probed red. **A source-text pin cannot tell two call sites apart; if a property is "it survives a restart", the test has to restart something.**
- **Baseline: `silent.tryexcept_count` 709 → 710, hand-bumped with attribution, never `--update` (P171).** The one new guard is the probe's. It carries a `# noqa: silent-swallow`, but that counter comes from `silent_failure_audit.py`, which is **not** noqa-aware (P223) — hence a bump rather than a suppression. A concurrent +1 seen locally came from another session's UNCOMMITTED files and was deliberately **not** absorbed into my attribution.
