- **Mitigation pattern:** when a fix changes how a value is COMPUTED, grep for the persisted copy of that value in the same breath — persistence turns yesterday's policy into today's behaviour, and every "the new code handles it" argument has to be re-checked against the state already on disk. And when a cheaper call pattern depends on a semantic you cannot verify (OR vs AND), the honest ship is the MEASUREMENT, not the optimisation.

### P318. [MEASURED 2026-08-19, operator: "can you leverage the research mode to solve it" -> "implement"] The gate is wrong on BOTH sides — and the ALPHA side is understated MORE than the fee was, so P315's own "arm it" recommendation is WITHDRAWN before it shipped
P315 established that CDE fees are per-contract and ~3x the modeled figure, and recommended arming `coinbase_per_contract_fees`. Research mode was then pointed at "can we make it profitable end to end", and the answer inverted the recommendation. **Arming the honest fee ALONE would reject trades whose realized edge is 2.5x-27x their cost.** Nothing live was changed; the flag stays OFF, now for the OPPOSITE reason to the one P315 recorded.
- **THE MEASUREMENT that flips it.** Realized gross per position change, at honest per-contract fees, 6y (`training/funding_legs_lab`):
  | asset | config | legs | gross bps/leg | cost bps/leg | ratio |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | BTC | un-damped | 814 | 40.1 | 16.21 | **2.5x** |
  | BTC | bands 4/4/24 | 313 | 137.4 | 16.18 | **8.5x** |
  | ETH | un-damped | 248 | 112.5 | 15.09 | **7.5x** |
  | ETH | bands 4/4/24 | 60 | 450.2 | 16.59 | **27.1x** |
  The gate asserts a FLAT **30bps** of edge per trade (the regimebook seat's `_rb_edge = 30.0 * abs(_rb_dir)`) against **27.7bps** of round-trip friction at honest fees — **~1.08x, i.e. break-even**. The book holds ~40 bars and captures 40-450bps per leg.
- **The defect is a UNITS MISMATCH, not a threshold.** `30.0` was calibrated for the TREND seat's fast per-tick signal (P231 records `base_edge_bps = 40` as "a constant chosen to clear the gate"), and the P298 regimebook seat inherited the same shape for a multi-WEEK regime book. So the gate compares a per-TICK alpha against a per-ROUND-TRIP cost. Correcting the cost side while leaving the alpha side at 30bps does not make the gate honest — it makes it wrong in the expensive direction, which is exactly the failure P167 warns about with the sign reversed.
- **WITHDRAWN, in writing, at both sites:** the flag's declaration in main.py now carries the measured table and the instruction that arming REQUIRES the alpha side calibrated to the seat's own holding horizon first; the P315 test that pinned "not yet armed" is rewritten to pin "not armed BECAUSE the alpha side is understated more", so the guard states the real reason. An anti-rot test fails if the seat ever stops asserting the flat 30bps — because at that moment the objection dissolves and the arming decision must be RE-OPENED rather than left blocked by a stale comment.
- **The research that produced it, recorded because two of its steps changed my mind.** (a) The turnover lever is real — P256's persistence bands cut BTC cost 132.0% -> 50.6% and took the book to 328.3% net / Sharpe 1.01 / CI excluding zero, positive in all three eras. (b) **It does NOT transfer**: the same bands leave ETH at 191.2 vs hold 253.8 (Sharpe 0.71 -> 0.51, WORSE) and SOL at 502.9 vs hold 703.3 — 1-of-3 against P262's 5-of-5 bar, the signature of a 6-config sweep read on its own window. Those parameters are NOT shipped. (c) The correct baseline is RISK, not return (P301's lesson): ETH's already-certified, un-fitted trend-only book returns 184.1 vs hold's 253.8 but at **Sharpe 0.72 vs 0.58 and 41% less drawdown (-87.0 vs -147.9)**.
- **The era split is the real shape of the product, and it is why the 6-year aggregate misleads.** Book vs hold, honest fees: pre-design **-27.1 / -19.5** (BTC) and 34.1 / 75.3 (ETH) — hold wins the bulls; design 36.2 / 96.3 and 24.5 / 54.5 — hold wins; **validation (most recent) +8.8 / -29.4 (BTC) and +5.8 / -24.3 (ETH), with a THIRD of the drawdown — the book wins outright, and holding LOSES MONEY.** A crash-dodge underperforms in bull markets by construction, so scoring it on a bull-dominated sample answers the wrong question. Caveat kept: the validation window is the most-read in this repo (~7 reads, P260) and carries that discount.
- **SOL: hold wins on every metric and every slice** (return 703.3 vs 336.2/502.9, Sharpe 1.08 vs 0.81/0.85, drawdown -247.6 vs -199.1/-262.3). Dropping it is supported — but the sanctioned mechanism is the ROUTING set (`scripts/coinbase_set_assets.sh`), which is operator-run by design (P141), and there is deliberately NO config-only way to zero an asset: **both sizing paths floor at one contract** (`_sized_contracts` returns `max(1, ...)`, `target_for`'s fixed fallback does `int(... or 1)` then `max(1, fixed)`). Recorded rather than routed around.
- **What is NOT established.** No configuration has been shown to beat holding on RETURN across the three traded assets at honest fees; the one that does on BTC is fitted. The live -$251 is still not a test of any of this — the book became the decider two days ago and the loss belongs to the trend seat (16h IC -0.046). And the capital constraint is unmoved: at ~$10.8k only nano contracts are tradeable, CDE lists nothing between nano (0.01 BTC) and BTI (1 BTC, ~$64k = 6x sleeve equity), so the fee floor stays ~9-14bps/leg until roughly $427k of equity.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) when a gate compares two numbers, check the UNITS of both before correcting either — here the cost was per-round-trip and the alpha per-tick, so fixing the cost alone would have made a correct-looking gate reject 8x-edge trades; (b) a cost correction is only half a calibration, and shipping half a calibration is not conservative just because that half tightens; (c) when a recommendation is overturned by the next measurement, correct it AT THE SITE that would be acted on — P315's "arm it" was already in this file and in the flag's own comment, and a future session would have executed it without ever seeing this entry.

### P315. [MEASURED 2026-08-19, operator: "read the code line by line, from the purpose of making the system actually trade and make money ... start the research mode"] CDE charges a FLAT FEE PER CONTRACT, the model charged 0/3bps — the live gate has been taking BTC entries at net −11bps, and the six-year certification was priced the same wrong way
The Trade-Frequency Reality Check ran first, and it PASSES: the system trades. 66 days live, **80 position changes (1.21/day), in-market on 85% of ledger records**, −$251.44 = −2.27% on invested capital (−6.3% on the $3,997 actually deployed for most of the window ≈ **−35%/yr**), bleeding a steady −$25..−$50/week rather than in one event. So the bottleneck is economics, not plumbing — and the economics were wrong at the source. Module `core/cde_fees.py`; tests `tests/test_p315_per_contract_fees.py` (20); five falsification probes red-then-restored.
- **THE MEASUREMENT, from the venue's own reported fees rather than from our constants** (`data/fill_quality.jsonl`, the ledger P290 built for exactly this and which nobody had yet read for FEES — P290 re-derived only the SPREAD):
  | asset | liquidity | ct | notional | fee$ | fee/ct | **realized bps** |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | BTC | maker | 2 | $1,282.10 | 1.202 | 0.601 | **9.37** |
  | BTC | taker | 2 | $1,286.60 | 1.269 | 0.635 | **9.87** |
  | BTC | maker | 2 | $1,288.70 | 1.207 | 0.603 | **9.36** |
  | ETH | maker | 6 | $1,149.90 | 1.582 | 0.264 | **13.76** |
  `core/paper_fee_service.VENUE_FEE_STD` prices Coinbase at **maker 0.0000 / taker 0.0003** — 0bps and 3bps. So the taker leg is understated ~3x and the maker leg is understated by INFINITY: the model charges nothing for something that costs ~9.4bps.
- **The cause is structural, which is why no recalibration fixes it: the fee is a FLAT ~$0.60 (BTC) / ~$0.26 (ETH) PER CONTRACT.** Its cost in bps is `fee / (contract_size × price)` — it moves INVERSELY with price and is largest exactly where the contract is smallest. A percentage model cannot represent that at any calibration. ETH's per-contract fee is *lower in dollars* than BTC's and *higher in bps* (13.8 vs 9.4), because a 0.1-ETH nano is $192 of notional against BTC's $644 — pinned by test, because that inversion is the whole point.
- **IT CHANGES THE LIVE DECISION, not just the accounting.** Tonight's gate: `[VENUE-FEE] BTC: priced for COINBASE (taker=3.0bps maker=0.0bps)` → `Alpha gate: passes=True, alpha=22bps, threshold=18bps, net=8bps → ALLOW`. P167 charges friction ROUND TRIP (2 legs) at a 1.1x multiplier, so restoring the omitted ~6.9bps/leg moves that threshold to **~33bps against 22bps of asserted alpha — net ≈ −11bps, i.e. REJECT.** Every entry has been taken on economics that understate cost ~3x. Priced against actual turnover the fee bill is **~$74, about 29% of the 66-day loss** (a lower bound: the ledger is per-tick and cannot see intra-tick round trips) — against the ~$31 the gate believes it is paying.
- **AND THE CERTIFICATION WAS PRICED THE SAME WAY, which is the larger finding.** `funding_legs_lab.py:43` charged "half the measured CDE spread + **3bps fee**". Re-run with the honest per-bar fee (control first: `legacy_3bps` reproduces P297 to the decimal — book 243.90 / trend_only 151.66 / increment 92.24 / cost 32.56):
  | series | as certified (3bps) | **honest per-contract** |
  |---|---|---|
  | book (trend + funding legs) | 243.90 | **144.50** |
  | trend_only (P262-certified) | 151.66 | **120.53** |
  | **buy_and_hold** (does not trade; fee-immune) | 186.71 | **186.26** |
  | INCREMENT (funding legs) | 92.24 | **23.97** (Sharpe 0.59 → 0.15, CI [−0.56,+0.84]) |
  Cost 32.56% → **131.96%**. **At honest fees BOTH certified strategies LOSE to simply holding BTC over six years.** The re-priced cost is 4x the naive 2.6x scaling because the bps fee was far higher at historically lower prices — at BTC $10k a 0.01 nano is ~$100 of notional, so $0.60 is ~60bps/leg.
- **THE VIABILITY THRESHOLD, swept rather than asserted.** Because the fee is flat per contract, a contract N× larger divides its bps cost by N:
  | contract size | fee $/ct | book net% | B&H% | verdict |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | **1x (nano, today)** | 0.635 | **144.5** | 186.3 | **hold wins** |
  | 2x | 0.318 | 206.4 | 186.5 | book beats hold |
  | 5x | 0.127 | 243.6 | 186.6 | book beats hold |
  **Break-even sits between 1x and 2x** — the strategy needs roughly HALF the per-contract fee to justify trading at all. Two ways to get there, and they are arithmetically equivalent because cost scales linearly with turnover: **double the contract denomination, or halve the turnover** (at k=0.5 turnover, book ≈ 210% > 186%).
- **But there is no intermediate BTC product.** CDE lists nano `BIT` at 0.01 BTC and institutional `BTI` at **1 BTC = 100x nano** — $64,000 of notional per contract, **six times the entire sleeve equity**. Nothing sits between. So at ~$10.8k equity **nano is the only tradeable size, and at nano size six years of history say holding beats the certified book.** That is a CAPITAL-SCALE conclusion, not a signal one, and it is the first result in this campaign that no amount of signal work can move.
- **Shipped DEFAULT OFF, and arming it is the operator's decision (P141).** `coinbase_per_contract_fees` (P201 trio, absent from the live profile, pinned absent). Applied with `max()` so it can only ever RAISE the charge — a fee correction that could lower it would re-open the undercharging it exists to end (P167). An unpriceable tick keeps the modeled figure rather than fabricating a free trade (P2). **The expected consequence of arming it is that BTC stops entering**, which is the correct answer on this evidence and is exactly why it is not flipped silently — the P291 precedent, where a cost correction that changed which assets could trade was gated and armed only on explicit instruction.
- **SCOPE OF THE CONTAMINATION, stated honestly.** The same 3bps sits under `train_supervised_full.COST_BPS` ({BTC 6.0, ETH 8.0, SOL 13.0} ROUND-TRIP = 3bps/leg on BTC), which `mechanism_lab` imports, and under `sentiment_history_lab` and `breadth_exam`. So P262, P296, P301, P306, P307d and every verdict resting on them are priced at roughly a THIRD of the real nano-contract cost. **They are not thereby wrong — they are valid for a bps-fee execution and ~3-4x optimistic for the nano-contract CDE execution this system actually uses.** ETH and SOL are worse than BTC, not better (13.8bps and an assumed 17.4bps against BTC's 9.4).
- **SOL is ASSUMED, not measured, and says so.** No SOL fill with a reported fee exists, so it prices at the most expensive measured per-contract fee with `provenance="assumed_no_measured_fill"` (P167/P169: an unmeasured cost is assumed expensive, and an assumption must never read as a measurement). One measured SOL fill closes it. Pre-committed revision rule, the P290 pattern: these constants may be RAISED on any evidence and LOWERED only on ≥20 filled legs for that asset plus a new P-entry.
- **A correction to my own reporting inside the same session:** I first told the operator "friction is small (~$31-60)" — computed with the very model this entry corrects. Recomputed at measured per-contract fees it is ~$74 and ~29% of the loss. The lesson is the one this file keeps recording: **when you estimate a cost, check which model produced the estimate before quoting it.**
- **Also found while reading the money path, recorded not fixed:** `coinbase_whale_filter_enforce` is ON and has acted twice in two days (1 `block_entry`, 1 `flip_to_flat`) against the certified book, while whale's own IC is **t=0.26** (noise, P293c) — and the code comment at its own call site says *"this ships enforcement-OFF with a ledger ... the ledger is what earns the flip."* It was armed one day after that ledger began, so two days of evidence cannot have earned it.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) a fee expressed as a percentage is a MODEL, and the venue's reported fee is a MEASUREMENT — P169 built the provenance field to keep them apart, and the fee side of `fill_quality.jsonl` still went unread for a month while the spread side got re-derived; read every column of an instrument you built; (b) when a cost is charged per UNIT rather than per NOTIONAL, its bps cost is a function of price and contract size, so a backtest spanning a 6x price range cannot be priced by any constant — check the CHARGING BASIS before choosing the units; (c) when a strategy fails only on cost, sweep the cost to find the break-even before concluding anything about the signal — here it converted "the book is uncertified" into the far more useful "the book needs 2x the contract denomination, and no such contract exists at this capital".

### P314. [FIXED 2026-08-19, operator: "tackle this"] `llm_sentiment` was dark on BTC and SOL because the ARRIVAL RATE was too low — not because 4h was unreachable, which is how I had recorded it
**A correction to my own P299/P308 entries.** I measured the RSS corpus at 94 dated items, median age **20.5h**, with only 9 inside the agent's 4h freshness window (BTC **0**, ETH 1, SOL **0**), concluded that widening the window would fabricate recency — true — and then wrote that the darkness was "a data fact". **The second half was wrong.** The window was never the problem; four publishers are a CORPUS, not a firehose. More and denser sources change no definition of freshness at all. Tests `tests/test_p314_rss_arrival_rate.py` (17); four falsification probes red-then-restored.
- **Probed before adding anything (P218), in-window yield per candidate:** `gnews_btc/eth/sol` **100-102 items, 4-6 inside 4h each** (decisive); `bitcoincom` 10/4 median 5.1h; `cryptoslate` 10/3 median 7.1h; `ambcrypto` 16/2 median 7.2h; `coindesk` 25/2 median 12.7h. **REJECTED with their numbers:** `newsbtc` median **174.9h**, 0 inside 4h; `utoday` 97 items but median **64.4h**, 1 inside 4h. Both answer HTTP 200 with well-formed XML, so they LOOK healthy — **a source whose items are days old adds corpus and no freshness, which is exactly the failure being fixed**, and both are recorded in `REJECTED_SOURCES` so they are not re-added on a hunch. `coindesk` returns: P293c omitted it because the bare domain 308-redirects and "an unverified redirect target is not a source"; the arc outboundfeeds URL answers 200 and is now verified.
- **ASSET SCOPING, because the query IS the relevance claim.** A Google-News "bitcoin" result headlined *"Crypto market rallies as ETF inflows resume"* is about BTC even though the word-bounded matcher cannot see it, so attributing by title alone would discard most of what makes those feeds worth adding. A scoped source answers for its own asset; title matching still runs on top so one item can serve several. Scope is ONE asset, never a wildcard, and P293c's word-bounding (which stops solution/sold/console flooding SOL) is untouched — both pinned, both probed.
- **MEASURED AFTER, live in production:** `[RSS] 455 headline(s) from 11 source(s); relevant {BTC: 165, ETH: 116, SOL: 107}` (was 94 from 4), `[RSS_BLEND] BTC: +9` and `SOL: +5` where neither had a blend line at all, and — the thing that was actually broken — **`LLM_SENTIMENT BTC/SOL: src=haiku status=live tradeable=True`**, where both had read `src=f&g status=fallback_global headlines=0 tradeable=False`.
- **RECORDED, NOT CLAIMED.** This makes the agent able to SPEAK, not right: its 90d IC is +0.040 (4h) / +0.048 (16h), both insignificant, and it reaches an order only through P298's de-risking-only conviction channel. It also turns on ~2 extra Haiku calls per 4H tick (BTC and SOL previously fell back to F&G and skipped Haiku entirely), and Google News aggregates lower-quality outlets than the four curated feeds. The headline cap of 50 per call is unchanged, so what improved is the FRESHNESS of those 50, not their number.
- **[SHARED-TREE INCIDENT, and this one has now happened in BOTH directions on the same day.]** I ran `git add <my two files>` and committed — and the commit contained **8 files**, because **`git commit` commits the whole INDEX, not the paths you just added**, and the parallel session had already staged their in-flight work into it. My commit went CI-red on their mid-edit state; their own next commit was titled *"P311b: HOTFIX — P311 swept a parallel session's uncommitted main.py hunk into its commit"*, i.e. the identical accident pointing the other way, hours apart. Their hotfix cleared the red and my change survived intact at origin. **The mechanism, not the lesson: in a shared tree commit with a PATHSPEC — `git commit -- <paths>` — which commits only those paths and ignores whatever else is staged.** `git add` followed by a bare `git commit` is not path-scoped and never was; P225's "treat my staged changes as already-shared state" understated it, because the danger is not only what I stage, it is what someone else staged while I was not looking.
- **Mitigation pattern:** when a gap is written off as "a data fact", check whether the constraint is the THRESHOLD or the SUPPLY. Here the threshold was correct and defensible, the supply was four slow publishers, and the honest fix touched no definition of freshness — it just measured a dozen candidate feeds and kept the ones that were actually fast. Two of the twelve were rejected for staleness alone, which is the same measurement saying no.

### P313. [FIXED 2026-08-18, operator: "can you read through the codebase" -> "fix all"] A config surface that could neither take effect nor complain — and the naive fix would have ARMED a hold-cost trap rather than closed one
Tests `tests/test_p311_config_surface_and_health.py` (41); five falsification probes red-then-restored; full suite green; gate clean.
- **THE FINDING, verified through the real parser rather than read off the dataclass.** 28 `ProductionConfig` fields were declared, defaulted (**17 of them to True**), read on the live path — and assigned NOWHERE in `from_file`. Writing `profit_max_enabled: false` into the live profile returned **True**. And `validate_loaded_config` only inspected the `risk` and `tranche` SECTIONS, so the same key raised no "unknown key" warning either: **silent in both directions — it could not work, and it could not tell you.** All 28 date to the initial commit, so this was never a regression; it was born unwired (the P16 shape, inverted: gated but never settable).
- **The shape that made it invisible: the config exposed the DIAL but not the SWITCH.** `regime_leverage` — a real map putting the two dominant regimes at **2.0x** — parses fine; `regime_leverage_enabled`, which decides whether that map is applied at all, did not. Same for `thesis_budget` (its sub-parameters parse from a nested section, only its master `enabled` did not), and `profit_max` was ALREADY parsed as a nested section — but only **3 of its 12 keys**, so `ultra_aggressive_5y.json`'s documented `false_breakout_action: SCALE` works while a sibling `enabled` would not have. So the fix EXTENDS the conventions already in the file rather than inventing one.
- **THE PART THAT MATTERS MOST, and it inverts the risk: fixing the parsing alone would have shipped a hazard.** `update_funding_rate` has **exactly ONE production caller**, and it sat INSIDE the `if self.config.regime_leverage_enabled:` block — so **P291b's venue-true hold cost was transitively gated on a flag named for LEVERAGE**. With that flag off, `update_for_leverage` never runs (margin fees keep their **0.0** dataclass defaults) AND `_funding_bps_per_4h` stays None (so `_margin_cost_bps`'s venue-true branch cannot fire): the entire hold-cost term collapses to **0.0**. That is the **UNDERCHARGE** direction, which P167 names as the dangerous one — overcharging costs opportunity, undercharging spends money. An operator disabling "regime leverage" would reasonably expect to stop LEVERAGING, not to stop charging for CARRY. **Latent only because the flag could not be set — so making it settable and decoupling the funding call had to land in the SAME change.** P291's own wiring test pinned the `asset=` tag and independence from the spread flag, but nothing pinned reachability past this gate; that pin now exists. **ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING and is pinned too:** `update_funding_rate` takes `max(rollover, funding)` while `update_for_leverage` ASSIGNS that field, so funding must run AFTER the leverage block or the leverage write clobbers it.
- **How the parsing is done, and why not the obvious way.** A roster (`_LATE_CONFIG_KEYS`) is applied to the CONSTRUCTED object, so an absent key falls through to the dataclass default BY CONSTRUCTION — there is exactly ONE copy of every default and the table cannot drift from it. Restating defaults in `from_file` is precisely what P265 found silently reverting `thesis_budget_max_reentry` on every boot. A malformed value is logged and SKIPPED, keeping the default: a bad config line must not take the boot down (P85's restart-loop lesson) and must not silently install a wrong-typed value either. `bool` is checked before `int` (bool is an int subclass). **Byte-identical for the live profile, asserted field-by-field across the whole dataclass** — no profile sets any of these today.
- **DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED, each with its reason at source:** `mode` is documentation-only (the CLI overwrites it unconditionally, P227) and EVERY profile already sets it, so parsing it would be a live behaviour change wearing a hygiene fix's clothes; `simulate_disconnect_tick` is a TEST hook, and making it config-settable would hand a live profile an exchange-disconnect simulator (a P141 activation, not plumbing).
- **The schema half.** `validate_toplevel_keys` flags unknown top-level keys, and the known set is **PASSED IN from `dataclasses.fields(ProductionConfig)`** rather than restated in the schema module (P310's rule: import the producer's declaration). It is additive — omitting the argument preserves the old behaviour exactly. Verified against **all 11 profiles: zero false warnings**, because a schema that cannot tell a real key from a typo makes the operator ignore the one line that would flag a REAL typo (P303).
- **HEALTH_T1 was firing every tick on 2 of 3 assets for the system working as designed.** The P298 regimebook seat overwrites direction/confidence/edge but never touched `primary_strategy` (set by `core/trend_decision_layer.py:270`), so a certified-FLAT book (ETH/SOL trend-only outside bull) logged `edge=0bps with strategy=trend_following — alpha gate may reject`, naming a producer that did not make the decision. Live counts confirm the mechanism exactly: **ETH 4, SOL 4, BTC 0** — BTC never warns because its book is directional. The check's return value is discarded, so this was never behavioural — **but T1 exists to catch the P155-era "a strategy is live but edge is 0, so nothing can trade" condition, and a permanent benign WARN on two assets makes a REAL edge=0 fault there indistinguishable from the standing one** (P202/P240). Fixed BOTH ends: the seat now reports itself (`primary_strategy = "regimebook"`, written to both dicts per P170), and T1 keys on whether a position is INTENDED rather than on a label. A nonzero direction with zero edge still WARNs — that is the fault worth hearing, and it is pinned.
- **The existing suite caught a real flaw in my own fix, which is the entry's sharpest lesson.** `test_warn_when_momentum_zero_edge` passes `intent=None`, and my first version read a missing direction as `0.0` — collapsing **UNKNOWN into FLAT** and PASSing the check exactly when there is least information. That is the P2 error, in the fix for a check about silence, in the direction of more silence. Unknown now falls through to the old label-based behaviour and still WARNs.
- **Falsification, five probes, each red-then-restored:** re-gate funding on leverage; disable the late overrides; stop passing `known_toplevel`; revert T1 to label-only; remove the seat's `primary_strategy`. **The first probe stayed GREEN and had to be fixed:** my test walked to the FIRST `if ... regime_leverage_enabled` node, and with the funding call re-gated there are TWO such blocks — it inspected the innocent one. Anchor uniqueness is never something to assume (P238); the helper now iterates every match.
- **A process note worth keeping.** The baseline bump was nearly made on an unattributed number: the on-disk counter read 708 against HEAD's 707, and the honest move was to reset to HEAD and re-derive the delta rather than accept it (P175). The true delta is **+3**, each attributed in `_p313_note`: the split leverage/funding guards, the config coercion guard, and T1's direction coercion. Bumped by hand — `--update` re-seeds all seven baselines including mypy (P171).
- **Minors closed in the same pass:** `strategies/_warmup_state.py`'s docstring claimed observations carry timestamps and are dropped by age; they are bare floats aged by ONE file stamp. Harmless — the consumer restores into a BOUNDED deque so stale values roll off structurally — but it is the P294 comment-as-claim shape, and it now says what the code does plus what would make it a real gap. P310's anti-vacuity guard was widened from **2 named writers to all 7**: its detector has three narrowing preconditions (directory list, the literal `strategy_shadow` string, an inline 3-key dict) and a guard that samples two modules cannot notice coverage shrinking — which is the failure a rot detector exists to prevent. **Corrected while inspecting: `strategies.derivatives_flow_v1` is NOT an escaped writer** — it only DECLARES names, and the module that actually writes its records is detected; `PRODUCER_MODULES` serves two roles, and only the writer direction is enforced.
- **Recorded and deliberately NOT changed:** the regimebook seat publishes `quant_confidence = 0.4` on a FLAT direction (the P224/P307 pattern fixed for soldex). Verified inert three ways — fusion detects abstention by `|dir| < 0.01` and excludes abstainers from the `avg_conf` denominator, the alpha gate rejects on `edge=0` regardless, and the DRL consumer is SHADOW — so changing it would move an attribution evidence stream for no behavioural gain (P299's discontinuity rule).
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) a config field is only settable if something ASSIGNS it — grep `from_file` for the assignment, never trust the dataclass declaration, and prove it through the parser; (b) before making a dormant switch settable, trace everything nested INSIDE its block — a flag named for one subsystem can be the only thing arming another, and arming the config surface without checking that ships the trap instead of closing it; (c) when a health check keys on a LABEL, any layer that later overwrites the DECISION without updating the label converts the check into permanent noise — and permanent benign noise is indistinguishable from the fault the check exists to catch.

### P310. [BUILT 2026-08-18, operator: "address this"] The producer/consumer contract — a MECHANISM for the class that produced P264, P295d and P309, instead of a fourth fix for a fourth instance
Three times a consumer's belief about a producer diverged silently, and all three passed their own tests: **P264** (the ledger parser read `ts` as ISO-only while regimebook writes an epoch float — the family was invisible to the promotion gate), **P295d** (`seat_check` read `agents.<name>.<h>` while the emitter writes `agents.<name>.horizons.<h>` — every candidate parsed to n=0 and the tool recommended flattening a live book from nothing), **P309** (the shadow allowlists keyed on the LEDGER-FILE PREFIX while the scorer groups by the record's `strategy` FIELD — two families never pooled, an archive section that never rendered). **The common cause is not carelessness, it is a test-design property: in each case the fixture and the consumer were written by the same hand and agreed with each other. A test that constructs BOTH sides cannot catch a naming or shape mismatch — only the producer can.** P280's rule applies: "make sure X" is a request for a mechanism, not a confirmation. Tests `tests/test_p310_producer_consumer_contract.py` (8); four falsification probes red-then-restored, one per historical shape; full suite 5,225; gate clean.
- **NAME CONTRACT (kills P309's class).** Every shadow producer now declares `SHADOW_STRATEGY_NAMES` — a single source (P172) that consumers may not restate. The conformance test asks the eight producers what they emit (31 names) and holds the consumer to it **in BOTH directions**: every classification must name a real producer (so `ma_filter` vs `ma_filtered` fails loudly), and every producer name must be classified exactly once (so a new family cannot silently default into per-asset scoring and into the promotion table with nobody having decided). A third class `PER_ASSET_FAMILIES` was needed to express "deliberately per-asset" as distinct from "nobody has looked at this yet" — the same missing-vs-neutral distinction (P2) this file enforces everywhere else.
- **It found two real gaps on its first run, which is the point.** (a) `regimebook_fgshort` — the parallel session's brand-new P307e producer, correctly added to the consumer's allowlist and absent from my declaration. (b) Five names emitted but classified nowhere: `mlpshadow`, `ml_factor`, `funding_mean_reversion`, `funding_post_etf_regime`, `regimebook_banded`. Now 31 declared, 31 classified, zero orphans either way.
- **SHAPE CONTRACT (kills P264's class).** A record built the way a producer builds it — epoch-float `ts` included — must survive the consumer's real loader, and both timestamp forms must parse, so fixing one shape cannot break the other. Plus the PREMISE P309 violated, pinned as a premise rather than left implicit in a comment: the scorer groups on the record's `strategy` value, not on the filename.
- **ANTI-ROT, because a registry is exactly the thing that silently stops covering.** An AST detector finds every module building a shadow-record dict (`strategy` + `asset` + `direction`) and fails if one is not in `PRODUCER_MODULES`. Scoped to modules that reference `strategy_shadow`: matching on `"strategy"` alone false-positived on a logging `extra={"strategy": ...}` in `p0_safety_integrator`, and on `exit_alpha_tracker` / `signal_quality_tracker`, which build dicts with the same three key names but write a different record family entirely. **A scanner that cries wolf is a scanner someone disables** (P202 applied to tooling). An anti-vacuity test asserts the detector still finds the known writers, or the rot check would pass forever (P174).
- **Falsification, one probe per historical failure:** reintroduce the P309 prefix keys → 2 red; add a producer name nobody classified → 1 red; make a producer stop declaring its names → 3 red; reintroduce the P264 ISO-only parse → 2 red.
- **A mistake worth keeping, made while running those probes:** cleaning up probe 3 with `git checkout -- defense/mlp_shadow.py` **silently wiped the uncommitted declaration** that probe was testing, and the suite went red for a reason unrelated to the probe. That is P265's recorded lesson verbatim — *probe reversal must be surgical string-edits, never a tree checkout* — and it is sharper when the work under test is itself uncommitted, because `checkout` reverts to HEAD rather than to the pre-probe state. Copy the file, or edit the string back.
- **Honest scope.** This covers the SHADOW-LEDGER producer/consumer boundary — the one that produced all three incidents. It does not cover P295d's boundary (`agent_ic_review` → `seat_check`), which has one producer and one consumer and is pinned by that entry's own literal-report fixture. The general principle it encodes is the transferable part: **when a consumer restates a producer's identifier or shape, the restatement is the defect waiting to happen; import it, or hold it to a contract the producer defines.**

### P309. [FIXED 2026-08-18, operator: "any remaining tasks"] Running P299's own features against REAL producer output found BOTH of them keyed on the wrong identifier — and the archive list would have buried two live strategies had it worked
The P264 discipline applied to myself: a decision tool's first run against real producer output is part of shipping it. P299 shipped pooled scoring and an archive section, both tested, neither ever run on live ledgers. Pulled the ledgers (`september_check.py`, end-to-end clean) and ran them. Tests `tests/test_p309_allowlists_key_on_strategy.py` (25) + the P299 pins re-keyed; four falsification probes red-then-restored; full suite 5,199; gate clean.
- **`compute_per_strategy_ic` groups by the record's `strategy` FIELD — I keyed both allowlists on the LEDGER-FILE PREFIX.** That is the P294 lesson verbatim ("the thing that separates two claims is whatever the SCORER groups by"), committed by the author who had quoted it two entries earlier. Consequences, all silent:
  | my key | real strategy name | effect |
  |---|---|---|
  | `ma_filter` | `ma_filtered` | never pooled |
  | `whale_filter` | `whale_filtered` | never pooled |
  | `cascade` | `cascade_anticipation` | archive never rendered |
  | `microstructure` | `ofi`, `kyle_lambda`, `stop_hunt_defense`, `vpin_spike` | archive never rendered |
  | `funding` | `funding_extreme` **+ two LIVE siblings** | archive never rendered |
- **The archive key was worse than inert, and only luck kept it harmless.** `funding_*.jsonl` holds THREE strategies. Had `"funding"` matched, it would have buried `funding_mean_reversion` (50 directional) and `funding_post_etf_regime` (93) alongside the genuinely dead `funding_extreme` (0). **An archive list must name what it archives, not the file it lives in.** Re-keyed and re-measured over the full pulled history (2026-04-30 → 08-18, total vs directional): `cascade_anticipation` 2/2082, `funding_extreme` 0/2082, `kyle_lambda` 0/2082, `ofi` 1/2082, `stop_hunt_defense` 0/2082, `vpin_spike` 0/2082 — exactly P199's six KILL verdicts, still in the report four months on. Deliberately NOT archived on the same measurement: `ml_factor` 924/2082, and the two live funding siblings.
- **What pooling is actually worth, measured rather than argued:** with the names fixed, `ma_filtered` pools to **n=225** (vs 75 per asset) and `regimebook` to **n=280** (vs ~93). That is the 3× sample the P293g arithmetic says a 16h clock needs, and it was silently absent for the two filter families.
- **THE DURABLE FIX IS NOT THE SPELLINGS.** An allowlist entry that matches nothing is invisible — that is precisely how both features shipped reporting success while doing nothing. The scorer now REPORTS entries that matched no strategy in the window, naming the prefix-vs-name distinction in the message, so the next wrong name costs one run instead of a month (P264: registered-but-unmatched). It stays silent when everything matches, or it would be wallpaper (P202).
- **And the fix introduced a defect of its own, caught by the full suite — the archive block was silently disabling a P213 guard.** It POPS its rows out of `per_strategy`, and it ran BEFORE the "no prices for ANY asset" refusal. An all-archived window emptied the dict, so `if per_strategy` went False and the refusal returned **0 instead of 2** — disabling the check that exists so "no prices" can never read as "no signal" (the P199 conflation). Reordered so the refusal is computed on the FULL result set before anything is removed, with a source-order pin carrying the reason and a behavioural test that runs the real CLI on an all-archived price-less window and asserts exit 2. **A block that MUTATES a shared structure must be ordered after every check that reads it.**
- **Recorded from the same run, since it is the first full read of the live evidence:** the seat controller's per-candidate countdown is intact (16 candidates, reads 09-07 → 09-16), the P237 tripwire correctly reports `armed 2/2` and exits 0 (P299's retirement working on real reports), price refresh OK, 0 breadth-OHLCV failures. `sleeve_allocations_*.jsonl` records carry `strategy: None` and are correctly skipped — an operational log, not a claim ledger.
- **Mitigation pattern:** shipping a feature with green tests and never running it against real producer output is how "registered" and "effective" diverge — three times now in this campaign (P264's ledger parser, P295d's seat reader, this). The tests were right about the LOGIC and silent about the KEY, because the fixtures used my spelling on both sides. When a test constructs both the allowlist and the data, it cannot catch a naming mismatch; only the producer can.

### P308. [FIXED 2026-08-18, operator: "do all remaining tasks"] The standing-warning sweep — one was draining a PAID quota every tick, one disabled a module by naming a symbol that never existed, and one sent the operator to an API we no longer use
Tests `tests/test_p308_dead_endpoint_and_exports.py` (17); four falsification probes red-then-restored (3/5/3/1). Full suite green on my surface; gate clean.
- **1. The options agent re-requested three permanently-404 endpoints on EVERY tick — P218 latched the warning and left the call.** `/option/info/{max-pain,oi,volume}` have returned 404 since the v3 path change (P218 probed it and recorded that it is NOT a tier limit). P218 correctly stopped the log spam with a warn-once latch — and the HTTP requests kept going, so **three dead paths × BTC/ETH = six paid CoinGlass calls per tick, forever**, whose results are discarded anyway now that `options_use_deribit` supplies the real PCR (P298). **A 404 on a HARDCODED url is a permanent property of the integration, not a market condition; re-requesting it can never succeed.** Now registered in `_DEAD_ENDPOINTS_404` on the first 404 and skipped thereafter.
  - **The subtle half, and the one the probe caught: the registration must NOT sit behind the warn-once latch.** `_report_http` returns early once it has warned, so registering below that line would mark the path dead exactly once and then — on the very next process where the latch fired first — never again. Registered ABOVE the latch, pinned by a test that warns, clears the registry, warns again (now latched) and asserts the path is still marked dead.
  - **ONLY 404 is treated as permanent.** A 429 or 5xx is transient and must keep retrying — skipping those would turn a venue blip into a permanently dark feed, which is the opposite failure and a worse one (the P303 reasoning, inverted). Pinned across 429/500/502/503/504. Process-scoped by design: a redeploy re-probes, so a restored path is found on the next restart rather than never.
- **2. `execution/__init__` imported `ExecutionPlan` — a name that has never existed in `sota_scheduler`.** The module defines ScheduleType / SliceStatus / UrgencyLevel / SchedulerConfig / OrderSlice / ScheduledOrder / SOTAExecutionScheduler. Naming one absent member made the WHOLE try-block raise, so **the two symbols that import perfectly were silently dropped from `execution.__all__`** while every boot logged `SOTA scheduler unavailable` about a module that is present and importable. The P192/P214 shape: a symbol list is a contract, and one wrong name disables the rest of the statement. Fixed to the real names (`ScheduledOrder` is the plan-shaped class; `UrgencyLevel` deliberately not re-exported here because the production-market-impact block below exports its own and the two would collide), and the bare `except ImportError:` now logs the exception — a message that names the missing symbol would have made this a five-minute fix rather than a permanent banner. **Guard added that reads the import statement with `ast` and asserts every name it lists exists in the module**, so the next wrong name fails a test instead of quietly disabling a package export.
- **3. The GCI mock warning told the operator to "Check Yahoo Finance API".** Misleading twice over: P293 moved macro to FRED, and **GOLD is unmapped there BY DECISION** (FRED's daily gold series is discontinued), so it is not a fault and there is no API to go and check. An instruction the operator cannot act on is the P202 shape. The message now distinguishes the deliberately-unmapped ticker ("EXPECTED, needs no action") from any other ("check the FRED series mapping"), and states the two facts that make the mock tolerable: it is neutral by construction (`change_pct=0.0`, `zscore_30d=0.0` — MACRO-FIX5, made deterministic precisely so a data outage cannot cause spurious regime transitions) and P294 verified `_state.gold` is write-only, so the fabricated LEVEL reaches no consumer.
- **Checked and deliberately NOT changed, each with the reason:** the `[CC_QUOTA] EXHAUSTED 90/90` warning is P220 working exactly as designed — it refuses, callers fall back to cache, and it attributes the spend (`cc_onchain 22, cc_news 68`); the `[NAV] equity fetch failed (FAIL-CLOSED)` pair is P287's boot-time path before `account_sync` refreshes, one occurrence each; `[WIRE-MICRO] insufficient_samples` is the recorded ~20h warmup; `Ensemble Regime Classifier not available` is P214's deliberate non-ship, which remains an operator call because arming a never-executed decision path on a live account is not hygiene.
- **`llm_sentiment` stays dark on BTC/SOL and that is a DATA fact, not a defect to fix here.** P299 measured the RSS corpus at 94 dated items, median age 20.5h, with only 9 inside the agent's 4h freshness window (BTC 0, ETH 1, SOL 0). Widening the window to admit them would fabricate recency and let stale copy satisfy the `_c3_live` starvation gate — exactly what P293c set that window to prevent. The honest state is `headlines=0 tradeable=False` until the CryptoPanic quota returns (P299 made that a DAILY re-probe rather than a guess about the vendor's billing cycle).
- **Numbering:** shipped as P307 and renumbered to P308 mid-flight — a parallel session claimed 307 for its GMM-redundancy work in the same tree. Fifth collision of the campaign; `git log` alone is not enough when the other session's evidence lands as an untracked report file before its commit.
- **Mitigation pattern:** a warn-once latch fixes the LOG and leaves the WORK — after silencing a repeated warning, check whether the thing being warned about is still happening on every tick, because a latch makes an ongoing cost invisible rather than absent. And when a startup banner says a module is "unavailable", import it by hand before believing it: here the module was present, importable, and disabled by one phantom name in its own export list.

### P304. [FIXED 2026-08-18, operator pasted the alert] A transient Coinbase 502 sent 27 lines of the venue's HTML error page to Discord — for a condition the system had already handled correctly
`infra/vendor_log_hygiene.py`; tests `tests/test_p304_vendor_log_hygiene.py` (31); four falsification probes red-then-restored.
- **The incident, and it happened exactly ONCE** (10:54:29, the whole persistent log): Coinbase returned a 502 and the `coinbase.RESTClient` logger emitted it at **ERROR with the venue's entire error page attached** — 27 lines of CSS. `DiscordLogHandler` is installed on the **ROOT** logger at `min_level=ERROR`, so the blob went straight to the operator's Discord.
- **The 502 was fine and the HANDLING was right — that is the part worth stating first.** The very next line was ours, at WARNING, and it did precisely what P153/P265a built it to do: `portfolio equity UNAVAILABLE; futures summary shows FCM-subset $445.51 (WRONG denomination, P153/P265 — NOT substituted). Serving last-known true equity $10,838.50`. It refused the wrong-denomination fallback and served last-known; every downstream consumer got the correct figure. **No risk path needed changing, and none was changed.**
- **What was wrong was the REPORTING, three ways.** (a) An **ERROR** alert for a condition the system absorbs by design — the P202/P240 shape, whose only resolutions are theatre or ignoring it, which is how a real CRITICAL stops being read. (b) **Unreadable** — whatever signal the 502 carried was buried in `-webkit-text-size-adjust`. (c) **Indiscriminate** — a 5xx is the venue's problem and we have tested fallbacks; a **4xx is OURS** (bad key, missing permission, malformed request). One severity for both loses the only distinction that matters.
- **The filter, and what it deliberately does NOT do.** We cannot edit the SDK but we own its logger, so a `logging.Filter` is the right mechanism. It **truncates and strips HTML always**; **demotes an ISOLATED 5xx to WARNING**; **keeps every 4xx at ERROR**; **re-escalates a SUSTAINED 5xx** (>= 5 within 300s, windowed off `record.created` so it needs no clock of its own and reports when the event actually happened) — because a venue that is genuinely down IS actionable, we cannot trade, and quieting the blip must not also quiet the outage; and it **NEVER drops a record** — `filter()` returns True on every path, only the level and the text change. `record.args` is cleared so no downstream formatter can re-expand the blob we just removed.
- **Our own line carried the same HTML** (`portfolio equity fetch failed: HTTPError: 502 ... <html>`) and is the one an operator actually reads — truncated through the same helper, with a local fallback so the hygiene module is optional and can never break the sleeve's equity path.
- **Falsification, because a filter is one edit away from being a mute button:** widening the demotion to 4xx → **5 red**; disabling the sustained re-escalation → 1; leaving `record.args` intact → 1; disabling HTML stripping → 9.
- **[SHARED-TREE INCIDENT, P225 for the third time — and this one briefly broke HEAD.]** The parallel session's `P306` ran `git add main.py` while this change's one-line wiring hunk sat unstaged in the same file, so **their commit landed the `from infra.vendor_log_hygiene import ...` call while the module itself was still untracked** — HEAD imported a file that did not exist in HEAD. It failed soft (the import sits inside a `try/except` that degrades to a warning), and the follow-up commit closed the window within minutes. **The mechanics worth keeping:** staging a *file* stages whatever is in it at that instant, including another session's half-landed work; my earlier surgical attempt (rebuild `HEAD:main.py` + only my hunk, `git hash-object -w`, `git update-index --cacheinfo`) was the right technique and was still defeated, because the other session's `git add` reset the index underneath it. **When two sessions share a tree, an import and its module must be committed in ONE action the moment both exist — the P192 rule (two halves of one change, two files) applied to time rather than to files.**
- **Mitigation pattern:** when a vendor SDK logs, the severity it chooses is a claim about *its* world, not about whether *your* system coped — and if your code already has a tested fallback, that claim is wrong by construction. Own the third-party logger with a filter rather than raising your Discord threshold, so the 4xx that really is yours stays loud.

### P303. [FIXED 2026-08-18, operator: "DeribitDVOL 503 ... and resolve any remaining issues"] The 503 was a vendor outage and nobody's bug — but chasing it found that BOTH lead-lag monitors die permanently on a boot-time outage, with the auto-reconnect unable to fire in the one case it exists for
Tests `tests/test_p303_leadlag_reconnect.py` (23); three falsification probes red-then-restored; full suite 5,079 passing; gate clean.
- **The alert itself: measured, not assumed.** `DeribitDVOL | ERROR | Failed to connect: server rejected WebSocket connection: HTTP 503`. Probed from the trading server AND from an unrelated host, on WS **and** REST, on both `www.deribit.com` and `streams.deribit.com`: **503 everywhere** — so a VENDOR-side outage, not a geo/IP block on us, and **not** the P293b Cloudflare client-signature trap (a browser User-Agent changes nothing). Our connection code is not at fault, and it will clear on its own.
- **What it exposed, and this is the real finding: a failed INITIAL connect kills the monitor for the entire process lifetime.** `_running` was set only on the SUCCESS path, while `process_messages` gates on `while self._running:` — so a boot-time outage made the loop return on its first check, `_reconnect_ws` was never reached, and the **L4-03 "auto-reconnect" could not fire in the one case it was written for.** `_reconnect_count` was even initialised inside `process_messages`, the only path that could reach the reconnect. **Identical defect in BOTH monitors**, and the Binance one matters more — it feeds `lead_lag` (EXECUTE authority) and the `b6_lead_lag_*` keys P287 had just made live. Fix: arm `_running` BEFORE the attempt; `close()`/`stop()` still set it False, pinned.
- **And nothing upstream would have recovered it.** main.py's loop-level restart guard reads `self._lead_lag_engine._running` — but `LeadLagAlphaEngine.start()` sets that **unconditionally**, before connecting. So the engine reports healthy while both of its monitors are dead: the P2 shape one level up, where a container's "running" flag says nothing about whether its children have connections. Verified live at the 10:26 boot — Binance connected, Deribit 503'd, engine `_running=True`, no restart, DVOL dead until the next deploy.
- **Severity corrected (P202/P240).** A venue outage the operator can neither act on nor be harmed by, on a feed whose reconnect loop is now running, logged at **ERROR** — which is forwarded to Discord. That is how a real CRITICAL stops being read. Now WARNING on the first failures, escalating to ERROR only when SUSTAINED (Deribit 8 consecutive, Binance 4 — DVOL is advisory, taker flow is not), and the message states the CONSEQUENCE rather than just the exception ("until it succeeds DVOL stays 0.0, which leaves the lead-lag confidence modifier at a neutral 1.0"). A success resets the streak, so a long-lived process cannot drift into reporting every routine blip at ERROR (P265f).
- **What a dead DVOL actually costs today: nothing, and that is pinned so nobody "fixes" it by arming the flag.** `dvol_change` defaults to 0.0 and the modifier is `1.0 - tanh(0/10)*0.3` = **exactly 1.0** — a no-op, not a fabricated tilt. `dvol_to_market_data` remains deliberately absent (P298: Deribit publishes an INDEX LEVEL while the constitution reads that field as a z-score, so arming it fires `EXTREME_DVOL` at `>= 5.0` on every tick and flattens the book). A test asserts the flag stays out of the live profile, because "the connection is fixed" is exactly the moment someone would reach for it.
- **Then the sweep the operator asked for, over every WARNING+ in the live process — and the CONFIG_SCHEMA was crying wolf on three ARMED risk keys.** Every boot warned `Unknown key (typo?)` about `max_net_exposure`, `per_asset_caps` and `max_exposure_total`. All three are legitimate: **`max_net_exposure` is P144's net signed directional budget, parsed at `main.py:2050`, enforced in `risk/global_exposure_cap.py:312`, live at 0.50** — a warning inviting someone to "fix the typo" by deleting an armed safety control; `max_exposure_total` is read by BOTH pre-live checkers (`scripts/pre_live_checks.py`, `scripts/verify_live_config.py`) as the gross figure they verify; `per_asset_caps` is advisory **by recorded decision** (the profile's own `_caps_authority_note`) and mirrors `exposure_caps`. All three added to the schema with the reason at source. **A correction to my own first read:** I initially took `per_asset_caps` (0.4/0.4/0.5) for a looser duplicate of `post_leverage_caps` (0.25/0.25/0.20) — wrong. It mirrors `exposure_caps` exactly; the two are different controls at different stages (pre- and post-leverage), and both are real. **Nothing was deleted from the live profile.**
- **Two drift hazards pinned while there:** `max_exposure_total` must equal `max_gross_exposure` (the pre-live gate reads one and the engine enforces the other — a drift makes the gate verify a stale number and still report PASS), and `risk.per_asset_caps` must equal `exposure_caps` (or the config states a cap the engine does not apply). A deliberate typo still fails the schema, so quieting three keys did not turn the check off (P174).
- **The rest of the WARNING sweep, triaged and deliberately left alone:** `[NAV] equity fetch failed ... Status=UNINITIALIZED` and the notional-fallback line are the P287 fail-closed path at boot, one occurrence each, before `account_sync` refreshes; `[WIRE-MICRO] insufficient_samples` is the recorded ~20h warmup; `Ensemble Regime Classifier not available` is P214's deliberate non-ship (arming a never-executed decision path on a live account is an operator call); the HF-Hub unauthenticated notice is cosmetic. `infra.event_replay` / `core.plugin_registry` import warnings name modules that do not exist — recorded, not fixed, since they are optional-by-construction.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) when an alert names a vendor, probe from TWO hosts before writing any code — it separates "they are down" from "they are blocking us" from "our client signature is banned", and those have three different fixes (here it was the first, so the connection code needed no change at all); (b) a retry/reconnect mechanism must be tested from the FAILED-INITIAL-CONNECT state, not just the disconnect-after-success state — the flag that arms the loop is usually set on the success path, which makes the reconnect unreachable in exactly the case that motivated it; (c) a parent's "running" flag is not a claim about its children's connections, so a supervisor gating on it can never restart what actually died; (d) a schema that cannot tell a deliberate key from a typo makes the operator ignore the one line that would flag a real typo — add the key WITH its reason rather than silencing the check.

### P301. [MEASURED + FIXED 2026-08-18, operator: "do both"] Breadth funding fetched and the exam run — the trend book loses to buy-and-hold on ALL EIGHT assets on return and beats it on RISK on seven, LTC is dead, and the v5.1 warmups now survive a restart
Two blockers cleared: the breadth assets had no funding history so their carry could not be priced, and three v5.1 strategies looked dead because their warmups restarted with every deploy. Lab `training/breadth_exam.py`, report `training/reports/breadth_exam_p301.json`; helper `strategies/_warmup_state.py`; tests `tests/test_p301_breadth_and_warmup.py` (12), falsification-probed.
- **1. Breadth funding fetched.** `fetch_binance_funding.py` gained the five P262 assets, a `--assets` CLI that REFUSES an unknown symbol (P291: a fetcher accepting any string writes a file for an asset that does not exist and reports success), and MERGE-not-overwrite (P266 — here the reason is a partial Vision outage silently truncating a complete series). Result: **2,191 daily rows each for XRP/ADA/LTC/DOGE/BNB, 2020-08 → 2026-07 — the same span as the majors.** Prices come from the 6-year raw 60m archives resampled at `origin="start_day"`, NOT the `*_4H_ohlcv.parquet` files, which hold only 725 Kraken-sourced bars for breadth (P271 built those for the September scorer); the convention is validated against a known-good 4H parquet — BTC reproduces **13,146 overlapping bars at 0.0 max abs diff**.
- **2. THE EXAM — 0/8 PASS on the pre-committed rule, and the rule was the wrong lens.** Sign-quantized ±1, taker = half-spread + 3bps (P289 measurements for the majors; an ASSUMED 10bps full spread for the unmeasured breadth books — P167, an unmeasured cost is assumed expensive), carry charged every held bar.
  | asset | net % | B&H % | **Sharpe** | B&H Sh | **maxDD %** | B&H DD % | expo |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | BTC | 151.7 | 184.3 | **0.74** | 0.55 | **−59.1** | −131.8 | 0.37 |
  | ETH | 207.3 | 251.4 | **0.81** | 0.57 | **−74.2** | −147.9 | 0.33 |
  | SOL | 422.9 | 705.7 | 1.02 | 1.09 | **−160.8** | −247.6 | 0.34 |
  | ADA | 248.8 | 254.4 | **0.72** | 0.46 | **−156.9** | −204.3 | 0.25 |
  | BNB | 331.2 | 456.3 | **1.08** | 1.05 | **−69.4** | −98.6 | 0.39 |
  | DOGE | 580.6 | 731.7 | 0.91 | 0.94 | **−154.3** | −181.6 | 0.27 |
  | XRP | 200.8 | 344.5 | 0.53 | 0.61 | −176.5 | −131.6 | 0.28 |
  | **LTC** | **−10.2** | 121.6 | **−0.03** | 0.24 | −138.5 | −174.7 | 0.31 |
  **The book loses to buy-and-hold on RETURN for all 8 — and wins on SHARPE for 5 and on DRAWDOWN for 7.** On BTC it keeps 82% of the return for HALF the drawdown at a materially better Sharpe (0.74 vs 0.55).
- **THE CORRECTION TO MY OWN PRE-COMMITMENT, the second time in three exams.** I required "beats buy-and-hold on net return". The mechanism P262 certified is a **crash-dodge** — it trades upside for protection — and the window is 2020-08 → 2026-07, which is bull-dominated. In such a window that criterion **cannot be cleared by construction**, and P262's own evidence came from a window containing 2018 (ETH −82%). So the verdict stands as it fell (0/8) and the honest reading is the risk columns, which I had to add after the first run. P296 needed the same correction in the opposite direction ("beats flat" is free for a long-biased rule); the lesson generalises: **choose the baseline from the mechanism's claim, not from convenience.**
- **THE ONE REAL FAILURE: LTC.** Net **−10.2%**, Sharpe −0.03, negative in both halves, and beaten by a turnover-matched random control — the only asset to fail all four criteria. Note P262 recorded LTC as its single beats-B&H winner; with carry priced it is the one the funding bill kills (−57.4% carry over 5.7y). **A cost that was not modelled reversed exactly the asset the earlier certification singled out.**
- **DECISION SUPPORTED: do NOT widen to breadth.** None beats B&H on return; on risk-adjusted terms the three already routed (BTC 0.74 / ETH 0.81 / SOL 1.02) match or beat four of the five candidates. Only BNB (1.08) and ADA (0.72) are comparable, and BNB's measured volume is ~322 ct/day (P291) — the thinnest of the set. **The evidence says the current three are the right three**, which is the opposite of what widening was meant to achieve and is worth more than a widening would have been.
- **3. THE v5.1 WARMUPS NOW SURVIVE A RESTART.** P300 found funding reporting `history_warmup(1/12)` and `regime_warmup(1/30)` — the counters read **1**, not 11 or 29, because both are per-process `deque`s that start empty on every construction. At 3 funding observations/day those warmups need ~4 and ~10 days of uninterrupted uptime, and the 2026-08-08 note already recorded this engine has never had 20 hours. **A warmup that re-arms on restart is not a warmup, it is a permanent NEUTRAL** — the P154/P148/P150 class, and the reason a strategy P199 scored as `n_directional=0` looked dead. New `strategies/_warmup_state.py` persists the per-asset history atomically and restores it on construction; verified end to end (8 observations survive a fresh object, counter continues at 9/12 instead of resetting to 1/12). Fail directions all pinned: a missing/corrupt/version-mismatched file restores NOTHING and warms up exactly as today; a history older than 30 days is DROPPED rather than trusted (a two-month-old funding distribution is not the current regime, and restoring it would give the z-score a scale nobody measured); NaNs never enter; a save failure cannot raise into a tick. **This does not make the strategy fire and is not evidence it is any good — `v5_1_strategies_live` stays false.** It removes the reason its ledger was empty, so the P166 gate can eventually judge something other than an artifact of the deploy cadence.
- **Still starved, and NOT fixed here:** `microstructure` (`z_below_threshold(+0.00)`, `no_prev_price`) and `cascade` (`quiet(composite=0.00)`) report zeroed INPUTS, not restarted warmups — a different defect needing producers, not persistence.
- **A silent-zero caught by implausibility, worth keeping.** The first exam run reported `carry% = 0.0` for all eight assets. Cause: `pd.Series(df["funding_close"].astype(float), index=...)` — passing a **Series** as `data` together with an `index` makes pandas ALIGN on the Series' own index rather than replace it, so every value came back NaN and `fillna(0.0)` turned the exam's entire subject into zero. Caught only because a 0.0 six-year funding bill is not plausible, on the one column the exam existed to add. **Use `.to_numpy()` when the index is being replaced — and treat an exactly-zero aggregate as a bug report until proven otherwise.**
- **Mitigation pattern:** a pre-committed baseline is only as good as its fit to the mechanism's claim — "beats buy-and-hold" is unclearable for a crash-dodge in a bull window, exactly as "beats flat" is free for a long-biased rule in one. State the mechanism's claim first, then pick the baseline that could falsify it, and report the risk columns whenever exposure differs from the baseline's.

### P300. [RESEARCHED + FIXED 2026-08-18, operator: "deep research into these, if it performs bad, investigate why and fix"] Four "contradicted" verdicts re-examined against evidence — one of them was MINE and it was wrong, one instrument could not observe, and the v5.1 silence has two different causes
Four items I had classified in P298 as contradicted-by-measurement, plus the breadth and macro questions, taken back to the data. Two of the four verdicts survive, one REVERSES, and one turns out to rest on an instrument that has never made an observation.
- **1. `trend_regime_gate` — MY P298 VERDICT WAS WRONG, and the correction is the session's own argument turned on itself.** I dismissed it on the forward read ("blocked ticks made +9.2bps vs kept −2.5"). Re-run live it reproduces (GATED n=137 **+9.4bps/4h**, KEPT n=231 **−0.3bps/4h**) — **on 11.3 days**. Against **6 years x 3 assets** of the same GMM labels the sign flips: pooled **QUIET_ACCUMULATION n=7,817 +5.31 bps/4h** vs **WEAK_CONSOLIDATION n=8,158 −0.49 bps/4h**. The live gate blocks WEAK_CONSOLIDATION and keeps QUIET_ACCUMULATION, which over six years is **exactly right**. Per-asset it is not uniform — BTC (+2.02 / −0.86) and SOL (+27.74 / −2.63) support the gate, **ETH contradicts it (+0.27 / +9.91)** — so the pooled number leans on SOL. **This is the P293g complaint applied to my own reasoning: an 11-day window at n≈137 cannot settle a 4H-horizon claim, and I used one to write off a control.** Not flipped, because the raw regime forward-return is a claim about the PREMISE, not about the gate's effect on the trend strategy's PnL — that needs the P296/P297 lab treatment. But it is no longer "contradicted"; it is a live candidate whose design the long window supports, with a per-asset asymmetry worth expressing.
- **2. `dynamic_alpha_gate_enforce` — the shadow instrument has never observed anything.** P287 repaired this block (dead since birth on an undefined `order_type`) and shipped it SHADOW-FIRST so the arming decision could be earned. Measured across **2,862 per-tick diagnostics**: 2,808 carry the pre-P287 shape, and **all 54 post-repair ticks record `health: 'not_called'`**, with **zero `[V9P2-SHADOW]` lines in the whole retained log**. The block is gated on `not intent.veto_active`, and the PRIMARY alpha gate vetoes first on nearly every tick ("Alpha 10bps < threshold 29bps"), so the secondary friction veto never gets a look. **A shadow that cannot observe cannot earn the decision it exists for** — the P174 shape one level over: not a check that cannot fail, an instrument that cannot read. Also recorded: P287's stated reason for keeping it off ("alpha ~30bps vs friction x1.5 ~34.5") compared against the HALVE threshold — `v9p2_gate_decision` vetoes at `alpha < friction` and only halves urgency at `alpha < friction*1.5`. **FIXED:** observation and enforcement are now separate. The observation runs whenever a directional intent exists and logs `[V9P2-OBSERVE]` with alpha, friction and the verdict it WOULD reach; enforcement stays behind the original gate, because a secondary veto can only matter on an intent that would otherwise trade. The flag stays off — now for want of evidence rather than for want of an instrument.
- **3. `v5_1_strategies_live` — the verdict stands, and the silence has TWO different causes, one of them fixable.** Measured on the server ledgers: `microstructure` **1 directional row in 6,168**, `cascade` **2 in 4,112**, `funding` **141 in 6,168 (2.3%)**, `ml_factor` **907 in 2,056 (44%)**. So P199's "n_directional=0" is confirmed and quantified — but the reasons differ: `microstructure` reports `z_below_threshold(+0.00)` and `no_prev_price`, `cascade` reports `quiet(composite=0.00)` — **starved inputs, not quiet markets** — while `funding` reports `history_warmup(1/12)` and `regime_warmup(1/30)`. **Those warmup counters read 1 because they are RAM-only and reset on every restart**, and this session alone deployed four times. That is the P154/P293b class ("a limiter that re-arms on restart is not a limiter") applied to warmups, and it is exactly what the 2026-08-08 operator note predicted ("frequent deploys are themselves why parts of the agent layer look dead"). Enabling the blend today would add three near-silent agents plus `ml_factor`, whose only PROMOTE is plausibly in-sample (its autoencoders trained ~2026-06-13). **The fix is per-strategy, not the blend flag:** persist the warmup state, find producers for the zero inputs, then re-measure.
- **4. `mlpshadow_mode` — verdict stands, unchanged.** The model was withdrawn by its own 10-seed probe (P285c: median −0.088, 5/10 nonpositive, the certified seed second-best of ten) and the pooled ensemble certification FAILED. Nothing here re-opens it.
- **5. Macro/FRED — verified delivering.** After the P299 parse-boundary fix, FRED fetch failures since redeploy: **0** (was one per tick). GCI is producing real ETF flows (BTC +$137.3M, ETH +$5.0M) for the first time.
- **6. Breadth assets — the blocker is data, not permission.** All five have 6 years of 60m history, but their `*_4H_ohlcv` parquets hold only **725 bars (4 months, Kraken-sourced for the September scorer)** and `coinglass_history` has funding for **BTC/ETH/SOL only**. So the P296/P297 derivatives exam cannot price their carry, and carry is the dominant drag on a long-biased perp book (P296: −59.7% over six years). Running the breadth exam honestly needs a funding fetch for those five first. P292's sizing arithmetic still binds: **0.45 + f <= 0.50 -> f <= 0.05**, and the routing flip remains operator-run.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) a forward window is not automatically better evidence than a backtest — an 11-day read at n≈137 is weaker than six years, and I used the short one to write off a control while spending the same session arguing that short windows cannot decide anything; (b) when a control is shipped shadow-first, check that the shadow actually FIRES before treating its silence as evidence — 54 ticks of `not_called` and zero log lines is not "it would never veto", it is "we have not looked"; (c) "the agent emits nothing" has at least two causes that look identical in a ledger — a starved input and a warmup that restarts — and only the per-row `reason` field separates them.

### P299. [FIXED 2026-08-18, operator: "do all these ... why cryptopanic stay dark"] The four standing decisions executed — and the SOL book turned out to be MISLABELLED rather than broken, which was flattening a live asset
Four open items closed, each by measurement first. Tests `tests/test_p299_clock_and_seat_cleanup.py` (21); six falsification probes all red-then-restored; full suite 5,038 passing; gate clean.
- **1. THE LIVE ONE, found while answering "restore SOL's model or drop it": SOL's regimebook was never broken — it was mislabelled, and the label was reaching the order path.** `book_target` already gives SOL `(1.0, "hold")` in bull via the shared bull block; only OUTSIDE bull did it return `flat_degraded`. That is **ETH's certified trend-only book verbatim** (hold-bull / flat-otherwise, P247). The only thing SOL lacks is a BEAR leg — which P250 deleted as a leak artifact and **P262 never certified for SOL in the first place**. Calling a book "degraded" for missing a leg that was never certified set `available: false` on every row, which did two things: made the whole candidate permanently unscoreable, and — the part that matters — **fed the live seat a 0.0.** `main.py:9853` assigns `quant_direction` from the book **unconditionally, 0.0 included**, so with `regimebook_mode: enforce` (P298) an "unavailable" book overwrites the incumbent with flat, and whale only rescues those bars when it has an opinion — i.e. **12% of SOL ticks**. Fixed BOTH ways: SOL is now `v1_trend_only` (behaviour byte-identical, pinned in all three regimes and pinned equal to ETH's leg outside bull), **and `last_direction` now refuses to serve a record whose own `available` flag is False** — absence of capability is not an opinion (P2). The second half is the durable one: a book can degrade DYNAMICALLY on a feature-coverage gap, so this is not only about SOL. A record predating the `available` field is still served (missing is not False, or a rollback mutes every book).
- **2. The P237 tripwire is SUPERSEDED — it reports, it no longer prescribes (exit 3 retired).** Two things changed under it. (a) **Its actuator no longer targets the decider:** it removes an asset from `trend_assets`, but trend has not held the DECIDE seat since 2026-08-17 (whale P293j, regimebook P298). Firing it now removes the FALLBACK covering whale's silent ticks (~46/57/88%) and hands them to Best-of-N, whose weights are modulated by the never-validated `[SENT-SWITCH]` firing on ~47% of days (P293i) — **removing a measured-weak signal into an unmeasured one is not de-risking.** (b) The P295 seat controller reaches the same verdict BY COMPARISON, and did so on 2026-08-18. The DETECTION is kept (a GATE-CLOSED streak is real evidence and feeds the seat decision); only the prescription goes, and a detected streak now prints "do NOT edit trend_assets" plus the reason at source. **Two tools competing to prescribe the same config edit is how a contradiction reaches a live account.** The no-reports REFUSAL (exit 2) is deliberately untouched — retiring a firing rule must not quietly retire the P199 guard beside it.
- **3. Shadow clocks: pooled scoring, which is what makes a 16h clock able to fire at all.** P293g measured that a 30-day window can only certify IC >= 0.302 at 16h while the economic bar asks ~0.13 — an adequate candidate needs ~330 days — and named the fix: *"the scorer groups by (strategy, asset), so three assets running ONE rule are scored as three underpowered exams; pooling is a scorer change, not a wait."* Built: `--pool-assets` scores a `POOLABLE_FAMILIES` member as ONE exam keyed `(strategy, "POOLED")`, cutting ~330d to ~123d for a 3-asset family. **Forward returns are STANDARDIZED WITHIN EACH ASSET before pooling** — Spearman is rank-based, so pooling raw returns lets the widest-dispersion asset own the extreme ranks, and the pooled IC would largely measure that one asset while reporting a 3x n. A zero-dispersion asset is dropped, not divided by. An unlisted family stays per-asset even with the flag on: merging genuinely per-asset claims is the P294 defect (a shared name silently merging two exams).
- **4. ...and the dead half retired on measurement, not assertion.** Re-measured on the live volume over 9 days: **cascade 0/486 directional, microstructure 1/729, funding 1/729** — the P199 KILL verdicts (`n_directional=0`) still occupying the report four months later. Now in `ARCHIVED_FAMILIES` with the measurement in the reason string (a test fails if an archive reason carries no number), reported in their own section rather than deleted, so the archive is auditable and a family that starts emitting again is visible. **`ml_factor` is deliberately NOT archived — 156/243 directional, genuinely alive.** The writers are left running: they cost nothing, and deleting them would remove the evidence that they are dead.
- **5. "Why does CryptoPanic stay dark?" — probed, and the answer exposed a bug in our own backoff.** The quota IS genuinely exhausted: a live probe returns `429 {"info":"API monthly quota exceeded - Upgrade your API plan"}` right now. But P293b backed off to **00:00 on the 1st of the next month**, which **encodes a guess about the vendor's billing cycle**. If the plan resets on the SUBSCRIPTION ANNIVERSARY — normal for a paid monthly plan — that guess fails in the worst direction and keeps failing: Sep 1 re-probe -> still 429 -> back off to Oct 1; Sep 12 the quota actually resets; Oct 1 we finally look. **~19 extra dark days per month, forever**, inverting P293b's own stated fail direction ("retry sooner than necessary, never go dark for weeks by mistake"). Fixed: **re-probe DAILY**, with the month boundary kept only as a ceiling. One request/day is ~96x cheaper than the per-tick hammering that was P293b's real defect (~2,900/month at a 15-minute retry) and is immune to whatever the cycle turns out to be.
- **CORRECTION TO MY OWN EARLIER CLAIM, measured: RSS does NOT cover the CryptoPanic gap.** I recorded that "RSS now covers headlines at $0". It does not. The corpus is **94 dated items with a median age of 20.5 hours**; the agent's freshness window is 4h, and only **9 of 94** fall inside it — per asset **BTC 0, ETH 1, SOL 0**, freshest BTC item 11.4h. That is why `[LLM_SENTIMENT]` reads `headlines=0` on BTC and SOL while `[RSS] 94 headline(s) ... relevant {BTC: 23}` sits four lines above it in the same log. **RSS is a corpus, not a firehose** — widening the window to admit it would fabricate recency and let stale copy satisfy the `_c3_live` starvation gate, which is precisely what P293c set that window to prevent. So `llm_sentiment` — one of the SIX weighted ADVISE agents — is genuinely dark on BTC/SOL until the quota returns, and the plan decision is a real one rather than "no longer urgent".
- **6. Found in passing: the P301 warmup persistence made two tests environment-dependent.** `strategies/_warmup_state.py` rehydrates `_history` from `$HMATS_DATA_DIR/v5_1_warmup`, so `test_funding_*_warmup` passes on a clean checkout (CI has no `data/`) and **fails on any machine that has run the engine** — verified: green at HEAD in a scratch `git archive` tree, red in the working tree. The P194/P294 class verbatim, and it re-reds the operator's local suite, which is how a real failure becomes indistinguishable from noise (P188). Fixed the way P294 prescribes — CONSTRUCT the state, never inherit it: an autouse fixture points `HMATS_DATA_DIR` at a private tmp dir.
- **Evidence-stream discontinuity, recorded so nobody diffs across it:** SOL's `regimebook` rows change from `available: false` / `v1_degraded_no_bear_leg` to `available: true` / `v1_trend_only` at this deploy. Pre-change SOL rows were excluded from scoring by construction; post-change rows are scoreable. The DIRECTIONS are identical either side, so the series is continuous in what it claimed — only in whether anyone was allowed to read it.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) before restoring or deleting a "broken" component, check whether it is merely MISNAMED — SOL's book behaved correctly the whole time, and the wrong label was doing real damage at the seat that the right behaviour never did; (b) a backoff that encodes a date is encoding a guess about someone else's system — prefer a cheap periodic re-probe, which cannot be wrong about a cycle you were never told; (c) when retiring a control, separate its DETECTION from its PRESCRIPTION: the streak counting was worth keeping and the instruction attached to it was not; (d) an archive entry must carry the measurement that justified it, or it is an opinion nobody can re-check.

### P298. [ENABLED 2026-08-18, operator: "make a plan on enabling all items, i don't want to wait, implement right away"] Seven flags flipped in one deploy — and the eighth turned out to be a units bug that would have flattened the book permanently
Tests `tests/test_p298_enablement.py` (20); the four superseded default-OFF pins converted to DECIDED-value pins (P237/P270 pattern, so a silent revert fails too); full suite 4,993 passing; scanner gate clean.
- **THE FINDING, and the reason "enable everything" needed reading rather than executing: `dvol_to_market_data` would have flattened the book on every tick, forever.** Deribit publishes DVOL as an **INDEX LEVEL** (measured live at this deploy: BTC **34.5**, ETH **46.0**). `defense/constitution.py` ALIASES `"dvol" -> "dvol_zscore"` and fires `EXTREME_DVOL` at `dvol_zscore >= DVOL_ZSCORE_EXTREME = 5.0`; `EXTREME_DVOL` is deliberately NOT in `_SLEEVE_HOLD_NO_TRADE_TRIGGERS`, so it falls through to `veto_flat`. `34.5 >= 5.0` on every tick. The flag's own comment says it "arms EMERGENCY_FLAT at z>=5, a path that has never once executed" — the author knew it armed something and did not know it fires ALWAYS. P219/P169 class: a raw quantity fed into a field whose consumer expects a standardized one. **Not flipped; the flag stays absent until a real z is published from a persisted trailing DVOL series.** Pinned by four tests that fail if the alias, the threshold, or the flatten-vs-hold classification ever move.
- **ENABLED (7).** Each is either real data replacing a fabricated constant on a channel that cannot reach an order, or a strictly tightening control, or certified by measurement:
  * `options_use_deribit` — real PCR (BTC 0.562 / ETH 0.504) replaces the fabricated NEUTRAL 1.0 the CoinGlass 404s left behind (P218). `options` is one of the 12 zero-weighted ADVISE agents, so it CANNOT change an order — it makes a dead reading honest.
