  * `exchange_netflow_to_flow_agent` — real netflow (BTC +4,050 coins/1d across 21 exchanges; ETH −15,016 across 20) replaces a structural 0.0 that has pinned `flow_direction` for the life of the system. Order impact today is nil (ADVISE modifies `base_exposure`, discarded twice).
  * `sentiment_zscore_mode: "historical"` — on P296 evidence: same Sharpe as linear (0.91 vs 0.92) on **33% less exposure**, therefore **less than half the funding carry** (−23.9% vs −49.5% over 6y). Chosen for its carry profile, not for edge — neither reading beat buy-and-hold.
  * `coinbase_whale_filter_enforce` and `coinbase_ma_filter_enforce` — both tightening-only entry vetoes on the sleeve driver, sharing one decision function (P172). Both fail OPEN on a silent agent, never filter exits, never force-exit an aligned position. The MA filter carries the strongest live evidence in the batch (P236 counterfactual: quant +24.9bps/tick when model_alpha agrees, **−78.9bps/tick when it disagrees, t=−3.42**).
  * `macro_gci_live` — `update_async()` never had a caller, so `get_macro_signal()` returned its INITIAL DEFAULT for the life of the system. Its indicators are now real FRED series. **Honest caveat: the leverage-cap map is TWO-DIRECTIONAL** (RISK_ON 0.7 tightens, RISK_OFF 1.2 and CRISIS 1.5 LOOSEN) — this is not a one-way safety fix.
  * `fusion_conviction_to_sleeve` — the ONLY channel by which the 22 non-DECIDE agents can reach an order. De-risking only: it can reduce magnitude, never increase it, never changes a sign, and an unusable ratio leaves size UNCHANGED rather than 0.
  * `regimebook_mode: "enforce"` — the best-evidenced change in the batch, on P297: the BTC book measured over 6 years in the derivatives expression at +243.9% vs trend-only +151.7% vs buy-and-hold +184.5%, funding-leg increment positive in ALL THREE eras, beating a same-cell random control by ~286 points, and the only Sharpe CI in that comparison excluding zero. The flag is GLOBAL and that is correct: `book_target` gives BTC the full book, ETH trend-only, SOL hold-bull-only — exactly the P247/P250 measured roster.
- **SEAT PRECEDENCE, made explicit instead of accidental.** The whale seat runs LAST and therefore wins by default — a deliberate P293d choice made when it was the only enforced seat. With the book enforced that default is backwards on evidence: the book is certified over 6 years and three eras; whale's own instrument puts it at 16h **t=0.26**. So whale now **defers to a DIRECTIONAL book target and seats only where the book is FLAT**, which preserves the P293j intent (whale trades where the incumbent could not) without letting the weaker signal override the stronger. A FLAT book is deliberately not a claim on the seat. The marker is cleared at the top of every tick beside the whale stash, because a stale "the book took the seat" would mute whale on a tick the book never ran (P155-L5).
- **NOT ENABLED (4), because measurement contradicts them — recorded so a later "enable everything" has to argue with the evidence rather than quietly include them:** `mlpshadow_mode` (the model was WITHDRAWN by its own 10-seed probe, P285c — median −0.088, 5/10 nonpositive — and its checker refuses permanently); `v5_1_strategies_live` (P199: 6 of 9 strategies scored KILL with `n_directional = 0`, i.e. they emit nothing at all); `trend_regime_gate: enforce` (forward evidence INVERTED — the ticks it would block made **+9.2bps/4h** while the ones it keeps made −2.5); `dynamic_alpha_gate_enforce` (P287 measured enforcement would flatten the standing BTC long, alpha ~30bps vs friction×1.5 ≈ 34.5bps).
- **Also not enabled, and it needs the operator's own command:** the five P262-certified breadth assets (XRP/ADA/LTC/DOGE/BNB) are fully expressible since P292 but require `config.assets` + sizing fractions moved together AND `scripts/coinbase_set_assets.sh`, which is operator-run by design (P141 blocks the agent from live routing changes). P292's arithmetic still binds: **0.45 + f <= 0.50 -> f <= 0.05.**
- **A config trap worth keeping: JSON last-key-wins silently ate the first flip.** Inserting `"regimebook_mode": "enforce"` near the top left an older `"regimebook_mode": "off"` further down, and `json.load` took the LAST one — the flag read back as `off` while the file plainly contained `enforce`. Caught only by reading the parsed value back rather than trusting the edit. Same for a duplicated `_regimebook_mode_note`. **Always re-read a config through the parser after editing it; a duplicate key is invisible in a diff and decisive at load.**
- **Mitigation pattern:** "enable everything" is a request to read what each flag ARMS, not to flip each one — and the reading is where the value is. Seven were safe or certified, four are contradicted by measurements already in this file, and one would have stopped the book trading entirely because a raw index level meets a threshold written for a z-score. A flag name is not a specification; the consumer's units are.

### P297. [MEASURED 2026-08-18] The BTC funding legs settled on 6 years of funding history — P262's "thin-and-negative" read REVERSES, and the criterion that failed them also rejects buy-and-hold
The one live-money component P262 explicitly could not certify: *"the BTC funding legs — the entire excess of +33.6% over trend-only's +16.6% — remain uncertified"*, on ~9 usable months of virgin-era funding. The data to settle it (2,191 daily rows, 2020-08 → 2026-08) had been on disk the whole time. Lab: `training/funding_legs_lab.py`; report `training/reports/funding_legs_lab_p297_BTC.json`. Same discipline as P296: verdict pre-committed before the run, `regime_label`/`causal_funding_z`/`book_target` IMPORTED from `defense/regime_book_shadow.py` (P172 — the lab cannot test a book that differs from the deployed one), derivatives expression (sign-quantized ±1, half the P289 CDE spread + 3bps, P245 carry on every held bar), funding z from strictly COMPLETED days (the P247-F1 leak).
- **What is under test is the INCREMENT, not the book.** Book and trend-only agree on every BULL bar, so comparing totals mostly compares the trend mechanism to itself; the funding legs live only in bear/peace, differing on **3,220 of 12,676 bars (25.4%)**. Scoring `book − trend_only` bar by bar isolates their marginal PnL (the P259 overlay framing) — and dictates the decisive control: **random signs in the SAME cells with the same turnover.**
- **RESULT (BTC, 6.0y, additive per-bar sums):**
  | series | net % | Sharpe | CI90 | |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | book (trend + funding legs) | +243.90 | 0.94 | [+0.18,+1.59] | **excludes zero** |
  | trend_only (P262-certified) | +151.66 | 0.74 | [−0.11,+1.41] | includes zero |
  | buy_and_hold | +184.46 | 0.55 | [−0.21,+1.23] | includes zero |
  | **INCREMENT (funding legs)** | **+92.24** | 0.59 | [−0.10,+1.28] | includes zero |
  | increment: random same-cell | −194.03 | −1.52 | [−2.21,−0.86] | excludes zero |
  Era increments: pre-design **+16.87%**, design **+43.68%**, validation **+29.03%** — positive in all three, including the two the legs were not selected on.
- **VERDICT AS IT FELL: FAIL**, on exactly one of four pre-committed criteria — the increment's Sharpe CI includes zero. The other three pass: net > 0, positive in design AND non-design eras, and it beats the same-cell random control by ~286 points.
- **THE FINDING IS THAT THE FAILING CRITERION DOES NOT DISCRIMINATE.** The same test rejects `trend_only` — the certified mechanism the FAIL's own prescription falls back to — and rejects **buy_and_hold**, i.e. owning BTC for six years. That is established by the BASELINES, independently of the funding result, so it is not a post-hoc rescue: a criterion that rejects buy-and-hold cannot single out one component. **The prescription attached to FAIL ("the book should be trend-only") would remove the component that makes the book significant and fall back to one that is not** — the book's CI excludes zero ONLY with the legs in it. That is coherent statistically (the legs add return at ~zero net carry and modest incremental cost, raising the Sharpe of the combination) and incoherent as an instruction. **So the legs are NOT removed; what failed is criterion 3.**
- **P242's finding recurring at 12,676 bars.** P242 measured that no strategy including the baselines certifies at ~1,900 bars; this is the same result with 6.7× the data. A |t|≥2 bar on a single asset's 6-year Sharpe of 0.5–0.9 is not a filter, it is a rejection of everything — and the honest response is to name the criterion, not to keep re-running candidates against it.
- **What DOES discriminate here, and should be the standard for overlay components:** (a) the **same-cell random control** — it fails at −194% against the legs' +92%, a ~286-point separation, so the funding signal is doing real work rather than riding the regime mask it sits inside; (b) **era stability across three eras**, two of them unselected. Both are properties a coin flip cannot fake, and neither depends on a t-statistic the sample cannot support.
- **P262's caveat is answered, in the opposite direction to its expectation.** Its virgin-era read had ~9 usable months of funding and saw the legs subtract in 2020; six full years say they add in every era measured. That is not a contradiction — it is what happens when a thin window is replaced by a thick one, and it is the same lesson as P296: the wait was never the constraint, the data already existed.
- **Recorded, NOT claimed:** returns are additive per-bar sums, not compounded; the recorded P247 validation figures (book +33.6% vs trend-only +16.6%) are not reproduced exactly — this harness gives +51.5% vs +22.5% under a different cost model and carry treatment — but the RATIO is close (2.29× vs 2.02×), which is the sanity check that matters; carry on the increment nets to ~0 because the legs run 1,618 contrarian-long bars against 1,602 short ones; and this settles BTC only — ETH's book is trend-only by measurement and SOL's bear leg is parity-gated off.
- **Mitigation pattern:** when a pre-committed criterion produces a FAIL, check what else that criterion rejects BEFORE acting on it — if it also rejects the fallback and the do-nothing baseline, the criterion is the finding. Score the baselines every time for exactly this reason (P182), and prefer criteria a coin flip cannot fake (a same-cell control, era stability) over significance bars the sample size cannot support.

### P296. [MEASURED 2026-08-18, operator: "we don't have time to wait 330 days, can we leverage the backtesting"] The sentiment exam settled on 3,116 days of history instead of a 30-day clock — the contrarian reading is decisively NEGATIVE, and both momentum readings are indistinguishable from being long
Operator rejected the P293g wait and asked whether backtesting could substitute. It can, for this candidate, because the three F&G variants have **ZERO FREE PARAMETERS** — every threshold is imported verbatim from the live code (`defense/sentiment_variant_shadow.py`, P172 single-source), so there is no search for a search to overfit and multiplicity is 3, not thousands. That is the structural difference from the campaigns that produced false positives (P164 leak, P243/P244 era-collapse, P259b, P281, P283b→P285c): those FIT on the window they then read; this one only READS. Lab: `training/sentiment_history_lab.py`; report `training/reports/sentiment_history_lab_p295.json`.
- **Why the wait was never necessary here.** P293g's ~330-day figure is real but is a consequence of three unstated CHOICES, not a law: 16h horizon × one asset at a time × IC-as-the-statistic. Measured: to certify the economic-bar IC at |t|>=2 needs **370d at 16h/1 asset, 123d pooled over 3, 46d over 8, and 27d at 4h** (9d pooled). The scorer groups by `(strategy, asset)`, so three assets running ONE rule are scored as three underpowered exams instead of one — pooling is a scorer change, not a wait. And for any candidate whose inputs HAVE history the question is settled offline: F&G serves 3,116 days back to 2018-02-01 for free.
- **DERIVATIVES EXPRESSION (operator: "we focus on derivatives now"), the P283b decisive cell:** sign-quantized ±1 (the sleeve sizes by sign and discards magnitude, P273/P293d, so a continuous claim would measure a strategy the system cannot express); taker = HALF the measured CDE spread (P289) + 3bps fee, i.e. BTC 4.00 / ETH 5.75 / SOL 5.00 bps per leg; **funding carry charged every held bar** (P245 convention, Binance proxy with the P218 sign caveat). Causality: the value stamped day D drives bars from D+1 00:00 UTC only.
- **RESULT (6.0y, BTC+ETH+SOL equal-weight, additive per-bar return sums):**
  | variant | net % | Sharpe | CI90 | expo | carry % |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | sent_momentum_linear (the LIVE rule) | +363.8 | 0.92 | [+0.26,+1.54] | 0.66 | −49.5 |
  | sent_momentum_hist | +305.9 | 0.91 | [+0.31,+1.47] | 0.44 | −23.9 |
  | **buy_and_hold** | **+393.4** | **0.87** | **[+0.20,+1.55]** | 1.00 | −59.7 |
  | sent_contrarian | **−427.2** | **−1.20** | [−1.90,−0.49] | 0.63 | +53.0 |
  | random, turnover-matched | −217.7 | −0.56 | [−1.26,+0.14] | 0.73 | −13.2 |
- **THE CORRECTION TO MY OWN PRE-COMMITMENT, recorded because it is the load-bearing part.** The pre-committed rule required "beats FLAT", and by that letter both momentum variants PASS. **Flat is nearly free for a long-biased rule in a 2020-2026 bull market, and I had not pre-committed buy-and-hold — the exact baseline P182 added to the DRL trainer after "under-performs buy-and-hold" turned out not to be an outcome that harness could produce.** Added after the first run and reported as context rather than used to retroactively move a PASS to a FAIL (moving a goalpost after seeing a number is the selection sin this whole file exists to prevent). With it: both momentum variants return LESS than being long, and their Sharpe edge sits entirely inside overlapping CIs. **Honest verdict: the sentiment momentum rule is not distinguishable from a partial-exposure long.**
- **What it DOES settle, none of which the 30-day ledger could have:** (a) **the contrarian reading is decisively wrong** — Sharpe −1.20 with a CI excluding zero on the LOSING side. That is `signals/deterministic_sentiment.py`, the module this file lists as "Sentiment L1 (F&G) ACTIVE", and its output never becoming `sentiment_direction` was fortunate rather than designed (P293d recorded the sign disagreement; this is its verdict); (b) **`sentiment_zscore_mode` is roughly performance-neutral** — historical matches linear's Sharpe on 33% LESS exposure, so P293's "the live z-score is a miscalibrated linear rescale" is true as a DESCRIPTION and does not translate into edge; (c) **the derivatives finding: carry is the dominant drag.** Long perps cost −59.7% over six years vs −23.9% for the low-exposure variant. In perps, cutting exposure is worth far more than in spot because it stops the funding bleed — which reframes what a sentiment overlay is FOR: not direction, but when to be flat.
- **Anti-vacuity (P174), and the first control was the wrong one.** A naive random-sign control flips on ~2/3 of days, takes 1,485 flips/asset and a 96% cost drag — it fails, but for churn, testing a question nobody asked. Replaced with a TURNOVER-MATCHED random control (same flip rate as the live rule, random signs): −217.7%, CI includes zero, FAIL. The harness has teeth for the right reason.
- **Recorded, NOT claimed:** returns are additive per-bar sums, not compounded; the 2018-02→2020-08 F&G era is a genuinely virgin window (it predates every parquet) and is NOT yet tested because local price history starts 2020-08 — fetching it is the natural next step and the strongest available form (the P262 protocol); and this settles the SENTIMENT family only.
- **The triage this produces, which is the reusable output.** Of the 22 shadow families accruing, roughly 8 have inputs with real history and can be settled offline the same way — `sentvariant` (done), `xsmom`, `donchian`, `emaens`, the three `regimebook` variants, `eventfilter`, `etfflow` (with its reporting-lag caveat). The rest are structurally forward-only because their inputs do not exist historically: `derivflow`/`oidiv_*`/exchange-netflow (CoinGlass ~180d rolling), `calbasis` (CDE-native), and `ma_filter`/`whale_filter`/`mlpshadow` (they judge AGENT OUTPUTS, which were never stored). Forward-only is a data fact, not a clock choice — and that distinction is what tells you which waits are real.
- **Mitigation pattern:** when a promotion clock cannot fire, ask first whether the candidate needs FORWARD data at all — a rule with no fitted parameters can be judged on history the moment the history exists, and the objection that killed every previous backtest here (selection on a re-read window) simply does not apply to it. And whatever the pre-committed rule says, a long-biased strategy in a bull market must be scored against buy-and-hold: "beats flat" is a bar that being long clears by accident.

### P294. [FIXED 2026-08-17] The read-through of the uncommitted P293 batch — a shadow ledger that wrote into a running exam, a vacuous guard, a stash whose comment described code that was not there, and a local suite that had been red on the operator's own machine
Operator: "can you read through the codebase", then "fix all". The whole P293 series (a-g, ~2,166 insertions, 6 new modules) was sitting UNCOMMITTED and had never been through CI, so the read started there and worked outward. Every load-bearing claim was re-verified at source rather than from its comment (P228). Tests: `tests/test_p294_readthrough_fixes.py` (10) + extensions to the P293d/P293e suites; every fix falsification-probed red-then-restored.
- **1. The whale-filter ledger wrote into the ma_filter EXAM.** `build_whale_filter_shadow_harness` reused `MAFilterEchoStrategy`, whose `strategy_name` was the hardcoded `"ma_filtered"`, and `compute_per_strategy_ic` groups by the record's **`strategy` field — not by filename or ledger prefix** (`strat = r.get("strategy")`). Confirmed by running both harnesses: same group key. This is not merely "whale is unmeasurable": the ma_filter exam has a live config decision attached (`coinbase_ma_filter_enforce` on PASS, ~09-07 read), so rows from a different filter would have contaminated it, and the inflated `n` would have LOOSENED the gate's own `|t| >= 2` on a meaningless merge. `strategy_name` is now a constructor argument (default unchanged, so every existing P236 row keeps its meaning); whale writes `"whale_filtered"`. **Caught before any contamination happened** — the batch was never deployed, so no server ledger carries a pooled row. The sibling `sentiment_variant_shadow` in the same batch did this correctly with three distinct names, which is what made the asymmetry visible.
- **2. The guard on that harness could not fail.** `assert getattr(h, "log_prefix", None) == "whale_filter" or True` — the attribute is `_log_prefix`, so the comparison was ALREADY False and only the trailing `or True` kept it green. P174, inside the test written to guard the new ledger. Rewritten to assert the real attribute; a second test pins the PREMISE (that the scorer groups by the strategy field), so if that ever changes the distinctness requirement is re-derived rather than left implicit in a comment.
- **3. The type gate is red locally — and MOST of it is not this batch's, which is a correction to my own first reading.** The gate exits 1 with **+20 mypy findings**, and the obvious conclusion ("the batch is CI-red, P287g again") was wrong. Attributed the P175 way instead of asserted: a scratch checkout of HEAD (`git archive HEAD | tar -x`) scanned with the same mypy on the same dirs gives **1,073 errors, and the post-fix working tree gives 1,073 — the error sets are IDENTICAL, `comm` reports no difference in either direction.** So the residual delta is the **P227 environment fingerprint** between this machine and whatever stamped the baseline (the baseline even carries `str`/`int`/`float` "codes", which are not mypy error codes at all — its parser captures a trailing `list[str]` from the message text, so a per-code diff against it is not apples-to-apples). It must NOT be re-baselined (P175), and P253b is exactly why it does not gate deploys: the type gate's verdict lives in CI. **Three findings WERE genuinely the batch's** and are fixed at source, never baselined (P284b/P287g): two `var-annotated` on the GCI flow lists whose inference the new early-return broke, plus the whale-stash re-annotation (`no-redef`) and the whale-seat latch. The `rss_news_feed` Optional-valued header dict and `fear_greed_history`'s `float(None)`/`int(None)` coercions were fixed in the same pass — outside the gate's scanned dirs, so they never showed in its count, which is its own reason to run mypy wider than `CRITICAL_DIRS` when judging a data-layer batch.
  - **The gate could not run here at all, and that is why nobody had noticed.** `ci_check_invariants._run_scanner` uses `subprocess.run(text=True)`, so on this machine it died in a reader thread with the same GBK decode error **before executing a single scanner** — at HEAD as well. A gate that crashes before it starts and one that passes are equally silent about the code.
- **4. The whale stash had NO reset, and its comment said it did.** The comment claimed "Reset-then-set on EVERY tick, before any conditional write"; the code was set-only, at the whale bridge ~4,000 lines into the tick. Any tick returning before that line — P253's crash and disconnect returns, a prefetch failure, an exception anywhere earlier — left the PREVIOUS tick's whale direction, and the sleeve driver (which runs later in `run_live` regardless of how the tick ended) would filter an entry on a stale opinion and write that stale claim into the ledger. The MA filter it mirrors resets to 0.0 at `:9942` before its guarded block; whale now resets at the TOP of the tick. P155-L5, and the same defect P287 fixed for the eventfilter gated-dir stash — re-introduced in the same session that recorded it.
- **5. P293h existed in the code and NOWHERE in this file.** It changes the sleeve PnL definition (external capital flows subtracted), adds a persisted state field, and its tests live in the P293e file — a Rule 7 gap on a live-money MEASUREMENT. Recorded now, with two real gaps closed: (a) **`_last_equity_for_flow` was RAM-only**, so a fresh process always took the `prev is None` early return and a transfer landing while the engine was DOWN was invisible — which is the NORMAL sequence, since the wire is a manual operator step (P274) and deploys are frequent. It is now persisted (with its timestamp) and the tolerance scales with the elapsed gap, capped, because a multi-day outage accumulates more honest mark-to-market than one 4H tick; widening is the conservative direction, since the failure it guards against is silently reclassifying real PnL as a transfer. (b) **The 2026-08-16 deposit is NOT backfilled and cannot be** — `external_flow_usd` is still 0.0, so the ledger keeps reporting the inflated figure (measured +$6,850/+171% against actual trading PnL of -$225/-6.3%) until an operator sets it. **OPERATOR STEP, deliberately not automated:** only they know the true transferred amount, and inventing one is the fabrication this whole mechanism exists to end — set `external_flow_usd` in `data/coinbase_sleeve_state.json` to the deposit size with the engine stopped.
- **6. The local suite was red on the operator's own machine, and had been.** `pytest tests/` gave **45 failed + 14 errors**; the same tree under `-X utf8` gave **4,895 passed / 0 failed**. Every one was `UnicodeDecodeError: 'gbk' codec` — files read with the ambient Windows locale codec because no `encoding=` was passed. It reached the guards for P164 (the wavelet leak), P232, P285, P287, P291, P230 and P213. **This is P194's lesson inverted**: CI (Linux, UTF-8) was green while the operator's box was red, so the local suite carried no signal and a real failure would have been indistinguishable from the noise — the same way P188's red-on-main taught everyone to stop reading it. There is no `pytest.ini` or `pyproject.toml` in this repo to pin an interpreter flag, and a flag only fixes the machines that remember it, so the fix is the one P171 applied to the scanners: **state the encoding at the read site** (38 call sites across 26 files, including 3 production `read_text()` sites in main.py). A new AST-based guard keeps them out, with an anti-vacuity test (P174) and a false-positive test.
  - **Fixing the reads left 24 failures, because the trap has a SECOND half.** `subprocess.run(..., text=True)` decodes the child's output with `locale.getpreferredencoding()`, so a script that prints an em-dash hands the parent `stderr=None` — and the tests that assert a tool "REFUSES loudly" are exactly the ones that spawn it. 52 call sites across 32 files, including `tools/ci_check_invariants.py` itself (see item 3) and five in `scripts/live_watchdog.py`. Both halves are now covered by the same guard.
  - **The mechanical pass made the mistake its own guard now forbids, which is why the guard is AST-based.** A first regex pass rewrote `zf.open(...)` and `archive.open(...)` — zipfile/tarfile MEMBERS, which are binary and raise `ValueError` on `encoding=` — and one of the three was `data_mgmt/market_data_pipeline.py:483`, **on the live data path**. It also injected the kwarg inside a string literal, and silently missed every call containing nested parentheses (`write_text(json.dumps(x))`), which is why the first "fixed" suite still had 31 failures. Caught by compiling every file and by the guard itself; the regex pass was replaced with an AST rewrite keyed on `ast.Name` receivers only.
- **7. Minor, same batch:** the feed-degradation counter's `>=2`/`>=3` thresholds were calibrated when every member of that `gather` fed a live signal, and P293 added six feeds whose consumers sit behind default-OFF flags — so a Deribit + LunarCrush hiccup could raise a Discord **CRITICAL** about "data feeds failed" that the operator can neither act on nor be harmed by (the P202/P240 shape; note `_multi_feed_degradation` has no consumer, so the alert is the entire effect). The roster is now split, with GCI classified by its FLAG rather than its name, and advisory failures still reported at WARNING. `ExchangeNetflowFeed._fetch_real` replaced the whole snapshot, so one transient failure on ETH dropped that symbol AND stamped the fetch time, suppressing any retry for the full hour — the per-family-carry corner P265f/P287 closed for CoinGlass, reopened in a new feed; carried records keep their ORIGINAL timestamp, because carrying is not re-measuring. Its mock-mode zeros return `is_usable() == True` with no provenance, so the metrics object now carries `mock`.
- **Verified correct and deliberately left alone** (each was a plausible defect worth the check): the P293b `flow_direction` sign inversion is right — confirmed against both the producer (`onchain_feed:609`) and the sibling consumer (`flow_signal_integrator.py:241`), which already used the corrected convention; the Deribit PCR z-score history is not contaminated by the dead 404 path (the native site guards `if pcr_oi != 1.0`); the historical z-score does reach its consumer (`main.py:8135` copies it into `agent_signals` before the `abs(z)>1.0` read); the two conviction implementations are twins by design with a parity test that reaches the refusal branch; `_state.gold` is write-only, so the unmapped-GOLD mock fallback is inert.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) when a ledger family is reused, the thing that separates two claims is whatever the SCORER groups by — read its arithmetic before assuming a filename or a prefix is enough (the P236-followup lesson one level up: a field the scorer keys on is part of the claim, not metadata); (b) a comment describing a safety property is a claim to verify, not documentation to trust — "reset-then-set on every tick" was written above code that only ever set; (c) a mechanical fix over 100+ files needs a semantic parser AND a compile-everything check after every pass — the two shapes a regex cannot see (a receiver that is not the builtin, and nested parentheses) are exactly the ones that break the live path and hide the remainder, and the AST rewrite that replaced it then broke 8 files of its own by inserting an argument after a TRAILING COMMA. Three passes, three different failure modes, each caught only by compiling the whole tree; (d) when the local suite is red for environmental reasons, fix the environment dependence in the CODE: a documented interpreter flag is a checklist, and checklists are what P280 replaced with mechanisms.

### P316. [INVESTIGATED + FIXED 2026-08-18, operator: "can you investigate the reason behind"] Four items I had called time-bound — three of the four waits were a choice or a bug, and the cascade window I armed this morning is DISARMED on better data
Asked why four accrual items were stuck, I checked each instead of restating them. The P296 question — *does this candidate need forward data at all?* — answers three of the four. Tests `tests/test_p316_buffers_and_cascade.py` (13) + the P311 suite re-based; 6 falsification probes red-then-restored.
- **THE HEADLINE, and it is a correction to a decision I made hours earlier: the cascade window is DISARMED.** P311 calibrated its threshold from **11 days** of the derivflow ledger (85 rate observations per asset) using the CHANGE in a rolling 24h total between irregular ticks — a noisy estimator whose median is 0 — and concluded 5x fires on 1-2% of bars. The archive `training_data/coinglass_history` holds **1,039 per-4H-bar liquidation rows per asset, six months**, and it was on disk the whole time. On that basis 5x fires on **13.7 / 10.7 / 11.1%** of bars: a **~6x error**, and 13% is *routine*, which is the exact failure the re-calibration existed to remove.
  | C | BTC | ETH | SOL |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 5 | 13.7% | 10.7% | 11.1% |
  | 10 | 5.1% | 4.8% | 4.4% |
  | **20** | **2.1%** | **1.0%** | **1.5%** |
  | 30 | 0.9% | 0.3% | 0.7% |
- **And the DETECT itself was replayed against forward returns, which is the question I had said only a real burst could answer.** At 5x a spike carries **no information** — 24h forward return −0.06 / −0.28 / −0.18% against unconditional −0.04 / −0.02 / −0.04%, |t| <= 0.93 on all three. At 20x there is a weak **BTC-only** hint (−1.09% over 24h, t=−2.31, n=21) that does not survive being one of **12 cells** looked at (P306's own multiplicity rule). So the multiple is re-set to **20.0** — where the firing rate is exceptional and p99 is 20-27x on all three, so one multiplier still fits — and **the flag is OFF: an exceptional firing rate is a PRECONDITION, not evidence that a DETECT helps.** The structural fix stands and is what the constant is for.
- **2. "The v5.1 warmups need uptime" was already stale, and half of it was a BUG.** Measured on the server: the funding warmups hold **37 observations** against thresholds of 12 and 30 — **past both**, and `funding` is now emitting **12 directional rows in 72** (against P300's lifetime 141/6,168 = 2.3%), with reasons that name real market conditions instead of `history_warmup(1/12)`. P301's persistence worked and the strategy woke up.
  - **`microstructure` did not, and not for want of uptime.** It reports `z_below_threshold(+0.00)` — an *exactly* zero z. The producer is `_ofi_history`, a per-process deque needing **>= 5 samples at ONE sample per 4H tick = ~20 HOURS of uninterrupted uptime**, RAM-only, restarted at zero on every deploy. **The P301/P302 class in a third location the earlier fixes never touched.** Its second consumer matters more than the strategy: the SOL toxicity veto at `execution/sol_execution.py:422` (`abs(ofi_zscore) > 3.0`) **could never fire** — P265e made that consumer reachable and the input still never arrived.
  - **`_depth_history` has the identical shape** (>= 3 for the median, >= 5 for the collapse detector), so on a fresh process `orderbook_depth` is **exactly 1.0 by construction** — the median falls back to the current reading, making the ratio 1 — and the collapse branch is blind. Both read as "depth is normal".
  - Both now persist through `strategies/_warmup_state` — the P301 helper reused rather than reimplemented (P172: one atomic writer, one staleness rule, one set of fail directions). A cold start is **logged** rather than silently returned, because "nothing to restore" is precisely the state that made this invisible.
- **3. "regimebook_fgshort is months out" mis-states what is being waited for.** Its substantive evidence **already exists**: P307d measured the rule over six years with a pre-committed verdict and a same-cell random control. The ledger's job is a live-vs-lab **parity check**, not primary evidence — so the honest statement is "the historical answer is in hand and weak (+0.007/+0.050/+0.009 out of selection); the ledger is confirming the implementation matches it", which is a different and much shorter claim.
- **4. Whale/regimebook forward IC: `regimebook` IS pooled, the seat comparison is not.** `POOLABLE_FAMILIES` covers regimebook and its variants, so their clock is ~123d rather than ~330d. But the whale SEAT's IC comes from `agent_ic_review`, which groups per (agent, horizon) and does **not** pool — so that clock is still the long one, and it is a scorer gap rather than a data fact. Recorded, not fixed.
- **Shared-tree note (P225, fifth time — and this time the lesson landed).** The gate showed +2 swallow findings; rather than assume, I diffed the linter per file against origin and found the entire delta in `core/cde_fees.py`, an **untracked file another session had just created**. My changes contribute zero. Committed with a **pathspec** (`git commit -- <paths>`), the mechanism P314 identified, and verified the result in a clean clone before trusting it — which is what P311b failed to do.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) "we have to wait" deserves the same audit as any other claim — of four such items, one was already finished, one was a bug wearing a warmup's clothes, one was waiting for confirmation of evidence it already had, and only one was genuinely time-bound; (b) when a calibration is derived from a noisy proxy, find the direct measurement before acting on it — 11 days of a delta estimator and six months of the underlying series disagreed by 6x, and I had armed a live control on the wrong one; (c) a control that fires rarely and carries no measurable benefit is a cost with no offset — "protective and right-signed" is not evidence, and the honest state for it is off.

### P311. [MEASURED + ARMED 2026-08-18, operator: "do all"] The cascade threshold was wrong in BOTH directions and is now a multiple of each asset's OWN rate — plus the two dead switches deleted and the source-pin trap closed from the test side
Tests `tests/test_p311_cascade_thresholds.py` (19) + `tests/_guard_pins.py`; 6 falsification probes red-then-restored.
- **1. THE MEASUREMENT, from 11 days of the derivflow ledger.** A single `cascade_detect_liq_threshold = $10M/h` across three assets is wrong in **both** directions, because their liquidation activity differs by 12.5x:
  | asset | median 24h | baseline/h | rate p90 | rate p95 | rate max | max/baseline |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | BTC | $79.6M | $3.32M | $4.5M | $10.5M | $27.0M | 8.1x |
  | ETH | $37.8M | $1.58M | $2.0M | $4.6M | $18.9M | 12.0x |
  | SOL | $6.4M | $0.27M | $0.5M | $1.0M | **$2.09M** | 7.9x |
  $10M/h is ~3x BTC's normal rate (it fired on **7% of ticks**) and ~38x SOL's **maximum ever observed** (structurally unreachable, forever).
- **THE FINDING THAT MADE IT FIXABLE: the burst SHAPE is comparable across assets even though the LEVELS are not** — peak-to-baseline is 8.1x / 12.0x / 7.9x. That similarity is what licenses ONE dimensionless multiplier where three hand-set dollar figures would have been needed, and it is why the fix is **self-normalising**: `threshold = C x (that asset's own recent hourly rate)`, so nothing goes stale as volumes drift and there is no per-asset constant to re-derive later.
- **C = 5.0, chosen by a rule stated before reading the table it picks from:** the smallest multiple that puts EVERY asset at or below ~2.5% of ticks while still leaving the SMALLEST asset able to fire. Measured firing rates at 5x: **BTC 2.4%, ETH 1.2%, SOL 1.2%** (4x put BTC at 3.5%). `intensity` uses the same normalised denominator, or a small-cap asset reads ~0 intensity during its own cascade.
- **ARMED** (`cascade_real_liquidation_window: true`) — the effect is protective only (blocks T3/T4 adds, feeds PartialConsensus disable conditions) and both structural failures are now measured away. **HONEST LIMIT, stated rather than buried: 85 rate observations per asset, so "1.2%" is a single event.** This fixes the two STRUCTURAL failures; it does not finely calibrate the level. Raising the multiple can only make DETECT rarer.
- **Two fail directions pinned:** an unusable baseline (zero, negative, NaN, inf, non-numeric) falls back to the absolute pair and can **never** produce a zero threshold, which would make DETECT fire on every tick (P2 — absence must not become the permissive value); and the thresholds are set before the first update so a restored governor cannot AttributeError (P85).
- **A P234-class bug caught by reading line numbers:** the first draft defined `_liq_base_1h` AFTER the observe-log line that reads it — a NameError inside a swallowing try, i.e. an instrument that silently stops reporting. Reordered, and pinned by a test that asserts the definition precedes every reader.
- **2. THE TWO DEAD SWITCHES DELETED, not annotated.** `proof_log_enabled` was **set by `configs/sota_config.json`** and read by nobody, and `use_vol_percentile_adjustment` was **passed by a caller** (`enable_volatility_adjustment`) and read by nobody — so in both cases something believed it was configuring behaviour. Deleted at both ends together: removing only the field would leave the config key reported as an unknown "typo?", which is the P303 cry-wolf failure. Deliberately NOT wired instead — no stop-sizing branch was ever gated on the second, and adding one would arm untested sizing on a path that is Kraken-only and dormant (P275). The P307 no-reader roster shrinks **7 -> 5**; the three that remain have neither reader nor writer.
- **3. THE SOURCE-PIN TRAP CLOSED FROM THE TEST SIDE.** `assert "<condition>" in src` survives `if False and <condition>` — three sightings (P234, P251, P307). P307 froze a roster of 14 because extracting six predicates out of live modules was disproportionate. New `tests/_guard_pins.assert_guard_live` closes the same hole with **zero production risk**: it requires the condition to be the WHOLE condition of its `if`/`elif`/`while`, so any prefix fails. The five defeatable pins are converted; the roster drops **14 -> 9** (the nine remaining pin EXPRESSIONS, not guards, so the trap does not apply).
  - **It paid immediately.** Converting the feed-degradation pin revealed the real guard is `if self.alert_manager and len(_live_failures) >= 3:` — a CONJUNCTION. The substring form could not distinguish a whole condition from one clause of one, and now the pin has to say which.
- **Shared-tree note (P225, fourth time):** a concurrent session was mid-edit during one suite run, producing 16 failures that vanished on re-run. Attributed with a scratch `git archive HEAD` checkout (P175) rather than guessed — they were not mine. **[CORRECTED by P311b, same day — the sentence that stood here was wrong.]** I wrote that of two new try/except sites one was mine and one (`dataclasses.fields`) was the parallel session's uncommitted work, so the baseline moved 707 -> 708 rather than 709. Wrong on the mechanism: their site was inside a file I had already staged, so it **was** in my commit. A clean `git clone` of the commit measured **709** against a baseline of 707, and the 708 I wrote never reached the commit either — gate-per-commit on a real clone was clean at the parent and red at mine. HEAD was left calling `validate_loaded_config(known_toplevel=...)` against a signature that does not accept it, swallowed into a debug line, so the CONFIG_SCHEMA check was silently OFF until the hotfix. **The durable lesson is the one the parallel session found from the other direction: `git add` followed by a bare `git commit` is not path-scoped — commit with a PATHSPEC (`git commit -- <paths>`).** And attribute any scanner delta with a clean clone of the COMMIT: the working tree cannot tell you what you committed.
- **Mitigation pattern:** when a threshold is wrong for several assets, check whether the SHAPE of the distribution is stable across them before writing per-asset constants — if it is, one dimensionless multiple of each asset's own scale is strictly better: it cannot go stale, it needs no re-derivation, and it makes the detector mean the same thing everywhere. And when a trap recurs a third time, look for a fix on the TEST side before refactoring live code: the hole here was "a pin cannot tell live code from dead", which a stricter assertion closes without touching a single production module.

### P307f. [CORRECTION 2026-08-18] ShortControl has never run in production — P275 recorded it as live on the sleeve, and P276 hardened it on that premise
Found while auditing regime-name tables against the P307 vocabulary. Tests `tests/test_p307f_shortcontrol_not_live.py` (3); 3 falsification probes red-then-restored.
- **THE CLAIM BEING CORRECTED.** P275's finding #3 states *"SHORT_CONTROL's veto-override IS live on the sleeve"* — an override that clears non-protected vetoes on short intents, so a would-be flatten becomes an executed perp short. P276 then added `"Alpha gate"` to `_SC_PROTECTED_VETOES` to bound it. **The premise is false and always was.**
- **VERIFIED THREE WAYS, none of them inference:** main.py constructs the object only `if self.config.short_control_config`; `short_control` has **never** appeared in `configs/live_high_risk.json` (`git log -S '"short_control"'` over that file returns **no commit**), and the only profile that sets it is `configs/ultra_aggressive_5y.json`, which is not what docker-compose runs; and the live engine logs **zero** `[P5-01] ShortControl: ACTIVE` lines.
- **P276's hardening STAYS.** It is correct the moment the module is enabled, and removing a tightening because its subject turned out to be dormant is the wrong direction. What changes is the claim, not the code — no veto-override has been reaching the sleeve from here.
- **A stale table found beside it, harmless for a reason worth pinning.** `defense/short_control._BULL_REGIMES` holds `STRONG_BULL, PARABOLIC, BULL_TREND, MOMENTUM_RALLY, STRONG_TREND, IGNITION, EXPANSION` — of which **only MOMENTUM_RALLY exists in any live GMM vocabulary** (the rest belong to the EnhancedRegimeNavigator's naming), and it is compared against `market_data["regime_state"]`, which IS the GMM name. That is the P217 defect class exactly. It is harmless **only because `is_bull` gates nothing**: it is stored on a reporting field, while the override branch keys on `self.config.allow_short_in_bull`. Pinned — if `is_bull` ever becomes load-bearing, the staleness turns into a live loosening, permitting shorts through `STEADY_UPTREND`, which the P307 refit made 10-20% of bars.
- **Enabling it is now a decision rather than a side effect:** adding the config key, removing the construction gate, or making `is_bull` load-bearing each turn this file red and send the author to P141.
- **Mitigation pattern:** an audit finding recorded as "X is live" is a claim with a cheap check behind it — `git log -S` over the live profile plus a grep of the engine's own startup line. Two subsequent entries reasoned from this one without running either, and a hardening was spent on a dormant path. When a P-entry asserts something is live, verify it at the CONFIG and the LOG, not from the call graph: a call site that exists says nothing about whether the object was ever constructed.

### P307e. [BUILT 2026-08-18, OBSERVATION-ONLY] The funding-gated short wired as a forward ledger — and the BTC parity control caught the rule turning an ABSENCE into a live short on its first run
The P307d candidate ships as an exam, not a config flip. Tests `tests/test_p307e_fgshort_ledger.py` (13); 5 falsification probes red-then-restored; suite green, gate clean.
- **THE RULE, and the design property that makes it readable.** `fgshort_target` fills a book cell that CHOSE flat with a short when causal funding z > 1.0; it never overrides an opinion the book already has. So the ledger measures **exactly the increment under test** and nothing else — the same isolation principle P307d used to get a verdict where head-to-head scoring returned "not settled" three times.
- **BTC IS A FREE CONTROL, by construction.** BTC's book already shorts every non-bull cell at z > 1.0 (bear leg) and from z > 0.5 (peace leg), so the variant **cannot** differ from the deployed book there — pinned across the full regime x funding-z grid. Any divergence in the BTC ledger is a bug rather than a signal, and **ETH and SOL carry the entire claim**.
- **THAT CONTROL EARNED ITS KEEP IMMEDIATELY.** Its first run reported BTC diverging in exactly two cells: `regime="warmup"` with z > 1.0. `book_target` returns `(0.0, "warmup")` when no book exists yet, and my rule read that 0.0 as "the book chose flat" and shorted it. **A live short manufactured out of an absence** — the P2 collapse, in the rule written to test a P2-adjacent question, caught within a minute by a control that cost nothing to add. Fixed with an explicit `_FGSHORT_ABSENCE_LEGS` set (`warmup`, `flat_no_funding_history`), and the missing-funding case is named separately from the below-threshold case so the ledger can tell them apart later.
- **Registered as a POOLED family** (`POOLABLE_FAMILIES`), deliberately: P293g measured that a per-asset 16h exam needs ~330 days to certify while pooling three assets running ONE rule cuts it to ~123. Without pooling the candidate would sit on a clock that cannot fire — the defect P293g named. The file prefix needs no change: the scorer globs `regimebook_*`, which already picks up `regimebook_fgshort_*` the way it picks up `regimebook_adj_*`.
- **Observation-only, pinned:** the variant never writes `_last_records` (the seat stash), its write is fail-soft and runs AFTER the raw record is already on disk, and confidence is `|direction|` so a flat row contributes zero to the scorer rather than a saturated claim (P224/P236). The threshold travels on every row so a later change is auditable rather than silent.
- **What it does NOT earn.** The out-of-selection increments behind it are **+0.007 / +0.050 / +0.009** — "not harmful and mildly positive", not an edge — and the z > 1.0 threshold was designed on BTC in the design era, so the ETH/SOL design figures are partly in-family. Promotion needs the P166 cost-aware gate on FORWARD data, and on the pooled clock that is months away, not weeks.
- **Mitigation pattern:** when a new rule keys off another component's output, look for a case where the two are IDENTICAL by construction and pin it — a free control costs one test and catches the class of bug that no amount of reading the new rule would surface. Here the control was not designed to find anything; it found that the rule shorted every warmup bar.

### P307d. [MEASURED 2026-08-18] Is FLAT better than SHORT? Yes for the unconditional short the live trend seat holds — and the best arm is neither trend nor the current book
`training/short_leg_lab.py`, report `training/reports/short_leg_lab_p307d.json`. P307c showed two thirds of the book-vs-trend disagreement is ONE structural choice, so this lab holds the LONG LEG CONSTANT (+1 iff close>SMA200, byte-identical across every arm) and varies only the short cells. Every number below is the short leg and nothing else. Costs, 1-bar delay and funding carry from the shared chassis (P172); verdict pre-committed before the run.
- **THE PRE-COMMITTED RULE:** SHORT EARNS iff the increment over `long_flat` is positive in the DESIGN era, positive in the PRE-DESIGN era, and beats a same-cell turnover-matched random control in both. Deliberately not keyed on a t-statistic — P297 established that at this sample size a `|t| >= 2` bar rejects buy-and-hold too, so it does not discriminate; era stability and a random control are properties a coin flip cannot fake.
- **RESULT — the live trend seat's unconditional short: FLAT STANDS on all three.**
  | asset | `long_short` increment design / pre | `funding_gated` design / pre | random same-cell (design) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | BTC | +0.371 / **−0.352** | +0.117 / +0.007 | −0.879 [−1.91, +0.16] |
  | ETH | +0.470 / **−0.514** | +0.452 / +0.050 | −1.391 [−2.76, −0.03] |
  | SOL | **−0.010** / **−1.503** | +0.136 / +0.009 | −1.948 [−4.25, +0.09] |
  The unconditional short flips sign across eras on BTC and ETH and is negative in BOTH on SOL. **So the book's refusal to short is right, and the live trend seat is giving that away.**
- **THE MECHANISM, and it refutes the hypothesis I went in with.** I expected the short leg's case to rest on COLLECTING FUNDING. It does not. Decomposing a −1 held only in the short cells: price PnL is **+0.351 / +0.474 / +0.265 in design** and **−0.378 / −0.519 / −1.466 in pre-design**, while carry collected is **+0.071 / +0.055 / −0.132** — an order of magnitude smaller, and NEGATIVE on SOL. The short leg is an **era bet on price direction** (the design era contains 2022's bear; the pre-design era is 2021's bull), not a carry harvest.
- **THE ACTIONABLE FINDING: the best arm is neither the live seat nor the current book.** A **funding-gated short** — short only where causal funding z > 1.0, which is BTC's own bear-leg rule (P250) generalised to all three assets — is positive in BOTH eras on ALL THREE and beats the random control everywhere. Today ETH's and SOL's books go FLAT in every short cell, so this is a change to the book, not a defence of it. **Two caveats that must travel with it:** the z>1.0 threshold was designed on BTC in the design era, so the ETH/SOL design figures are partly in-family; and the out-of-selection increments are **+0.007 / +0.050 / +0.009**, i.e. "not harmful and mildly positive", not "an edge". It clears the letter of the rule on a hair. This is a **shadow-first candidate**, not a config flip (P141) — and it is NOT a resurrection of the P250-deleted SOL bear ridge, which was a fitted model; this is a one-threshold rule with no free parameters beyond the one it inherits.
- **An implausibility chased rather than waved through (P301).** BTC's `long_short` and `buy_and_hold` increments came back as an exact mirror (+0.371 / −0.371) although one pays 0.104 in costs and the other pays zero. Checked at the arithmetic: `long_flat`'s cost (0.0519) is **exactly half** `long_short`'s (0.1038), because 0↔1 transitions are half the |Δpos| of 1↔−1 transitions and B&H has no turnover — so the cost term mirrors around `long_flat` just as price and carry do. A real identity of this construction, not a degenerate harness. **Consequence worth stating: in THIS lab buy_and_hold carries no independent information** — it is mechanically the negative of the short increment — unlike in P296/P301 where it was a genuine baseline that changed a verdict.
- **Mitigation pattern:** when two composites disagree, do not re-score the composites — hold everything they share CONSTANT and vary only the disputed leg. Two thirds of a "which strategy is better" question turned out to be one binary choice, and isolating it produced a verdict where head-to-head scoring produced "not settled" three times. And state the baseline's independence: a control that is an arithmetic mirror of the treatment is a check on the harness, not evidence about the market.

### P307c. [MEASURED 2026-08-18, operator: "is the current mechanism better than trend"] Not settled — and the two labs that appear to disagree are answering different questions
`training/seat_headtohead_lab.py`, report `training/reports/seat_headtohead_p307c.json`. Same chassis as the other labs (imported, not restated — P172): 1-bar delay, per-side costs, **and funding carry**, because the sleeve trades perps and the two mechanisms hold OPPOSITE positions in exactly the cells where they disagree, so pricing them on price PnL alone would be asymmetrically wrong (P245/P296).
- **THE RESULT: SPLIT on all three assets — the book loses the design era and wins the pre-design era, every time.** Net after cost and carry:
  | asset | trend design / pre | book design / pre | book−trend | exposure trend/book |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | BTC | +1.125 / +0.084 | +0.970 / +0.401 | −0.155 / **+0.317** | 1.00 / 0.58 |
  | ETH | +0.828 / +1.024 | −0.024 / +1.111 | −0.852 / **+0.087** | 1.00 / 0.27 |
  | SOL | +1.357 / +2.949 | +1.079 / +2.975 | −0.278 / **+0.026** | 1.00 / 0.30 |
  A mechanism that wins in one era and loses in the other is era-fragile and the comparison is **not settled by this lab** (P243/P244). What the book does buy unambiguously is the same order of net at **a third to a half of the exposure** — but exposure is not the objective the sleeve is scored on, so that is a property, not a verdict.
- **CORRECTION TO MY OWN FRAMING, and it is why the lab was worth running.** I expected ETH and SOL to be degenerate ("their book IS trend-only"). Measured, they disagree on **66-67% of bars**. Decomposed over the design era:
  | asset | both LONG | trend SHORT → book FLAT | both SHORT | trend LONG → book FLAT |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | BTC | 2221 | 2249 | 436 | 287 |
  | ETH | 1662 | **3456** | 0 | 982 |
  | SOL | 1827 | **3321** | 0 | 950 |
  So the comparison is dominated by one structural difference — **trend is always in the market and the book refuses to short** — plus a second, smaller one nobody had stated: the regime labeler's BULL cell agrees with `close > SMA200` on only **83-84%** of bars, so the book's long leg is *stricter* than trend's, not identical to it.
- **RECONCILING THIS WITH P297, which read decisively FOR the book.** P297 measured BTC book +243.9% vs **trend_only** +151.7% over six years in the derivatives expression, with the book's Sharpe CI the only one excluding zero. That is not in conflict: **P297 compared the book against a LONG/FLAT trend and asked whether the funding legs add; this lab compares it against the LIVE ±1 trend seat, which SHORTS.** Two different questions, both correctly answered. The live-relevant one is this one — P295 established the trend seat quantizes to ±1 and the sleeve sizes by sign — and its answer is "not settled".
- **WHAT THE MECHANISM IS ACTUALLY DOING LIVE, which is the part that matters most and is easy to miss.** Over the 8.8 days of `regimebook_*` ledger since the seat was restored: **ETH 100% FLAT, SOL 100% FLAT, BTC 43% short / 1% long** (the rest flat). So today the book is trading BTC only, almost entirely through its **funding legs** — the one slice P262 recorded as uncertified and P297 then certified over six years — while ETH and SOL fall through to whale (which seats only where the book is flat, P298) or to trend. Any claim that "the current mechanism is better" is, for two of three assets, a claim about a mechanism that is not currently taking a position at all.
- **THE FORWARD LEDGER CANNOT SETTLE THIS FOR MONTHS, and the arithmetic should be stated rather than waited out (P293g).** 92 rows ≈ 15 days. For `|t| >= 2` that needs IC ≥ **0.21 at 4h** and ≥ **0.43 at 16h**, against an economic bar near 0.13. No amount of reading the current ledger produces a verdict this quarter; the honest instruments are the historical labs plus P297's six-year read.
- **Mitigation pattern:** when two labs seem to disagree about "is A better than B", check what each held CONSTANT — here one compared the book against a long/flat baseline and the other against an always-in ±1 baseline, and the apparent contradiction was entirely in the baseline, not in the mechanism. And before comparing two signals at all, decompose WHERE they differ: two thirds of this comparison turned out to be a single structural choice (short vs flat), which is a far more useful thing to know than either net figure.

### P307b. [MEASURED + RESOLVED 2026-08-18, same day] The saturation I reported as unfixed was an over-read of three 2-decimal log lines, the trend gate is RETIRED rather than left flippable, and the source-pin trap now has a mechanism instead of a lesson
Closing the items P307 left open. Tests: `tests/test_p307_gmm_and_defaults.py` (18) + `tests/test_trend_regime_gate_shadow.py` (16); 5 falsification probes red-then-restored.
- **1. THE CORRECTION: `regime_confidence` saturation is NOT unresolved, and my evidence for saying it was could not have supported the claim.** I compared three live `regime_conf=1.00 / 0.97` log lines against the refit's FIT-WINDOW mean (~0.88) and concluded the fix had not reached serve. Two errors in one comparison: **(a) it is unmatched** — an aggregate over 720 bars spanning many regimes against three readings all in ONE regime; **(b) `.2f` cannot distinguish 0.995 from 1.000**, and the runtime only logs its ">0.999" diagnostic when genuinely above that — measured over 3h of live logs, **1** such line against **28** readings printing "1.00", so most of those were 0.995–0.999, not 1.0.
  - **The matched measurement settles it.** Offline max-posterior over the recent 720 bars, CONDITIONAL on the assigned regime and through the serve-side `clip(±4)`: BTC QUIET_ACCUMULATION mean 0.852 with **22.7% of bars ≥ 0.965**; ETH QUIET_ACCUMULATION 0.918 with **34.6% ≥ 0.995**; SOL QUIET_ACCUMULATION 0.955 with **47.3% ≥ 0.995**. The live readings (BTC 0.97, ETH/SOL ~1.00, all in QUIET_ACCUMULATION) sit **inside** that distribution. Conditional means across regimes range 0.65–0.98, i.e. the posterior genuinely varies — against the pre-refit model's ~0.99997 on every tick (P215).
  - **And the bars are not out-of-distribution either**, which was the mechanism I had assumed: Mahalanobis distance to the assigned centroid over the recent 720 bars is **lower** than over the fit window (BTC p50 6.1 vs 7.3), with only **0.1–0.8%** beyond the fit-era p99 against 1.0% by construction. The runtime's own `distribution_shift` fallback fired **0 times in 3h**.
  - **Fixed so the log can answer this without an offline lab:** `regime_conf` now prints to **three** decimals with the **runner-up posterior** beside it. A confident call and a saturated one produce different numbers; at 2dp they did not, which is precisely the gap that let me draw the wrong conclusion.
- **2. `trend_regime_gate` RETIRED, not left as a flag.** P306 and P307 both measured the live gate set as negative in BOTH eras on ALL THREE assets — six reads out of six, reproduced either side of the GMM refit (pre: BTC −0.332/−0.210, ETH −0.687/−0.259, SOL −0.509/−0.657; post: −0.699/−0.289, −0.243/−0.228, −0.623/−0.258). Calling that a "drop candidate" and leaving `"enforce"` in the valid set means the next reader can arm a control that is measured to destroy money. `"enforce"` is now in `_RETIRED_GATE_MODES` and **refused LOUDLY** — a config that says enforce and behaves as shadow is the P16 shape, so it logs an ERROR naming the numbers rather than coercing in silence. **The shadow ledger keeps writing:** retiring a PRESCRIPTION is not a reason to retire the DETECTION beside it (P299). Re-arming now requires editing TWO constants and **both edits fail a test** — added because a probe that only re-admitted `"enforce"` to the valid set stayed GREEN.
- **3. THE SOURCE-PIN TRAP GETS A MECHANISM.** `assert "<condition>" in src` survives `if False and <condition>` — it proves code was written, not that it runs. That has bitten three times (P234's gate-hysteresis block, P251's stale-snapshot guard, and P307's GMM shape guard, whose first probe stayed green). A sweep of the recent test files finds **14** such pins; converting them all would mean extracting six predicates across six live modules, disproportionate for guards that are currently correct. So the roster is **frozen at 14: it may shrink, never grow**, with an anti-vacuity test on the scan and a staleness test that forces the ceiling down when it does shrink. A new one sends its author to the fix that works — extract the predicate, call it, pin the call site separately.
- **4. The seven no-reader switches, dispositioned individually rather than in bulk.** `defense/trade_gate.use_maker_fee` (the one on a sleeve-binding component) and `alert_popup_enabled`, `use_birdeye`, `use_solscan` are annotated as inert at their declarations. Two are worse than inert and are recorded as such: **`configs/sota_config.json` SETS `proof_log_enabled: true`** — a config file configuring a switch nothing consults; and **`risk/adaptive_stop.py:1044` PASSES `use_vol_percentile_adjustment=enable_volatility_adjustment`**, so half of that caller's intent lands (`use_calmar_adjustment` is read) and half evaporates. Deliberately NOT wired: the whole adaptive_stop stack is Kraken-only and dormant (P275), so arming an untested stop-sizing branch there would be a P141 activation, not a fix. Deleting the fields was rejected too — removing a field a config sets is a contract change, and the P307 roster guard already makes an eighth fail.
- **5. Verified live rather than assumed:** the P301/P302 warmup persistence is writing on the server (`/opt/hmats/data/v5_1_warmup/{funding_mean_reversion,funding_post_etf_regime,kyle_lambda}.json`, stamped after the deploy), so those counters no longer restart at 1.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) before reporting a live metric as pathological, check the log's PRECISION and match the comparison's conditioning — three 2-decimal lines in one regime cannot contradict a conditional distribution, and here they did not; (b) when a measurement retires a control, retire the MODE, not just the recommendation — a flag left valid is a flag someone will flip, and the retirement must fail loudly rather than coerce; (c) when the same trap recurs a third time, stop fixing instances and freeze a roster: a ceiling that can only fall converts a lesson into a mechanism (P280).

### P307. [MEASURED + RESOLVED 2026-08-18, operator: "resolve this, and validate nothing is default on instead of off"] The duplicated GMM input was not free — it double-weighted `return_4h` and shifted a third of every asset's regime assignments, and my own P306 note said the opposite
Tests `tests/test_p307_gmm_and_defaults.py` (15); 7 falsification probes red-then-restored; full suite 5,173 passing; gate clean. Probe: `training/scripts/gmm_redundancy_probe.py`, report `training/reports/gmm_redundancy_probe_p307.json`.
- **THE CORRECTION TO P306, AND IT IS THE WHOLE ENTRY.** P306 recorded `ret_1h = ret_4h / 4.0` as a deliberate keep — train and serve agree, so parity holds — and added that "being exactly `ret_4h/4` it is perfectly collinear with `ret_4h`, so the GMM runs on 9 independent inputs, not 12", i.e. harmless. **The first half is right and the second half is wrong.** After the StandardScaler the two columns are IDENTICAL, and in a **full-covariance** GMM an identical column is not a no-op: the covariance is singular in that plane (regularised by `reg_covar=1e-2`) and the `return_4h` direction is counted **TWICE** in the Mahalanobis distance that assigns every bar to a cluster. "Carries no information" and "costs nothing" are different claims, and I asserted the second from the first.
- **MEASURED, with the production recipe** (same scaler, same `GMM_BASE_CONFIG`, same k=3..8 BIC search with the 2% minimum-regime rule, same split-aware fit boundary), refitting under three feature sets:
  | asset | ret1h≡ret4h/4 | corr | k (12) | k (11) | **ARI full vs no-dup** |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | BTC | True | 1.0 | 6 | **7** | **0.690** |
  | ETH | True | 1.0 | 7 | 7 | **0.657** |
  | SOL | True | 1.0 | 7 | 7 | **0.741** |
  Roughly a third of bar-to-regime assignments differ, and on BTC the duplicate changed BIC's **model-order** choice. **Honesty note carried in the probe: BIC is NOT comparable across these variants** — a different feature count is a different likelihood space, so the 12-feature fit reporting a lower BIC says nothing about which model is better; the discriminating statistic is the ARI, not the BIC.
- **RESOLVED by removing three inputs, not by making `ret_1h` real.** Dropped: `return_1h` (the duplicate), `cross_asset_correlation` and `spread_percentile` (per-asset CONSTANTS on both sides, P221 — zero variance, contributing no likelihood but an extra BIC-penalty dimension, which is what moved SOL's k). **The alternative — feeding the real Kraken hourly return P306 built — was rejected: it would make a network fetch a REQUIRED input to the live regime classifier, so an outage would break classification.** Removing a distortion adds no failure mode; adding a dependency does.
- **Executed as ONE P215 set**: both builders (`training/scripts/rebuild_pipeline.py` and `data_mgmt/market_data_pipeline.py`), the parquets, and the fitted artifacts moved in a single step. **Result: all three assets now select k=8** (was 6/7/7), the census is far more balanced (no cluster above 34%, against the old two-clusters-93% saturation), and `regime_confidence` softens to ~0.88. **`NEUTRAL_DRIFT` and `MOMENTUM_RALLY` exist in all three vocabularies again** — which retires the P306 note that "NEUTRAL_DRIFT does not exist in ANY clean vocabulary" and makes the previously-unreachable kraken_quant BULL bucket reachable.
- **The old artifact carried its own evidence, visible in the shipped JSON**: coordinates 0 and 1 of every deployed cluster mean were bit-identical (`-0.5857913130009003` twice on BTC). A test now fails if any deployed mean regains a duplicated coordinate pair — the tell is cheaper to check than the cause.
- **THE TREND-GATE VERDICT WAS RE-DERIVED, NOT CARRIED OVER**, because the refit changed the labels the lab reads. It gets **stronger**: the live gate set is negative in both eras on all three assets (**BTC −0.699 / −0.289, ETH −0.243 / −0.228, SOL −0.623 / −0.258**), now covering 27–40% of bars with NEUTRAL_DRIFT genuinely present. And the four single-regime "passes" P306 recorded as probable multiplicity noise **collapse to one** (BTC PANIC_SELLOFF, 1% of bars) — the caveat was right. Both reports are kept (`trend_gate_lab_p306.json` pre-refit, `_p307.json` post-refit); do not diff across them without saying which labels each used.
- **A half-moved P215 set is now LOUD instead of silent.** A mismatched artifact/builder pair used to surface as a numpy broadcast error inside a broad handler — i.e. as a quiet fallback to the ADX proxy, exactly the state a partial deploy leaves behind. It now refuses with both shapes named. **The predicate is a module-level pure function because the first falsification probe of the guard stayed GREEN against `if False and ...`** — a pin on source text proves the code was written, not that it runs (P234/P251, third sighting).
- **THE DEFAULT-ON AUDIT.** 37 boolean `ProductionConfig` fields: 17 default TRUE, and 16 of those are ABSENT from the live profile (so they run on their code default). Widening to the whole tree found **87** `enable_*` / `*_enabled` / `use_*` dataclass switches defaulting True. Triage: the 13-strong `profit_max_*` family is the P287 finding and its vetoes remain classified sleeve-HOLD (pinned); everything else has a reader and was classified BINDS-SLEEVE vs KRAKEN-ONLY in P275. **Seven have NO reader anywhere in the tree** — switches that look armed and control nothing (P177): `use_birdeye`, `use_solscan`, `enable_proof_logs`, `proof_log_enabled`, `alert_popup_enabled`, `use_vol_percentile_adjustment`, and — the one that matters — **`defense/trade_gate.use_maker_fee`**, on a component that DOES bind the sleeve, reading as "the gate prices maker fills" when the gate always prices the taker fee. Annotated at source rather than deleted (removing a config field is a contract change), and the roster is guarded both ways: an EIGHTH fake switch fails, and an entry that gains a reader fails too, so it cannot become a parking spot.
- **THE LIVENESS AUDIT found one real defect.** Live `[AGENT-TRACE]`: BTC 7 of 16 agents directional, ETH 4, SOL 1 — with `flow` and `options` emitting for the first time (P298's netflow + Deribit PCR replacing structural zeros). Every `HEALTH_S*` check PASSes; the only WARNINGs are the micro warmup (persistence shipped P302, needs uptime), the cosmetic HF-Hub notice, and `[TREND-LAYER] +0.00` — which is correct, since BTC's direction now comes from the restored regimebook seat, not from trend. **The defect: `soldex` emitted `dir=+0.00 conf=1.00` on SOL.** Confidence was the raw liquidity score, independent of direction, so a deeply liquid DEX with NO arbitrage opportunity published a maximally-confident non-signal — and both fusion and the IC scorer consume `direction x confidence`. **This is the P224 defect verbatim, in a second agent.** Fixed the same way: confidence collapses to 0 on a flat direction, with the undiluted liquidity reading still available as `soldex_liquidity_score`.
- **Evidence discontinuities, recorded so nobody diffs across them:** every `regime`, `regime_proba_*` and `regime_confidence` value in the parquets changes definition at this refit, so the DRL shadow stream (P214's caveat, again) and any lab reading `_ctx` regime labels are not comparable across it. The regimebook shadow is UNAFFECTED — it uses its own causal SMA200 + funding labels, not the GMM.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) "carries no information" and "costs nothing" are different claims — a perfectly collinear feature is redundant in an information sense and still re-weights the geometry any distance-based model uses, so measure the effect instead of reasoning from the redundancy; (b) when a fix would restore a feature's *value*, ask first whether it would also add a *dependency* — removing a distortion is strictly safer than feeding a live model from the network; (c) a falsification probe that leaves the asserted substring in place (`if False and ...`) tests nothing, so any guard worth pinning belongs in a function the test can CALL.

### P306. [MEASURED + FIXED 2026-08-18, operator: "do all remaining tasks"] The last two fabricated inputs replaced with measurements, the cascade governor found scrambling three assets into one state machine, and the trend gate settled by the lab — it SUBTRACTS 6 reads out of 6
The remaining-task list closed. Tests `tests/test_p306_measured_inputs.py` (31); new modules `data_mgmt/feeds/{kraken_hourly,dvol_history}.py`; new lab `training/trend_gate_lab.py`.
- **1. THE 1H PRICE MOVE IS NOW MEASURED.** `price_change_1h_pct` was `price_change_4h_pct / 4.0` — a fixed rescale wearing a measurement's name, the P293 sentiment-"z-score" shape — and its two consequences both sat on the live risk path: the cascade governor DETECTs at `1h <= -3%`, which under a /4 rescale needs a **12% 4H move** (unreachable), and it forces `exhaustion >= 0.5` whenever `|1h| < 1%`, which `|4h/4|` almost always is — **so the governor claimed the price was STABILIZING throughout any real cascade.** Now the last COMPLETED Kraken hourly candle (public, keyless, unmetered; the regimebook harness already reads the same endpoint at `interval=240`, and its `KRAKEN_PAIRS` map is IMPORTED rather than restated, P172). The in-progress hour is dropped by a close-time predicate (P253c) and a completed candle older than 2.5h REFUSES rather than asserting a stale move (P156); every failure path returns None and leaves the old value, never 0.0 (P2). **ARMED** (`real_1h_price_change: true`) — a measurement replacing a fabrication with no free parameter, and both effects are protective. Both numbers are logged every tick regardless of the flag (P300).
- **2. DVOL PUBLISHES A Z-SCORE, WHICH IS WHY IT COULD FINALLY BE ARMED.** P298 was right to refuse this flag: `constitution.py:81` aliases `dvol -> dvol_zscore` and fires `EXTREME_DVOL` at `>= 5.0`, while Deribit publishes an index LEVEL (BTC ~34) — publishing it would have read as z=34 and **permanently flattened the book** (EXTREME_DVOL is not in the sleeve HOLD set). The missing piece was a denominator, and it did not have to be accrued: Deribit's volatility-index endpoint takes an arbitrary range, so **401 daily rows back to 2025-07-14 exist on the first tick** — unlike the P301 warmups, whose accrual is precisely what made three other signals look dead. Live at enablement: **BTC 34.13 → z −1.38, ETH 45.69 → z −1.96**, both at the BOTTOM of their own trailing year, so the honest z is the *opposite* of what the raw level asserted. SOL gets NO key (Deribit lists zero SOL options; an absence stays an absence). Also clamped `dvol_score = max(0.0, ...)`: EXTREME_DVOL is one-sided, so a calm market must contribute 0 activation rather than a negative one — behaviour-identical today (both consumers take `max(trigger_scores.values())`), but this is the first change that makes the value genuinely negative, and a summing consumer would read calm as risk RELIEF.
- **3. THE CASCADE GOVERNOR WAS ONE STATE MACHINE FED THREE ASSETS IN SEQUENCE.** `get_cascade_exhaustion_governor()` is a singleton and main.py feeds it BTC → ETH → SOL every tick, so `prev_metrics` was always **the previous ASSET's** reading: `liq_acceleration = (this_vol − prev_asset_vol)/prev_asset_vol` and `price_velocity = this_1h − prev_asset_1h`. Measured on the live ledger: BTC ~$6.19M/h arriving straight after SOL ~$0.16M/h makes SOL's acceleration **−0.97**, which clears `< −0.5` and raises the exhaustion signal on essentially every SOL tick **purely from the iteration order**; `market_data["cascade_phase"]` was likewise read off a machine last transitioned by a different asset. The P225 cross-asset leak, on a live risk path, for the governor's entire life. Instances are now keyed by asset (no-arg calls unchanged for callers with no asset in scope); the tranche guard in `execution_service` and `governor_integration.check_tranche` resolve per-symbol. **Per-asset state is persisted and restored** (`all_governor_states` / `restore_governor_states`, pre-P306 flat files still accepted) — adding per-asset machines without that would have introduced the P301/P302/P303 reset-on-deploy class myself.
- **4. THE TREND GATE, SETTLED BY MEASUREMENT — IT SUBTRACTS.** P198's split was per-tick signed returns measured IN-SAMPLE; the forward reading was inverted; neither is the decision. `training/trend_gate_lab.py` asks the actual question — does zeroing the trend seat in the gated regimes leave more money after costs? — with a **pre-committed verdict** (ENFORCE only if it wins in BOTH eras) and the chassis imported from `mechanism_lab` (P172). Result for the live gate set, net after cost: **BTC −0.332 design / −0.210 pre-design; ETH −0.687 / −0.259; SOL −0.509 / −0.657. Six reads out of six negative.** Enforcing it would have destroyed money on every asset in every era, and the "inverted" forward evidence was right. `trend_regime_gate` **stays shadow and is now a DROP candidate, not a promotion candidate.** Recorded and deliberately NOT promoted: the per-asset single-regime gates that do pass both eras (BTC EXTREME_VOLATILITY, ETH EXTREME_VOLATILITY + PANIC_SELLOFF, SOL STEADY_UPTREND) — 4 passes out of ~22 looks is about what multiplicity gives, so they are a hypothesis, not a result. **Vocabulary note that matters: `NEUTRAL_DRIFT` does not exist in ANY of the clean k=6/7/7 vocabularies (P267), so the live gate has been WEAK_CONSOLIDATION only** whatever the config text says.
- **5. THE CASCADE LIQUIDATION WINDOW STAYS OFF, AND NOW FOR A MEASURED REASON.** The P219 derivflow ledger carries `total_liquidations_24h` per asset per tick, so the P304/P305 delta estimator was REPLAYED over 10.5 real days instead of waiting for a cascade to happen: BTC p50 $0/h, p90 $4.2M, **p95 $10.06M/h against a $10M threshold** (5 DETECTs in 10.5 days), ETH p95 $4.7M, **SOL max ever observed $2.09M/h — it could never fire at all.** So the single global `cascade_detect_liq_threshold` was calibrated against the fabricated average, and arming the real window would convert a never-firing detector into one firing every other day on BTC while staying structurally unreachable on SOL. **Re-arming needs a re-derived, per-asset threshold — the P265 volume-collapse lesson — not a predicate flip.** Pinned absent.
- **Also fixed:** the `[P147-b]` enrich path wrote `price_change_4h_pct` as a PERCENT while the pipeline writes a FRACTION and every threshold reads a fraction — a 100× disagreement between two producers of one key, latent only because the pipeline always sets it (a pre-armed P2). **Deliberately NOT changed:** the pipeline's second `ret_1h = ret_4h / 4.0` at :2155 is **GMM feature #1**, and `rebuild_pipeline.py:368` computes it identically — train and serve agree, changing one side alone is a train/serve skew on a live regime classifier (P214/P221), and changing both is a feature-definition change where {GMM, parquets, checkpoints} move as ONE set (P215). Worth recording as a finding: being exactly `ret_4h/4` it is **perfectly collinear with ret_4h**, so with P221's two constants the GMM runs on **9 independent inputs, not 12**.
- **Mitigation patterns:** (a) when a threshold has never fired, ask whether its INPUT could ever reach it — here two of the governor's three inputs were uniform shares of a slower aggregate, which is exactly the arithmetic that erases the burst the detector exists to see; (b) before arming a repaired detector, REPLAY it on whatever ledger already recorded its inputs — 10.5 days of derivflow rows answered in minutes a question that "wait for a real cascade" would have left open for months, and the answer came back the opposite of the expected one; (c) a singleton whose state is fed per-asset in a loop is a cross-asset leak by construction, and the tell is any `prev_*` field on an object shared across a fan-out.

### P293. [AUDITED + FIXED 2026-08-17, operator: "check macro / big-money flow / market emotion … then repair all + do we need new sources"] Three signal families audited; macro was dead through THREE independent paths, all by the same mechanism — a consumer wired to a cache no producer ever fills — and the answer to "new sources?" is NO for four of five gaps
Full plan: [docs/DATA_COVERAGE_PLAN.md](docs/DATA_COVERAGE_PLAN.md). Tests `tests/test_p293_data_coverage.py` (59); eight falsification probes all red-then-restored with anchor-uniqueness asserted (P238); full suite 4,778 passing; gate clean.
- **The audit verdict, from live logs + per-tick diagnostics + read-only probes:** only sentiment's cheapest layer (F&G L1) was actually running. **MACRO dead three ways** — (a) the FRED feed has a valid key and `mock=False` but **no caller of `fetch()`/`start()` exists anywhere in the non-test tree**; every consumer calls `get_macro_context()`/`get_latest()`, which are pure CACHE READS, so it served `macro_risk=0.5` forever; (b) `GlobalContextInformer` needs yfinance, which is **not in the engine image** (`ModuleNotFoundError`), and its `update_async()` **also has no caller** — `"GlobalContext updated"` appears **0 times in the entire log history**; (c) `macro_feed.py` is constructed with no args → `source=MOCK`, VIX/DXY/rates are `random.uniform()`.
